# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Working rules for this project (READ FIRST)

These are user-imposed rules for how Claude should behave in this repo. They override the default harness behavior and the superpowers skill system.

1. **Do NOT invoke any `superpowers:*` skill** (brainstorming, TDD, debugging, writing-plans, using-git-worktrees, verification-before-completion, etc.) when working in this repo. The user has disabled them for URY. Non-superpowers skills (`update-config`, `simplify`, `find-skills`, …) are fine if they actually apply.
2. **Never `git push` without explicit permission for that specific push.** A prior approval is not standing. When permission is granted:
   - Create a new branch — never push to `develop` or `main` directly.
   - The push must go out under the user's git credentials (`buildwithmoi`) from his local machine. Do not attribute commits/pushes to Claude.
   - No `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailers on commits that will be pushed to this remote.
   - No GitHub PRs created by me. The user merges branches manually.
3. **Test before asking to push.** Do not surface "ready to push?" until the change has been verified working — rebuild affected frontends, restart/migrate the bench, walk through the reproduction path, and confirm the original bug is gone AND nothing adjacent broke. If something can only be tested in the user's browser session, say so and wait for his confirmation before raising the push question.
4. **Current direction: reduce permission restrictions.** The existing role-gating in URY is tangled and blocks legitimate users (including Administrator). Default to loosening overly aggressive checks, and always surface other restrictive checks you notice to the user *before* touching them.
5. **Log every fix in the Fixes log section below.** Each entry: date, symptom, root cause, file(s) changed, verification. Future-me reads this before making similar changes to avoid reintroducing the same bug.
6. **Write unit tests for non-trivial features and run them before asking to push.** Any feature with real logic — resolver functions, validators, math over time / dates / money, schema expansion rules, overlap detection, etc. — gets a test file with MULTIPLE scenarios (happy path, edge cases, boundary conditions, negative cases). The goal is that the user does NOT have to manually verify every scenario; the test suite catches regressions. Tests live alongside the doctype (`test_<doctype>.py`) following Frappe convention. Run them via `bench --site <site> execute <module>.<run_function>` if `bench run-tests` is blocked by ERPNext bootstrap issues on the site (this is a known pre-existing blocker — use a `run_*_tests()` wrapper that calls `unittest.TextTestRunner` directly). Manual browser testing by the user is for FINAL end-to-end validation only, never as a substitute for unit tests. See [ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/test_ury_shift_assignment.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/test_ury_shift_assignment.py) for the pattern (24 scenarios for cross-midnight shifts).
7. **Keep session memory current** at `~/.claude/projects/-home-patoo-fb-dev16-5-apps-ury/memory/`. After any meaningful checkpoint, update the relevant memory file so the next session can resume without re-explanation.

## What this is

URY is a **Frappe/ERPNext custom app** for restaurant management (POS, KDS, P&L reports). It depends on `erpnext` and `hrms` and is installed into a Frappe bench via `bench get-app` / `bench install-app`. Nothing in this repo runs standalone — it must live inside a `frappe-bench/apps/ury` checkout and be served by a Frappe site.

Current working checkout: [apps/ury](.) inside `fb-dev16-5` bench. Sibling paths `../../../sites/common_site_config.json` and `../../frappe` are assumed to exist (the Vite dev servers read `common_site_config.json` directly — see [urypos/proxyOptions.js](urypos/proxyOptions.js)).

## Repository layout — the four sub-projects

This is a **polyglot monorepo** containing one Python app and three independent Node frontends. They share no code; each is built and shipped separately, but all three frontend bundles end up served by the same Frappe site.

| Path | Stack | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| [ury/](ury/) | Python / Frappe | Backend: DocTypes, hooks, whitelisted APIs, fixtures, scheduled tasks |
| [pos/](pos/) | React 19 + TypeScript + Vite + Zustand + Tailwind | **v2 POS** (current) — terminal-based cashier UI |
| [urypos/](urypos/) | Vue 3 + Vite + Pinia + Tailwind + Flowbite | **v1 POS** (legacy) — support ends Dec 2025 per [README.md](README.md) |
| [URYMosaic/](URYMosaic/) | Vue 3 + Vite + Tailwind | Kitchen Display System (KDS) with KOT printing |

All three frontends use [frappe-js-sdk](https://github.com/The-Commit-Company/frappe-js-sdk) to call whitelisted Python methods, and [qz-tray](https://qz.io/) for direct-to-printer output. The React `pos/` also uses `socket.io-client`-style realtime via a custom listener ([pos/src/lib/kot-listener.ts](pos/src/lib/kot-listener.ts)); the Vue apps do the same.

## Build & install — the critical detail

**Each frontend builds into `ury/public/<app>/` and its HTML entry is copied into `ury/www/<app>.html`.** That's how Frappe's website layer picks them up and routes `/pos`, `/urypos`, `/URYMosaic` (see [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py#L48-L52) `website_route_rules`). Both output directories are **gitignored** — they are regenerated on every build.

Top-level [package.json](package.json) orchestrates all three:

```bash
yarn install           # runs postinstall → installs all 3 frontends
yarn build             # builds all 3 frontends in sequence
```

Individual subproject commands (run from the subproject directory):

```bash
# pos/ (React)
cd pos && yarn dev              # Vite dev server
cd pos && yarn build            # build → ../ury/public/pos + copy HTML
cd pos && yarn lint             # ESLint (TypeScript)

# urypos/ (Vue legacy)
cd urypos && yarn dev
cd urypos && yarn build         # build → ../ury/public/urypos + copy HTML

# URYMosaic/ (Vue KDS)
cd URYMosaic && yarn dev
cd URYMosaic && yarn build      # build → ../ury/public/URYMosaic + copy HTML
```

The Vite dev servers in `urypos/` and `URYMosaic/` proxy `/app|/api|/assets|/files` to the Frappe webserver port read from `sites/common_site_config.json` — so Frappe must be running for dev to work. `pos/`'s [vite.config.ts](pos/vite.config.ts) does **not** configure a proxy (check before adding one).

**When you change frontend code you must `yarn build` for it to appear on the site** — Frappe serves the built assets, not the dev sources, unless you've mounted the Vite dev server separately.

## Installing / running the app inside a bench

Standard Frappe lifecycle (from [INSTALLATION.md](INSTALLATION.md)):

```bash
bench get-app ury <url>               # already done in this checkout
bench --site <sitename> install-app ury
bench --site <sitename> migrate
bench --site <sitename> build         # Frappe asset pipeline
bench --site <sitename> run-tests --app ury    # server tests
```

Node ≥ 18.20 required. `required_apps = ["erpnext"]` is enforced in [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py#L11); `hrms` is a soft dependency for employee reports.

## Backend architecture

The Python package is a standard Frappe app. Key entry points:

- **[ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py)** — the manifest. Declares DocType event hooks, scheduler tasks, fixtures, `page_js` overrides, and web route rules. **This is the first file to read when tracing how a POS action reaches the server.**
- **[ury/ury/doctype/](ury/ury/doctype/)** — ~30 custom DocTypes (URY KOT, URY Menu, URY Table, URY Restaurant, URY Daily P and L, sub-POS closing types, etc.). Each follows the Frappe convention: `<name>.json` (schema) + `<name>.py` (controller) + optional `<name>.js` (desk form script).
- **[ury/ury/hooks/](ury/ury/hooks/)** — doc-event handlers wired up in `hooks.py` under `doc_events`. These extend stock ERPNext DocTypes (POS Invoice, Sales Invoice, POS Profile, POS Opening/Closing Entry, Customer, Item) with URY-specific validation and side effects.
- **[ury/ury/api/](ury/ury/api/)** — whitelisted endpoints called from the frontends (KOT generation, KOT display, KOT reprint, order numbering, printing, validation, menu course validation, button permission).
- **[ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)** — additional whitelisted API surface (`getTable`, `getRestaurantMenu`, …) specifically for the POS frontends.
- **[ury/www/pos.py](ury/www/pos.py), [ury/www/pos.html](ury/www/pos.html)** (and `urypos.*`, `URYMosaic.*`) — Frappe www entry points. `pos.py` builds the boot context (CSRF, session) and the `.html` is the built Vite shell copied in by `copy-html-entry`.
- **[ury/public/js/](ury/public/js/)** — desk-side JS loaded via `app_include_js` and `page_js` in `hooks.py` (POS print hooks, quick entry, KOT integration inside the Frappe desk).

Scheduled task: `ury.ury.api.ury_kot_validation.kotValidationThread` runs **every minute** (`scheduler_events.cron`, [hooks.py:157-163](ury/hooks.py#L157-L163)).

Fixtures in `hooks.py` export a large set of Custom Fields (mostly on POS Invoice, Sales Invoice, POS Profile, POS Opening/Closing Entry, URY Menu Course, Printer Settings) plus `Role` records named `URY %` and all `Client Script`s. **When adding a Custom Field that URY relies on, append its name to the fixtures list in hooks.py** or it will not ship to other installations.

## Frontend architecture — `pos/` (React v2)

- State: **Zustand** store in [pos/src/store/pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts), organized into slices under [pos/src/store/slices/](pos/src/store/slices/) (auth, config, orders).
- API client layer: [pos/src/lib/](pos/src/lib/) — one file per domain (`menu-api`, `order-api`, `invoice-api`, `payment-api`, `pos-profile-api`, `terminal-api`, `customer-api`, `aggregator-api`, `kot-listener`, `print-qz`, `pos-display`, …). All wrap `frappe-js-sdk`.
- Routing: React Router v6, basename `/pos` ([pos/src/App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx#L126)). Pages in [pos/src/pages/](pos/src/pages/).
- Terminal gating: on boot, `App.tsx` resolves a saved terminal from localStorage via `terminal-api`, then shows `TerminalSetupScreen` if none is registered. Terminals are `URY POS Terminal` DocTypes.
- Printing: QZ Tray via [pos/src/lib/print-qz.ts](pos/src/lib/print-qz.ts) (signed with `privateKey.ts` — **do not commit real keys**; `cert.pem` is gitignored).
- Path alias `@` → `src/` (see [pos/vite.config.ts](pos/vite.config.ts)).

## Frontend architecture — `urypos/` (Vue v1, legacy)

- State: **Pinia** stores in [urypos/src/stores/](urypos/src/stores/) — one per domain (`Auth`, `Menu`, `Table`, `Customer`, `invoiceData`, `posOpening`, `posClosing`, `recentOrder`, `Notification`, `frappeSdk`, …).
- Router guards in [urypos/src/main.js](urypos/src/main.js#L19-L29) redirect unauthenticated users to Login and auth'd users away from Login.
- Uses `@syncfusion/ej2-vue-calendars`, `flowbite-vue`, `jsrsasign` (for QZ Tray signing), `moment`.
- Path alias `@` → `src/`.

## Frontend architecture — `URYMosaic/` (Vue KDS)

- Minimal Vue 3 app: two views ([Home.vue](URYMosaic/src/views/Home.vue), [Login.vue](URYMosaic/src/views/Login.vue)) and a `kot.vue` component. Uses `masonry-layout` for the KOT tile board.
- No Pinia; state is component-local. Realtime KOT updates come from `socket.io-client` subscriptions to the Frappe realtime bus.

## Testing

Server-side tests run inside bench:

```bash
bench --site <site> set-config allow_tests true
bench --site <site> run-tests --app ury              # all tests
bench --site <site> run-tests --app ury --doctype "URY KOT"   # one doctype
bench --site <site> run-tests --app ury --module ury.ury.doctype.ury_kot.test_ury_kot
```

CI ([.github/workflows/ci.yml](.github/workflows/ci.yml)) spins up a fresh bench with MariaDB, installs URY, and runs `bench run-tests --app ury`. Note CI currently pins Python 3.10 and Node 14 — the Node 14 pin is stale relative to the ≥18.20 requirement in `INSTALLATION.md`; prefer local Node 18+ for development.

No frontend test suites are configured. `pos/` has `yarn lint` (ESLint); the Vue projects have no lint script wired up in their `package.json`.

## Brand naming — "URY" vs "ExPOS Restaurant"

**The product has been rebranded from URY to ExPOS Restaurant, but the underlying DocType names, Python package, URL slugs, and file paths are all still `URY …`**. The rebrand is implemented through Frappe's translation layer, not via renames.

- **Translation source:** [ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv) — maps `URY → ExPOS`, `URY Restaurant → ExPOS Restaurant`, `URY Menu Item → ExPOS Menu Item`, etc. Add new mappings here when you reference a new DocType name in user-visible Python strings.
- **Workspace labels** use the `"label": "ExPOS X"` / `"link_to": "URY X"` split (see [ury/ury/workspace/ury/ury.json](ury/ury/workspace/ury/ury.json) line 42+).
- **What to do in backend Python** — when a `frappe.throw` / `frappe.msgprint` message references a DocType name, **wrap the DocType name in `_()` and interpolate it with `.format()`** so the en.csv mapping applies. Example:
  ```python
  frappe.throw(
      _("No {0} is configured for branch '{1}'. Open '{0}' in the desk...").format(
          _("URY Restaurant"), branch_name
      ),
      title=_("Restaurant Not Configured"),
  )
  ```
  Do NOT write `_("No URY Restaurant is configured...")` — the translator matches whole strings, so the mapping in en.csv (`URY Restaurant`) won't fire inside that longer sentence.
- **What to do in the React POS (`pos/`)** — there is no translation layer. **Hardcode the brand string** (`ExPOS Restaurant`, `ExPOS Menu Item`, etc.) in user-visible text. Keep URL paths, DocType arguments, and API payloads literal — they still reference the real `URY …` names (e.g. the deep-link target is `/app/ury-menu-item/new`, not `/app/expos-menu-item/new`). If the brand changes again, grep for `ExPOS` in `pos/src/` to find every hardcoded occurrence.
- **Never rename the DocTypes, Python package, URL slugs, or file paths.** A rename would break the database schema, all existing records, and all API callers. This is a label-only rebrand.

## Gotchas

- **The React POS (`pos/`) is an installable PWA — ALWAYS consider it when changing the POS frontend (2026-07-13).** It ships a web app manifest ([pos/public/manifest.json](pos/public/manifest.json), `start_url`/`scope` = `/pos`, `display: standalone`), app icons ([pos/public/icons/](pos/public/icons/) — 192 / 512 / maskable-512 / apple-touch-180, all committed source, NOT the gitignored build output), PWA meta in [pos/index.html](pos/index.html), and a mobile/tablet install prompt ([pos/src/components/InstallPrompt.tsx](pos/src/components/InstallPrompt.tsx)). Implications for any POS frontend change:
  - **Design for standalone + mobile/tablet.** When installed it runs full-screen with NO browser chrome (no address/back bar), so navigation must be reachable in-app (bottom nav, in-app back). Test at mobile/tablet widths, not just desktop.
  - **Use `h-[100dvh]`, never `h-screen`, for the app shell / full-height layout** — `100vh` hides the bottom nav/cart behind the mobile browser's address bar. See [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx).
  - **Assets are served under the Vite base `/assets/ury/pos/`.** Public files (`pos/public/*`) land there; reference PWA URLs absolutely (`/assets/ury/pos/...`). If you rename/move icons or the manifest, update index.html + the manifest.
  - **Keep the manifest valid** (name, `start_url`/`scope` `/pos`, 192+512 icons). Use `manifest.json` (NOT `.webmanifest`) so Frappe/nginx serve it as JSON. PWA install only works over **HTTPS** (won't prompt on `http://localhost`).
  - **No service worker yet** (so no offline). Adding one needs a Frappe route so the SW scope can cover `/pos` (assets are under `/assets/ury/pos/`) — see the 2026-07-13 PWA fixes log entry.
- **Gitignored build outputs.** `ury/public/{pos,urypos,URYMosaic}` and `ury/www/{pos,urypos,URYMosaic}.html` are all gitignored. Don't edit them — they get overwritten by `yarn build`. Edit the source under `pos/src/`, `urypos/src/`, or `URYMosaic/src/` instead.
- **Three frontends, one backend.** When changing a whitelisted API, check all three frontends for callers. They don't share a client — each has its own API layer.
- **`pos/` has `main.tsx.backup` and `print.ts(.backup)` files in `src/lib/`.** Treat these as developer scratch; don't import from them.
- **Custom Fields live in fixtures, not DocType JSON.** Look in [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py#L249-L381) `fixtures` to see which fields are shipped. A field not in that list will not be installed on other sites.
- **QZ Tray signing keys.** `pos/privateKey.js`, `urypos/privateKey.js`, `pos/src/privateKey.ts`, and `ury/public/pos/assets/ury/files/cert.pem` are local-only — the `.pem` is gitignored; the `privateKey*` files contain placeholders in the repo. Never commit real keys.
- **Legacy POS sunset.** `urypos/` and `URYMosaic/` v1 support ends Dec 2025 per README. New work should target the React `pos/` unless specifically fixing the legacy apps. Always confirm with the user which POS they mean before making changes.
- **URY role permissions baseline lives in `ury/permissions.py`.** `URY Cashier`, `URY Captain`, and `URY Manager` are URY-specific roles that don't have permissions on standard ERPNext doctypes (POS Profile, POS Invoice, Customer, Item, etc.) by default. The `ensure_role_permissions()` function in `ury/permissions.py` defines a baseline set of `Custom DocPerm` rows and runs idempotently on every `after_install` and `after_migrate`. **When you add a new doctype to the React POS API surface, also add it to `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` or `WRITE_DOCTYPES` in `permissions.py`** — otherwise users with only the URY role hit a 403. The function skips existing Custom DocPerm rows so admin tweaks aren't overwritten on every migrate.
- **Custom fields have a dual source of truth — keep them in sync.** URY ships custom fields TWO ways and they're not equivalent:
  1. **`ury/fixtures/custom_field.json` + `hooks.py` `fixtures` list** — declarative. Imported on `bench install-app ury`. **NOT re-imported on `bench migrate`** (this is intentional Frappe behavior to prevent overwriting admin customizations).
  2. **`ury/setup.py` `get_custom_fields()` → `create_custom_fields()`** — programmatic. Called from `after_install` AND `after_migrate` (both hooks wired in `hooks.py`). Idempotent — re-runs every migrate, only creates fields that don't already exist.
  **Rule:** any custom field added to URY MUST live in BOTH places, or pre-existing installs (sites that installed URY before the field shipped) will silently lose the field. The failure mode is brutal: `doc.custom_xyz = value` becomes a no-op write because the doctype doesn't know about the column. No error, no log line, the value just disappears at save time. The 2026-04-09 Orders page debugging session burned a full round trip on this — `custom_terminal` was in `custom_field.json` but not `setup.py`, so existing sites had no terminal column on POS Invoice and orders never got tagged. When you add a custom field, edit both files in the same commit.
- **POS Opening Entry is per-terminal as of 2026-04-08.** Don't re-add `main_pos_open_check` (the captain-must-be-open gate) or `sub_pos_close_check` (the sub-cashier-must-close-first gate) to [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py) or [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py). Both were deleted on purpose. Under the new model, `posOpening(terminal)` returns 0 if any open entry exists for that terminal (shared mode, multi_cashier OFF) or if THIS user has an open entry on that terminal (strict mode, multi_cashier ON). The captain/sub-cashier ordering is gone — anyone can open the first entry of the day, and per-invoice attribution is preserved through `POS Invoice.owner` + `POS Invoice.custom_terminal`. The `custom_main_cashier` checkbox on POS Profile and the `custom_rooms` table on POS Opening Entry are deprecated (still in the schema for backward compat, but no longer read by the backend).
- **`POS Settings.invoice_type` must be "POS Invoice".** URY's order flow creates `POS Invoice` docs directly; if the site's `POS Settings.invoice_type` is set to "Sales Invoice" (the other ERPNext mode), every POS order submission throws `"Sales Invoice mode is activated in POS. Please create Sales Invoice instead."` from `validate_is_pos_using_sales_invoice` in [apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py). `ury.install.ensure_pos_settings_configured()` auto-sets this on every `after_install` and `after_migrate` (wired in [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py)), so a `bench migrate` brings drifted sites back into a working state automatically. If you're debugging a "Sales Invoice mode is activated" error: check the `POS Settings` singleton in the desk — something (manual edit, another app) has flipped it back.
- **A Custom DocPerm row REPLACES a DocType's built-in permissions — it does not merge.** As soon as **any** `Custom DocPerm` row exists for a doctype, Frappe ignores that DocType's built-in `DocPerm` rows *wholesale* (`Meta.get_permissions` loads Custom DocPerm and only falls back to the built-ins when there are none). So adding one custom row for one new role silently strips access from **every** role that relied on a built-in row — including ERPNext roles you never touched (Sales User, Accounts User, HR Manager, System Manager…). This bit URY hard on 2026-07-16: giving the new **URY Waiter** role a row on `URY Menu Course` left URY Cashier/URY Captain with `has_permission(... "read") == False` ("Failed to load menu categories") even though their built-in rows were still sitting in `tabDocPerm`. **Rule:** before adding the first Custom DocPerm to a doctype, copy the existing built-ins across as custom rows — [`permissions.py _materialize_builtin_perms()`](ury/permissions.py) does this and runs automatically from `ensure_role_permissions()`. And never assume "a built-in already grants this role" means the role is covered — check whether the doctype has been customised first.

- **`patches.txt` section headers matter — and BOTH must be present.** Any patch that reads columns/fields added by the same migration MUST be listed under `[post_model_sync]`. Patches without a section header default to `[pre_model_sync]`, which runs BEFORE Frappe syncs DocType JSON changes to the database. **Second trap:** once ANY section header exists in the file, Frappe's parser requires BOTH `[pre_model_sync]` and `[post_model_sync]` to be present or `bench migrate` throws `Patch type PatchType.pre_model_sync not found in patches.txt`. Always keep the file shaped like this even if one section is empty:
  ```
  [pre_model_sync]

  [post_model_sync]
  ury.patches.vX_Y.some_backfill
  ```
  Also: give any patch that reads a new column a defensive `frappe.db.has_column(...)` guard at the top so a mis-ordered `patches.txt` degrades to a noisy no-op instead of crashing the whole migration.

- **QZ Tray does NOT run on tablets/phones — printing from a tablet needs a LAN "print server".** QZ Tray is a Java desktop daemon; it installs on Windows / macOS / Linux only. **There is no iOS or Android build** and there never will be (no JVM on those OSes). So the desktop model — cashier on Windows with the printer attached, `pos/src/lib/print-qz.ts` connecting to `localhost` — only works on a desktop that is itself running QZ. A tablet cashier can't run a local QZ, so it must reach one over the LAN. **This is IMPLEMENTED via the POS Profile `qz_host` field (see the 2026-07-14 Fixes log entry):**
  - **Print Server mode.** Run ONE QZ Tray instance on any always-on Windows/Mac/mini-PC on the same LAN as the printers (this box doesn't need to be a POS terminal — it's just the print gateway). Set **POS Profile → QZ Host** to that machine's LAN IP (e.g. `192.168.1.50`). Every tablet on that profile then connects to the gateway instead of `localhost`. Both the **bill** ([print.ts](pos/src/lib/print.ts)) and the **KOTs** ([kot-listener.ts](pos/src/lib/kot-listener.ts) → `printKotWithQz(printer, html, qz_host)`) are sent to that host — the backend returns `qz_host` on `get_latest_kot` / `print_pending_kots_for_invoice` so the KOT path uses the same gateway as the bill. Empty `qz_host` ⇒ `localhost` (unchanged desktop behaviour).
  - **Secure vs insecure is auto-derived in `print-qz.ts` (`qzUsesSecure`):** `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` → insecure WS (loopback is exempt from the browser mixed-content block, so `ws://localhost` works even on an HTTPS POS page). A **remote host → WSS (port 8181)** because `ws://<remote-ip>` from an HTTPS POS page is mixed content and browsers silently block it. (Trade-off: if a site serves the POS over plain HTTP and wanted insecure `ws://` to a remote gateway, that path is no longer taken — add an explicit `custom_qz_secure` toggle if that ever comes up. The tablet use case is HTTPS, which needs WSS anyway.)
  - **TLS / cert gotchas on the tablet:**
    - **iOS Safari** blocks WSS to a self-signed cert. The QZ community cert must be trusted OR you use QZ's signed override cert; on iPad you install the QZ cert via a config profile (Settings → General → VPN & Device Management → trust the cert). Without it the WSS handshake silently fails.
    - **Android Chrome** — same self-signed WSS problem. Either install the cert to the Android trust store, or connect over insecure **WS (8182)** and flag the gateway origin as allowed (`chrome://flags` "Insecure origins treated as secure" for `http://<gateway-ip>:8182`). Insecure-WS is easier for a closed store LAN but is plaintext on the wire.
  - **Bottom line to tell a customer:** yes, tablet printing to a networked printer is possible, but it needs (1) a small always-on PC on the shop LAN running QZ Tray as a print gateway, (2) the QZ host configured in the POS Profile, and (3) the QZ cert trusted on each tablet (or insecure-WS + a Chrome flag). It is NOT a pure-tablet, zero-extra-hardware setup — a desktop/mini-PC somewhere on the network is mandatory because QZ can't run on the tablet itself. An alternative that skips QZ entirely is a **cloud/LAN print API** (e.g. a printer with native ESC/POS-over-TCP, or a service like PrintNode) — bigger rework, parked.

## Fixes log

Running record of bugs fixed and non-obvious traps discovered. Add new entries at the top. Each entry should answer: what went wrong, why, where it was fixed, how it was verified.

### 2026-07-30 — Take-away / delivery contact prompt (optional, editable afterwards)
- **Ask (user):** "when there is a takeout or delivery order when the user is adding the order for the first time it should come with a prompt dialogue box for the user to enter the name and also the mobile number of the person and it should not be mandatory. but the user can click on the order and edit and add the name or phone number or edit it as well later."
- **Storage — two NEW POS Invoice custom fields**, `custom_order_contact_name` + `custom_order_contact_mobile` (Data). **Deliberately NOT reusing the existing `mobile_number` field**: that one carries `fetch_from: "customer.mobile_number"` with no `fetch_if_empty`, so ERPNext re-pulls the CUSTOMER's number on every save and would silently overwrite a per-order value. Both new fields are `allow_on_submit` so a number can still be added to an already-PAID delivery — which is exactly when it's usually needed — and both carry `depends_on eval:doc.order_type=='Take Away' || doc.order_type=='Delivery'` so they stay hidden in the desk for other order types. Declared in all three sources ([custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures) per the dual-source rule. `custom_order_contact_mobile` gets `search_index` + `in_standard_filter` so an order can be found by phone number.
- **Prompt ([OrderContactDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderContactDialog.tsx)):** fires on the FIRST submit of a NEW Take Away / Delivery order. Both fields optional and visibly so — **Skip is a first-class button**, not a quiet link, because a walk-in take-away often has neither and making the cashier hunt for an escape would slow the queue. The mobile input is `type="tel"` **not `number`** (number strips leading zeros and rejects `+`, spaces and dashes that people actually type). The dialog re-seeds on every open — it stays mounted, so without that it would show the PREVIOUS order's details (the same trap already fixed in CommentDialog).
- **Composing with the waiter prompt was the fiddly part** ([OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx)). `handleSubmit(waiterOverride?, contactOverride?)` — the contact gate runs AFTER the waiter gate so the two chain instead of racing: waiter dialog re-enters `handleSubmit` with its pick, we reach the contact gate, that dialog re-enters with BOTH. `pendingWaiter` carries the waiter across the contact prompt, otherwise a waiter-using profile would lose it on the second pass.
  - **`contactPrompted` is what prevents an infinite loop.** The contact is optional, so "skipped" and "left blank" are indistinguishable — emptiness can NOT be used to mean "not yet asked". It's set BEFORE re-entering.
  - **Re-armed on cart-empty and on order-type change.** Without the cart-empty reset the flag would survive a successful submit and only the FIRST take-away of a whole shift would ever be asked.
- **Editing later ([Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) + new `update_order_contact`):** the right panel shows a Contact block for Take Away / Delivery orders with an Add/Edit button reusing the same dialog in `mode="edit"` (one component, so field rules and the phone keypad can't drift between capture and correction). The number renders as a `tel:` link so it's tappable on a tablet. The panel is updated optimistically via `selectOrder` so the change shows without waiting for a refetch.
- **`update_order_contact` semantics:** a **blank string CLEARS** the field (so a mistyped number can be removed, not just overwritten); works on paid invoices as well as drafts; **rejects cancelled** invoices. Writes only the two non-financial Data fields through `db.set_value`, so it never re-runs invoice validation or touches totals.
- **`sync_order` only stamps a NON-EMPTY value** — so a later update that doesn't carry the contact can't wipe one the cashier added from the Orders page. Verified explicitly.
- **Verified on dev:** `bench migrate` created both columns (`Data`, `allow_on_submit=1`). Create with contact → values stored and **whitespace-trimmed**; edit later → updated; blank → **cleared**; and an update WITHOUT the contact → **name preserved** (the no-wipe guard). A missing invoice is rejected. `py_compile` + `tsc` clean; `eslint` shows the **same 7 pre-existing problems before and after**; `yarn build` clean. All test invoices/KOTs removed and the opening entry's dates restored.
- **Deploy: `bench migrate` (2 new fields) + `bench restart` (new whitelisted method + changed `sync_order` signature) + redeploy the `pos/` build.**
- **NOT done:** the contact is not added to any print format — the client runs customised formats and silently editing those is riskier than useful. `print_hide_if_no_value` is set on both fields so they'd render cleanly if a format is extended later.
### 2026-07-30 — Settings page + KOT routing coverage check (catches the silent "item group in no production unit" loss)
- **The bug it exists to catch (found live on the Airport branch):** in **URY Production Unit** KDS mode, an item whose `item_group` matches no production unit gets **NO KOT AT ALL** — `process_items_for_kot` only builds the fallback KOT when `kds_mode != "URY Production Unit"` (deliberate, 2026-06-11: a drink shouldn't raise a spurious kitchen chit). The cost is that a forgotten item group **vanishes silently**: it bills, it prints on the receipt, and it never reaches any kitchen or bar screen. No error, no log line, nothing.
  - Live case: `BEER DRINKS` (13 items) was never added to the Bar unit. **Every beer ever sold was invisible to the bar.** Noticed months later, and only by accident during an order update.
  - **Verified it is NOT update-specific** — a brand-new order with a beer produces `KOTs created: 0`; a beer + food order produces only the kitchen ticket with the beer absent. The user first saw it on an update simply because the rest of the ticket still appears on a new order, so nothing looks wrong.
- **New backend `get_kot_coverage_audit(terminal=None)`** ([api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)): resolves the branch's menus via the existing `_collect_menus_for_restaurant` (so it covers `active_menu` + room-wise + order-type-wise menus, not just the default), pulls every `URY Menu Item`'s item group, and reports per group: `covered`, the `productions` it routes to, `item_count`, and `sample_items` (so an admin recognises what a group actually contains). Also returns `drops_unmatched_items` — **1 only in PU mode**; in Menu Course mode unmatched items land in a fallback KOT, so the page says so rather than raising a false alarm.
  - Gated by new **`_user_can_manage_settings()`** — Administrator / System Manager / **URY Manager only**. Deliberately EXCLUDES URY Captain (unlike most `_user_can_*` helpers here): this is branch-wide config, an owner/manager concern, not floor ops.
  - Also flags production units with **zero** item groups (dev site has `ChineseKitchen` like this) — they can never receive an order.
- **New Settings page** ([Settings.tsx](pos/src/pages/Settings.tsx)) at `/settings`, reached from the avatar menu. Built as a stack of independent **sections** (`KotCoverageSection` is the only one today) specifically so further settings drop in without restructuring — that was the user's stated intent.
  - Route wrapped in new [RequireSettingsAccess.tsx](pos/src/components/RequireSettingsAccess.tsx) so a manually-typed `/settings` bounces. Implemented as a route WRAPPER, not an early return inside the page — Settings calls hooks after the role read and an early return would break the Rules of Hooks (same reasoning as `RequireNotWaiter`). It only redirects when `user` is non-null, so a slow first paint doesn't bounce a legitimate admin.
  - Frontend gate `canAccessSettings` ([role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts)) mirrors the backend; the server check is authoritative.
- **Cart button relabelled** "Add New Order" → **"Place Order"** ([OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx)). The "Update Order" label for an existing draft is unchanged.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** the user explicitly declined the companion idea of logging dropped items server-side when PU mode discards unmatched items. The Settings check is the chosen mechanism — a pre-service check rather than post-hoc forensics.
- **Verified on dev:** the endpoint reproduces the live finding exactly — `BEER DRINKS 13 items NOT COVERED`, `uncovered_count=1`, `uncovered_item_total=13`, with the other four groups routed to Bar / Main Kitchen; `drops_unmatched_items=1` for this PU-mode profile. Permission gate: Administrator ALLOWED, a URY Waiter gets `PermissionError`. `py_compile` clean; `tsc` clean; `eslint` shows the **same 2 pre-existing problems before and after** (Header's unused `error`, OrderPanel's `any`) — none introduced; `yarn build` clean.
- **Deploy: `bench restart` (new whitelisted method) + redeploy the `pos/` build.** No migrate — no schema change.
- **Config fix this surfaced for the client:** add `BEER DRINKS` to the Bar production unit, and either populate or remove `ChineseKitchen`. Note this client does **not** print KOTs from production units at all (KDS screens only), so the separate per-terminal printer gaps flagged on 2026-07-30 are irrelevant to them — item-group coverage is what actually matters.

### 2026-07-29 — PIN management: first PIN needs no old PIN, admins can set another user's, all changes logged
- **Ask (user):** "if a user logs in and the user has not set a pin yet it should only set the new pin rather than asking him the old pin too. when the administrator or captain logs in too he should be able to select a user and set the pin for him or her as well. and lets keep a log so later administrator can pull a report on those changes done."
- **#1 — First PIN was IMPOSSIBLE to self-serve.** `change_pin` demanded `old_pin` unconditionally, and before that it hard-threw twice: no enrollment → *"Ask a captain to enrol you first"*; enrollment but empty `pin_hash` → *"No PIN is set on your enrollment. Ask a captain to reset it."* So a user with no PIN could never give themselves one. **Fixed:** `change_pin(new_pin, old_pin=None, terminal=None)` now branches — **no `pin_hash` ⇒ first-PIN path**, no `old_pin` required, and the enrollment is **auto-created** when missing (`_create_pin_only_enrollment`; `fingerprint_template` is not a required field, so a PIN-only enrollment is valid and is the normal state on a terminal with no reader). When a PIN **does** exist, `old_pin` stays mandatory and is verified against the same lockout counter `pin_login` uses — so this is not a bypass.
  - **`old_pin` moved to the SECOND parameter** because it's now optional. Safe: every caller passes named arguments (Frappe posts a JSON body), so parameter order isn't part of the contract.
- **#2 — Captains/admins can set someone else's PIN.** New **`admin_set_user_pin(user, new_pin, terminal=None)`** — `_require_enrollment_admin()` gated, no old PIN by design (it IS the forgotten-PIN recovery path), auto-creates a PIN-only enrollment, and **clears any lockout** so the cashier can trade immediately. Rejects users without a URY login role.
- **#3 — Audit trail.** New **`URY PIN Change Log`** doctype (`PINLOG-{YYYY}-{MM}-{#####}`): `user`, `action` (PIN Set / PIN Changed / PIN Set By Admin / Enrollment Unlocked / Enrollment Deleted), `performed_by`, `performed_at`, `self_service`, `terminal`, `ip_address`, `notes`. Written by `_log_pin_event` from **five** call sites — `change_pin` (both branches), `admin_set_user_pin`, `enroll_biometric` (when a PIN is supplied), and both `admin_reset_enrollment` outcomes.
  - **It NEVER stores a PIN or hash** — verified by asserting no log field contains the test PIN.
  - **Permissions are read-only ON PURPOSE.** [permissions.py](ury/permissions.py) grants `read/select/report/export` and deliberately **no write/create/delete**: the log is evidence and must not be editable by the people it records. Rows are inserted only with `ignore_permissions=True`.
  - **`_log_pin_event` is best-effort** (try/except → `frappe.log_error`). A failed audit insert must never roll back or block the PIN change — a cashier locked out of the POS because a log row failed would be far worse than a missing line.
  - **The "report" is the desk list view**, not a POS tab: `user`, `action`, `performed_by` and `self_service` are all `in_standard_filter`, sorted by `performed_at DESC`, with export. Audit data belongs in the desk where only elevated roles reach it.
- **Frontend ([ResetPinDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ResetPinDialog.tsx)):** on open it calls the new **`get_my_pin_status()`** (`has_enrollment` / `has_pin` / `can_set_for_others`) and **hides the Current PIN field entirely** when `has_pin = 0`, retitling to "Set Your PIN". Captains/admins get a "Set a PIN for another user instead" switch → debounced user search (reuses `search_users_for_login`) → the picked row states whether they already have a PIN. **Fails SAFE:** if the status lookup errors, `status === null` ⇒ old PIN IS required (backend enforces the real rule regardless, so the worst case is a redundant field, never an unguarded change). `forced` (expiry) mode is untouched.
- **Verified on dev:** first-PIN self-set with no enrollment → enrollment auto-created, `action=pin_set`, PIN verifies; self-change **without** old PIN when one exists → correctly rejected ("Enter your current PIN to change it"); with the correct old PIN → `pin_changed`; admin sets another user's PIN → `pin_set_by_admin`. **Security:** a URY Waiter calling `admin_set_user_pin` → `PermissionError`; a non-URY target (Guest) → rejected; `12345`/`abcdef`/`1234567` → all rejected by `_validate_pin_format`; no log field contains the PIN. `bench migrate` created the doctype + 2 perm rows. `py_compile` + `tsc` + `eslint` (clean) + `yarn build` clean. All test enrollments and log rows deleted; site left with 0 of each and biometric `enabled` restored to 0.
- **Deploy: `bench migrate` (new doctype + permissions) + `bench restart` (new/changed whitelisted methods) + redeploy the `pos/` build.**
- **Note:** biometric features are gated behind `URY Biometric Settings.enabled`, which is **OFF** on the dev site — `_require_enabled()` fronts all of these, so the site must enable it for PIN login/reset to work at all.

### 2026-07-29 — Who opens/closes the day: soft gate on closing, opening left ungated
- **Ask (user):** "the captains are the one that opens the day and closes the day too. but there are some cases where the cashier too can open the day and close the day as well. how do you think we should handle this".
- **State before:** NO role gate on either action — `posOpening` has none, End Shift shows for every billing user, and `submit_pos_closing_entry` had no role check. Cashiers could already do both; what was missing was any expression of "captains normally".
- **The decision — treat opening and closing differently, because their risk isn't the same.** Opening just declares the starting float: low risk, trivially corrected. Closing counts the drawer and consolidates the shift's invoices into the GL: that's where money errors live and it's effectively irreversible once submitted.
- **THE TRAP that ruled out a hard captain-only close.** `custom_daily_pos_close` blocks the POS when the previous day is still open (`validate_pos_close` → the provider's closing branch). So captain-only closing means: captain away one night → nobody can close → **the next morning nobody can trade either**. That is the same shape as the 2026-07-28 open/close deadlock — a rule that's right on paper and takes the outlet down in practice. Do not re-add it as a hard block.
- **Chosen model (confirmed with the user): opening stays ungated; closing is soft-gated — warn and record, never block.**
  - **Frontend ([POSClosingDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx)):** a non-captain sees a one-time confirmation panel ("Closing the day is normally your captain's job… you can still continue") with Cancel / **Continue to Close** before reaching the form. Gated inside the DIALOG rather than at its call sites, so it covers **every** route in — Header End Shift, the Shift Hours banner, and both POS-gate branches (existing-entry + previous-day). Suppressed once `submitting`/`success` so it can't reappear mid-close.
  - **Backend ([api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `submit_pos_closing_entry`):** stamps the new **`POS Closing Entry.custom_closed_by_non_captain`** from `_user_is_captain()` — resolved **server-side from the session user**, never trusted from the client. The frontend panel is a prompt; this is the record.
- **Schema:** new `POS Closing Entry.custom_closed_by_non_captain` (Check, read-only, `no_copy`, **`in_standard_filter`** so a captain can filter the list view for it). Added to all three sources — [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures — per the dual-source rule; without the setup.py half, pre-existing installs would never get the column and the stamp would silently become a no-op write.
- **Trap hit while doing it (worth remembering):** editing `custom_field.json` by `json.load` → `json.dump` **mangles the file** — `ensure_ascii` escapes every em-dash to `—` and the indent normalises, producing a 674-hunk / 2460-line diff. Reverted and inserted the entry as plain text instead (54 insertions, 0 deletions). Always append to that fixture textually, never round-trip it through Python's json module.
- **Verified on dev:** `bench migrate` created the column (`has_column` True; Check / default 0 / read_only / in_standard_filter); `_user_is_captain()` resolves True for Administrator and False for a plain waiter, so the stamp would be 0 and 1 respectively; writing the field persists and `frappe.db.count(..., {"custom_closed_by_non_captain": 1})` returns it, confirming it's genuinely filterable for review (test value restored). `py_compile` + `tsc` + `eslint` (clean) + `yarn build` clean. No duplicate doctype key introduced in `setup.py` (checked via AST — the 2026-04-12 trap).
- **Deploy: `bench migrate` (new field) + `bench restart` (whitelisted method changed) + redeploy the `pos/` build.**
- **Deliberately NOT done:** no `custom_restrict_close_to_captain` toggle. It was offered and rejected in favour of the soft gate precisely because of the daily-close cascade above. If a site ever genuinely wants a hard block, it MUST also handle "captain unavailable" or it will strand the outlet.

### 2026-07-29 — Per-outlet warehouse resolution (cost centre picks the outlet, item default picks the category)
- **Symptom (live, Landing Restaurant):** a POS Invoice stuck at `docstatus=0`; paying it threw `Row #1: Item B5009 has no stock in warehouse ...`. The drink had **72 in stock** — in `Beverage Stores - Airport WH - LR`, not the warehouse the sale posted to.
- **Root cause:** in item-warehouse mode (`custom_use_pos_warehouse` OFF) `_resolve_item_warehouse` returned the item's `Item Default.default_warehouse` and nothing else. `Item Default` is keyed on `(item, company)`, so on a one-company site an item has exactly ONE default — it can express the stock *category* (beverage vs food) but not the *outlet* (Airport / Sitout / HO). Landing runs 11 warehouses in a 4-category x 3-outlet grid; 107 items defaulted to a warehouse holding no stock, and 33 items are stocked at both Airport and Sitout, so no single default could serve both tills. ERPNext's `PosInvoice.validate_stock_availablility` ([pos_invoice.py:415](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py#L415)) then throws for any item with `availability <= 0` and `Item.allow_negative_stock` off — correctly, but against the wrong warehouse.
- **Fix:** the till's **cost centre** supplies the outlet. `POS Profile.cost_center` was already stamped on every invoice line by `sync_order`, so the input existed — it just wasn't used for warehouse resolution.
  - New `Warehouse.custom_cost_center` (Link -> Cost Center) tags each leaf warehouse with its outlet. In BOTH [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) and [setup.py](ury/setup.py) per the dual-source-of-truth rule.
  - New pure `_pick_outlet_warehouse(default_warehouse, cost_center, warehouses)` in [ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py): take the category group the item's default belongs to (the default itself when it IS a group node, else its `parent_warehouse`), then pick the child tagged with the till's cost centre. Short-circuits when the default already carries that cost centre.
  - `_resolve_item_warehouse(item_code, company, cost_center=None)` gained the third argument and delegates. **Backwards compatible** — with no cost centre it returns the item default exactly as before, so sites that don't tag warehouses see zero change.
  - `sync_order` fetches the profile's cost centre ONCE into `invoice_cost_center` (it was re-querying `POS Profile.cost_center` per item row) and threads it into the resolver.
- **Deliberate non-goal — it never guesses.** When a category has no warehouse for the till's outlet, resolution falls back to the item's own default rather than picking another outlet's stock. Silently deducting a Sitout sale from Airport's shelf is worse than an error: it drifts both outlets' counts with no signal. The fallback lets ERPNext raise `Item has no stock in warehouse X`, which is the error the cashier should see.
- **THE TRAP THAT BIT US — data and code must ship together.** The warehouse tagging + repointing 95 beverage items at the category GROUP node was applied to landing's database while the resolver was still only an **uncommitted working-tree edit**. Someone then re-cloned ury on landing (`3a45c9b`), which wiped the edit but left the data. The old code returns the raw default — now a group node, which can never hold stock — so EVERY drink sale failed with `has no stock in warehouse Beverage Stores WH - LR` (note: the group, not a leaf). If you see that error, the resolver is missing from the running code. Never apply the data half of this change to a site whose ury does not already contain `_pick_outlet_warehouse`.
- **Verified:** 16/16 unit tests in [test_outlet_warehouse.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/test_outlet_warehouse.py) — Fanta-at-two-tills, group-node defaults, incomplete categories, untagged leaves, unknown cost centres, empty tree, determinism, and the backwards-compatible no-cost-centre path. Run via `run_outlet_warehouse_tests()` with `env/bin/python` and `apps/ury` on the path (the direct-runner pattern from rule 6; `bench run-tests` is blocked by the pre-existing ERPNext bootstrap issue). Also verified live on landing: ALVARO resolves to the Airport beverage store at the Airport till and the Sitout store at the Sitout till, and a dish resolves to each outlet's food store.
- **Companion fix in gh_erp (separate app):** `auto_replenishment.create_work_order` sourced BOM ingredients from `Auto Replenishment Settings.raw_material_warehouse` — a single global setting, so every outlet's dishes consumed the same outlet's ingredients. New `resolve_raw_material_warehouse(fg_warehouse)` resolves per outlet using the same cost-centre tag, guarded by `frappe.db.has_column` so gh_erp still works on benches without ury.
- **Site setup this needs:** each POS Profile must carry its outlet's cost centre (landing had both tills on `100 - Main`), each leaf warehouse must be tagged with `custom_cost_center`, and every outlet needs a warehouse per category — a category with no warehouse for an outlet silently falls back.
