### 2026-07-29 — PWA escape hatch on the POS gate screens + keyboard-aware login card
- **Ask (user):** "when the user is at the Open POS Entry interface they cant go back on the [PWA]. so lets add a logout on that page… also the login page on the [PWA] should fit and be smart and visible enough. currently when you click and start typing your name the card should move up so you see the list well rather than most of it hiding behind the keyboard."
- **#1 — Sign out on the POS gate screens.** These dialogs are `fixed inset-0` and the POS runs as an installed PWA with **no browser chrome — no address bar, no back button**. A cashier who can't get past the gate (signed in as the wrong user, someone else's shift open, outside their shift window) had literally no way out but to force-quit the app. Added a shared `handleGateLogout` (module-scope in [POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx): `logout()` → `window.location.href = '/pos'` → guest → login screen; toast on failure).
  - Implemented as an opt-in **`showLogout`** prop on the shared `DialogShell` rather than a button on one branch, so **every blocking branch** gets it: the open form, the existing-entry card, and the previous-day "Previous POS Not Closed" card. Deliberately OFF for the transient states (`loading`, the "POS Opened" success flash) — there's nothing to be stuck on there and a sign-out link would just be noise mid-transition.
  - **`DialogShell` also got `overflow-y-auto` + `items-start sm:items-center`.** The card can exceed the viewport on a phone (the open form grows with one row per mode of payment), and a vertically-centred non-scrolling flex child clips its OWN top and bottom with no way to reach them. **Trap noted:** an early version also had `my-auto` on the card — in flexbox an auto margin **overrides `align-items`**, so `items-start` would silently never apply and the clipping would remain. Don't re-add it.
- **#2 — Keyboard-aware login ([BiometricLogin.tsx](pos/src/pages/BiometricLogin.tsx)).** The page was `min-h-screen … items-center`, i.e. centred against the **layout** viewport. On iOS the layout viewport does **not** shrink when the keyboard opens — the keyboard just covers the bottom — so the card stayed centred against the full height and the search results ended up behind the keyboard.
  - New reusable hook [use-keyboard-inset.ts](pos/src/lib/use-keyboard-inset.ts): returns the px currently covered by the keyboard, derived from `window.visualViewport` (`window.innerHeight - vv.height - vv.offsetTop`). Subtracting `offsetTop` matters — without it we over-report once the browser scrolls the visual viewport to reveal a focused field. An 80px threshold stops a collapsing URL bar being mistaken for a keyboard. Returns **0** when the API is missing (older browsers) and on Android Chrome, which resizes the layout viewport itself — returning 0 there is correct, otherwise we'd compensate twice.
  - Applied as `paddingBottom: keyboardInset` on the centring container, so the card re-centres inside the **visible** area; plus `min-h-[100dvh]` (not `100vh`, so the collapsing URL bar doesn't cut the card off), `overflow-y-auto`, and a switch to `items-start` while the keyboard is up. The logo/heading shrink and the subtitle hides when the keyboard is open so the list gets that space.
  - The results list's fixed `max-h-80` (320px) is now computed from the space actually left (`innerHeight - keyboardInset - 230`, floored at 132px) instead of running off the bottom of the screen. Belt-and-braces `scrollIntoView` on input focus (300ms, after the keyboard animation) covers browsers where `visualViewport` is unavailable and the inset stays 0. Confirmed `Input` is a `React.forwardRef<HTMLInputElement, …>`, so the `ref` actually lands.
- **Verified:** `tsc` clean; `eslint` shows the **same single pre-existing `catch (err: any)` error before and after** (no new issues); `yarn build` clean. **Frontend-only → redeploy the `pos/` build. No migrate, no restart.** The on-device keyboard behaviour is the user's browser test — it can't be exercised headlessly.

### 2026-07-29 — Admin "Print or Mark Printed?" prompt (test invoices without burning receipts)
- **Ask (user):** "when the system administrator logs in and takes an order or payment and clicks on print there should be a dialogue that pops up to ask to print from printer or mark print… vital when me as the admin is testing the invoices and payments and don't need to send it to the printer each time."
- **Why it was needed:** URY gates the Payment button behind `invoice_printed = 1`, so an admin testing the order→payment flow had to physically print a receipt on EVERY test run just to unlock Payment.
- **New [PrintChoiceDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/PrintChoiceDialog.tsx):** two actions — **Print to Printer** (normal flow) and **Mark as Printed** (nothing sent to any printer). Shown only to users passing the new **`canSkipPhysicalPrint`** ([role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts)).
- **`canSkipPhysicalPrint` is deliberately TIGHTER than `isCaptainOrAbove`** — **Administrator + System Manager only**, NOT URY Manager / URY Captain. Those are floor-ops roles who should be printing real bills for customers; a one-click "pretend I printed it" on live orders is not a control to hand out by default. Widen the list in that one helper if a site needs it.
- **"Mark as Printed" calls the SAME endpoint a real print calls** (`ury.ury.api.ury_print.qz_print_update` via `updatePrintStatus`), so the invoice lands in the identical state — verified on dev: `invoice_printed 0→1` and `custom_print_count 0→1`. Payment unlocks exactly as it would after a real print. (The count incrementing is harmless: admins have unlimited reprints via `canReprintInvoice`.)
- **[Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx):** `handlePrintOrder` is now a thin gate — admins get the dialog, everyone else keeps the unchanged one-click print. The body moved to `runPrintFlow(mode)` so both paths share the post-print logic (optimistic `invoice_printed`, Unbilled→Draft move, list refresh). Covers BOTH the first-print and Reprint buttons (they share the handler).
- **Two deliberate behaviours in `mark` mode:** (1) **held KOTs are NOT fired** — firing physically prints them, and the whole point is that nothing reaches a printer; they honestly stay pending. (2) Consequently the order is **not** dropped from the "Pending KOTs" filter (that branch is now gated on `mode === 'printer'`), because it genuinely still has pending KOTs.
- **[PaymentDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx):** the post-payment courtesy auto-print (2026-06-12) is **skipped for admins** with an info toast instead of a dialog. Rationale: payment is already gated behind a first bill print, so that auto-print is a SECOND physical copy — pure waste while testing, and interrupting the payment-success flow with a modal would be worse. Nothing is lost: the bill is already marked printed and Print on the Orders page still offers a real print.
- **NOT covered:** the Pending-KOTs-view "Print" button (`handleFirePendingKot`) still fires KOTs straight to the kitchen printer. That button's entire purpose is to send the ticket to the kitchen, and "marking" it without printing would mean the kitchen never gets it — a different semantic. Flagged rather than silently changed.
- **Verified:** `tsc` clean; `eslint` shows the **identical 11 pre-existing problems before and after** (no new issues introduced); `yarn build` clean; `qz_print_update` exercised on dev with state restored afterwards. **Frontend-only → redeploy the `pos/` build. No migrate, no restart.**

### 2026-07-29 — POS Invoice never picked up the POS Profile's cost center (header blank → wrong GL cost center)
- **Symptom (user):** "when creating a pos invoice it looks like it does not pick the cost center thats in the pos profile that the user is selling from".
- **Precise diagnosis — it's the HEADER, not the item rows.** URY already stamps `cost_center` on every item row ([ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) `sync_order`'s `invoice.append("items", ...)`), so per-item reporting looks correct. But `POS Invoice.cost_center` (the header) was **NULL on every order**. Confirmed on dev: profile `Airport` had `cost_center = '100 - Main - LR'`, invoice item rows carried it, invoice header was `None`.
- **Why ERPNext doesn't fill it in:** `POSInvoice.set_pos_fields` ([pos_invoice.py:693-710](../erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py#L693)) copies `cost_center`/`write_off_cost_center` from the profile only inside `if not for_validate`. URY builds the doc with `frappe.new_doc("POS Invoice")` then `save()`, and the save path runs `set_missing_values(for_validate=True)` — so that copy is **skipped every single time**. (It does not CLEAR an existing value, which is why setting it ourselves sticks.)
- **Why it matters — this is a real GL bug, not cosmetic.** ERPNext uses the **header** cost center for every posting that isn't an item row: the debtor/receivable entry (`make_customer_gl_entry`, [sales_invoice.py:1688](../erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py#L1688)), the POS cash/bank payment entries (`make_pos_gl_entries`, :1947), write-off (:1993/:2014) and rounding adjustment (:2066). With the header blank those legs silently fall back to the **company default** cost center.
- **It is invisible on a site whose company default equals the profile's cost center** — which is exactly the dev site's config (`100 - Main - LR` for both). It only bites in the multi-outlet setup cost centers exist for. Proved it by pointing the profile at `200 - Airport - LR` and diffing the generated GL: the **Debtors 1310 legs moved from `100 - Main - LR` → `200 - Airport - LR`**; income/tax lines correctly unchanged (those come from item/tax rows).
- **Fix:** new `_apply_pos_profile_cost_center(invoice, pos_profile_name)` in [ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py), modelled on the existing `_apply_pos_profile_taxes` (profile is the source of truth; blank profile fields are left alone rather than clearing an existing value). Sets `cost_center` **and** `write_off_cost_center`. Called from **three** places: `sync_order` (creation), `make_invoice` (payment — so drafts rung before this shipped get corrected when settled), and `split_invoice_by_item` (split bills are brand-new invoices that mirrored `pos_profile`/`taxes_and_charges`/`custom_terminal` from the source but **not** the cost center, so splitting an order silently moved its receivable legs back to the company default).
- **Verified on dev (`local.16.1`):** with the profile pointed at `200 - Airport - LR` (≠ company default) a real `sync_order` run produced header `cost_center = '200 - Airport - LR'` and item row `'200 - Airport - LR'`; the value **persists through a real `save()`** (the actual risk — ERPNext's validate path could have overwritten it, it doesn't); and the GL diff above confirms the debtor legs follow it. `py_compile` clean. Test invoice deleted and the profile + `M-0040` restored to their original values.
- **Deploy: `bench restart` only.** No schema change, no migrate, no frontend rebuild — `cost_center` is a standard ERPNext field that already exists.
- **Note:** historical invoices keep whatever they had (header NULL); the fix is forward-going. A backfill would mean re-posting GL on consolidated invoices — deliberately not attempted.
- **Config reminder for the user:** the fix propagates whatever is on the POS Profile. On the dev site the `Airport` profile is set to `100 - Main - LR` even though a `200 - Airport - LR` cost center exists — if each outlet should report separately, set each POS Profile's Cost Center accordingly, otherwise everything still lands on one.
### 2026-07-28 — THE POS open/close deadlock: opening entries are per-POS-PROFILE, not per-terminal or per-user
- **Symptom (user, multi-cashier ticked):** captain opens the day → on a second browser he's asked to open again → waiters/cashiers logging in are also asked to open → they hit **Join Session** and are told an opening already exists that they must close → they try to close it and get **"no sub opening"**. Unescapable loop; the POS is unusable.
- **ROOT CAUSE — three layers disagreed about what an opening entry is scoped to.** ERPNext core is the authority and URY was fighting it:
  | Layer | Scopes an open entry by | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | ERPNext `check_open_pos_exists` ([pos_opening_entry.py:64](../erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_opening_entry/pos_opening_entry.py#L64)) | `pos_profile` **only** | one open entry per profile, full stop |
  | ERPNext `check_user_already_assigned` (:73) | `user` **only** | one user may hold only ONE open entry anywhere |
  | URY `posOpening` (2026-04-08 revamp) | `custom_terminal` | wanted one per terminal |
  | URY multi-cashier | `custom_terminal` + `user` | wanted one per person |
  The dev site has **5 terminals on 1 POS Profile** — so URY kept asking for opens ERPNext will *always* refuse. **Reproduced empirically:** `posOpening('Bar')` returned `1` (needs open) even with multi_cashier **OFF**, and inserting that entry threw `"Airport is open. Close the POS or cancel the existing POS Opening Entry…"` — the exact user-visible message. Strict per-user mode is *unimplementable*: blocked twice over, and `override_doctype_class` is commented out at hooks.py:142, so nothing overrides core.
- **Why the escape hatch was also broken:** `calculate_closing_amount` ([ury_pos_closing_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py)) hard-threw `"No Sub POS Closing entries found between the given dates"` whenever multi_cashier was on — demanding a record **nothing can create** (no Sub POS Closing screen exists in `pos/`, and the doctype is in no URY role's permissions). Reproduced by calling the hook directly. That turned a recoverable state into a lockout.
- **THE MODEL (fixed):** **one open POS Opening Entry per POS Profile per shift.** Whoever arrives first opens the day; everyone else on any terminal of that profile just walks in. This is correct under BOTH deployment shapes — when each terminal has its own profile, profile-scoping *is* terminal-scoping. Attribution is not lost: it rides on `POS Invoice.owner` + `custom_terminal` + `cashier`. **`custom_enable_multiple_cashier` no longer affects the open/close gate at all** — no flag can make ERPNext accept a second open entry on one profile.
- **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):** `posOpening` scopes by the terminal's `pos_profile` (dropped the `user` filter AND the terminal-only filter; unconfigured terminal falls back to branch). `get_pos_open_entry` same scope, and now returns **`is_mine` / `opened_by` / `same_terminal`** so the UI can stop inviting people to close a colleague's shift. `validate_pos_close` dropped its user+terminal narrowing — that narrowing hid a genuinely unclosed previous day from everyone except the opener.
- **SILENT ORPHAN BUG fixed in the same pass — `_scope_invoices_for_opening_entry`.** It filtered by `pi.owner` (multi-cashier) **and** `custom_terminal`. With one shared entry across 5 terminals that **excluded every invoice rung by anyone else or on any other till** — they'd attach to no closing entry, never consolidate, and break the *next* close. Both filters removed; scope is now `pos_profile` + unconsolidated + `creation >= opening.creation`. ERPNext's "every invoice must be owned by the closing user" rule is satisfied downstream by the existing ownership-normalisation pass in `submit_pos_closing_entry` — the right place for it (keeps SCOPE honest, fixes ownership once at the boundary). **Proven:** re-stamped an invoice to a different owner+terminal → still in close scope (previously orphaned).
- **Closing hooks gutted ([ury_pos_closing_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py) → no-ops).** Deleted `calculate_closing_amount` (the blocker; also read the never-populated `custom_closing_amount`, so it **silently overwrote real counted cash with 0**, only ever read `sub_pos_closing[0]`, and its `posting_date <=` + `period_start_date >=` filters described an unbounded window across other profiles/companies) and `validate_cashier` (keyed off deprecated `custom_main_cashier`; assigned inside the loop instead of collecting a set, so only the **last** non-main user in `applicable_for_users` was ever blocked — whether you were stopped depended on row order). File carries a long "do not re-add" note.
- **`hooks.py` — the POS Opening Entry `validate` hook was MISWIRED and two gates had never once run.** It named `ury_pos_opening_entry.set_cashier_room` directly instead of the module's `validate()` orchestrator, so **`validate_terminal_branch` and `enforce_shift_gate` were dead for their entire life** — the shift gate added 2026-04-14 never fired on POS Opening Entry creation. Repointed at `.validate`. Confirmed live: the branch-mismatch guard now fires. **Needs `bench restart`** (doc_events are not picked up by clear-cache).
- **`set_current_time`** re-stamped `period_start_date = now()` on EVERY save when multi_cashier was on — submit is a second save, and any later desk edit moved the shift start forward again, silently truncating the shift for the Shift Hours banner and the close window. Now guarded to insert-only.
- **`sub_pos_closing.py`** wrote `custom_sub_pos_close`; the real field is **`custom_sub_pos_close_entry`** — so the link back to the opening entry was a stray Python attribute that never persisted. Fixed, and `on_cancel` now **clears** it instead of re-stamping `self.name` (copy-paste of `on_submit`).
- **Frontend ([POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx)):** deleted the `sub-cashier-ready` / `sub-cashier-waiting` branches, `mainCashierUser`, and `hasMainCashierOpened` ([pos-opening-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-opening-api.ts)). **"Join Session" called `handleOpen` — it tried to CREATE a second opening entry, i.e. the exact operation ERPNext always refuses. It never joined anything and could only ever produce the error that pushed cashiers into closing the captain's day.** The error handler now also catches **`Cannot Assign Cashier`** (`check_user_already_assigned`, which is what fires on a second browser and previously dead-ended in a raw error). The existing-entry card is now ownership-aware: **Close My Shift** only when `is_mine`, otherwise a read-only "X already opened this POS — just reload" with an explanation that closing it would settle their takings under your count.
- **Schema:** gave `custom_enable_multiple_cashier` a real description (it had **none** — which is why the box was ticked expecting shared behaviour) in BOTH [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) and [setup.py](ury/setup.py). Added to setup.py *specifically* so the description reaches pre-existing installs (fixtures are install-only); the entry deliberately omits `default` so `create_custom_fields` can't clobber an admin's value.
- **Verified on dev (`local.16.1`, 5 terminals / 1 profile):** all 4 symptoms reproduced BEFORE, all fixed AFTER. `bench migrate` clean; description confirmed live with `default` un-clobbered. Post-fix matrix — nobody re-prompted on any terminal for any user with the flag both ON and OFF; closing hook passes and **preserves the counted 250/99** (previously zeroed); unclosed previous day now visible to a non-opener; ownership metadata present; cross-owner/cross-terminal invoice stays in close scope; **regression: with no open entry `posOpening` still returns 1**. `py_compile` + `tsc` (clean bar the pre-existing privateKey.ts placeholder) + `yarn build` clean. Dev site state restored.
- **Deploy: `bench migrate` (field description) + `bench restart` (REQUIRED — hooks.py doc_events changed) + redeploy the `pos/` build.**
- **Do NOT re-add:** per-user or per-terminal opening-entry scoping; a "Join Session" button; `calculate_closing_amount` / `validate_cashier`; an `owner` or `custom_terminal` filter in `_scope_invoices_for_opening_entry`. Each one re-creates a specific limb of this deadlock.
- **Left alone deliberately (not in scope):** the `Sub POS Closing` doctype itself and `custom_main_cashier` / `custom_rooms` fields remain in the schema, now fully unreferenced by the open/close path — retiring them is a separate cleanup with migration risk for sites holding historic Sub POS Closing records. `multiple_cashier` is still read by [print.ts](pos/src/lib/print.ts) for bill-print routing, which is a legitimate unrelated use.

### 2026-07-23 — KDS: logout button + Served-tab sold summary (per-item qty, per-waiter, printable)
- **Goal (user):** on the kitchen display (URYMosaic KDS) — (1) a **logout** button on the production-unit screen, and (2) on the **Served** tab, a "sold" summary: items sold so far with quantities and the waiter who ordered them, per-item totals (e.g. "20 Coke", "10 Jollof Rice"), the grand total quantity, and a way to **print** it for end-of-day accounting. Mapped the KDS first with a 3-agent workflow (served frontend / auth+navbar / backend KOT schema).
- **Backend — new `get_served_summary(production, date)`** in [ury_kot_display.py](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py). Aggregates SERVED KOT items for a production/department on a day. Scope mirrors `served_kot_list` (branch + `order_status='Served'` + `docstatus=1` + `verified=0`) but over the **full day** (`DATE(served_at)=%(date)s`), not the 3-hour board window. Counts **type New Order + Order Modified** only (the items that went out) — excludes Duplicate reprints + Cancelled tickets so nothing double/over-counts. Returns `{items:[{item_name,total_qty}], by_waiter:[{waiter,total_qty,items:[]}], total_qty, distinct_items, ticket_count, date, production, kds_mode, branch}`.
  - **Two routing-mode paths (the crux):** read `POS Profile.custom_kds_routing_mode` (default "Menu Course"). In **URY Production Unit** mode the `production` arg is a real unit → filter `k.production=%(production)s` (skip for "All"). In **Menu Course** mode `k.production` is NULL → filter per-item by department via `LEFT JOIN URY Menu Course mc ON mc.name=ki.course` + `COALESCE(NULLIF(mc.custom_department,''),'Food')=%(dept)s` (default "Food" matches `_classify_kot_item_department`). All values are parameterised; only the hardcoded clause strings are f-string-interpolated (no injection).
  - **Gotchas handled:** `URY KOT Items.quantity` is a **Data/string**, not `qty` → `SUM(CAST(ki.quantity AS DECIMAL(18,2)))`. Waiter via `k.invoice → tabPOS Invoice.custom_waiter → tabURY Waiter.full_name` (LEFT JOINs; `COALESCE(...,'Unassigned')`). `served_at` (Datetime, set by `serve_kot`, cleared by `reinstate_kot`) is reliable for the day filter.
- **Frontend — logout ([Header.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/Header.vue) + [kot.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/kot.vue)).** URYMosaic's router/Login.vue are **dead** (App.vue renders `<KOT/>` directly; real auth is the Frappe session cookie checked in kot.vue's `auth()` via `frappe.auth().getLoggedInUser()`). Header gains a red Logout button (wrapped with Refresh in one right-aligned group so the centered Active/Served toggle stays put) that `$emit('logout')`; `emits: ["set-view","logout"]`. kot.vue wires `@logout="logout"` and adds `logout()` = `await frappe.auth().logout()` then the existing `redirectToLogin()` (→ Frappe `/login?redirect-to=Mosaic/<production>`).
- **Frontend — Served summary panel (kot.vue).** Mounted **inside the existing `v-if="viewMode==='served'"` block** (kept within the single root; given no `.grid` class so `masonryLoading()`'s `querySelector('.grid')` never binds it). A date picker (defaults to today, max today), Print button, totals row (total qty / distinct items / ticket count), the per-item list, and a collapsible per-waiter breakdown. `showServed()` fires `fetchServedSummary()` (calls the new endpoint with `production=this.production || "All"` + `summaryDate`); the date input re-fetches on change. The existing 3-hour served list stays below under a "Recently served" heading (still used for Reinstate).
- **Print:** URYMosaic has **no print/QZ infra** — used a self-contained **print window** (`window.open` → write a styled HTML report with an embedded `window.onload → window.print()` + auto-close), the same pattern as the React POS Reports "PDF" button. HTML-escapes all values. Works to a real printer or Save-as-PDF via the browser dialog.
- **Verified:** `py_compile` clean; **`get_served_summary` exercised on dev** — both routing-mode SQL paths run without error, and a seeded served KOT (20 Coke + 10 Jollof) aggregated to `coke=20, jollof=10, distinct=2, total=30`, waiter "Unassigned", then cleaned up. `URYMosaic yarn build` clean (built to `ury/public/URYMosaic` + `ury/www/Mosaic.html`). Live browser test is the user's (logout works; Served tab shows the day's per-item + per-waiter totals; Print opens the report; date picker views past days).
- **Follow-up (same day): served-page sidebar + configurable reinstate window.**
  - **Sidebar toggle** ([kot.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/kot.vue)): the Served view is now a `flex` sidebar + main. The sidebar toggles **Recently Served** (the reinstate list — first + default via `servedTab='recent'`) vs **Items Served** (the sold summary). Each panel is `v-if`-gated by `servedTab`; kept inside the single root with **no `.grid`** class (masonry). `showServed()` resets `servedTab='recent'` on entry.
  - **Reinstate config on the production unit** ([ury_production_unit.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_production_unit/ury_production_unit.json), URY-owned → native fields, bumped `modified`): new **`enable_reinstate`** (Check, default 1) + **`reinstate_window_hours`** (Int, default 0 → falls back to 3, `depends_on enable_reinstate`), both with descriptions. The 3-hour window was hardcoded in `served_kot_list`; now [ury_kot_display.py](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py) `served_kot_list(production=None)` reads the config for a real URY Production Unit (a department in Menu Course mode / "All" / missing unit falls back to enabled + 3h), uses the configured window, and returns `reinstate_enabled` + `reinstate_window_hours`. When disabled it returns an empty list + `reinstate_enabled:0`; the KDS then hides the "Recently Served" tab and lands on Items Served. The empty-state copy is now "…in the last N hours" (N from the config).
  - **Verified on dev:** `bench migrate` created both columns; `served_kot_list` returns `window=3` (default / 0→3), `window=5` (custom), and `reinstate_enabled=0` + empty list when disabled; "All" falls back to enabled/3h. Production unit restored after the test. `py_compile` + `json.tool` + `URYMosaic yarn build` clean.
  - **Deploy (updated for this feature set): `bench migrate` (2 new URY Production Unit fields) + `bench restart` (whitelisted `served_kot_list`/`get_served_summary`) + redeploy the `URYMosaic/` build.**
- **Follow-up (leak fix): `served_kot_list` was branch-wide → items served in the kitchen showed on the bar screen's Reinstate list.** The sidebar round made `served_kot_list(production)` read the reinstate CONFIG from the unit but the QUERY was still branch-only (leak). Now it scopes the query by production too, mirroring `get_served_summary`: **URY Production Unit mode** → `AND production = %(kds_production)s`; **Menu Course mode** → `AND EXISTS(... URY KOT Items ki JOIN URY Menu Course mc ... COALESCE(NULLIF(mc.custom_department,''),'Food') = %(kds_dept)s)`. `"All"`/no-production stays branch-wide. Frontend already passes `this.production` (from the sidebar round) — **backend-only, deploy = `bench restart`** (no migrate, no URYMosaic rebuild). Verified on dev: a served KOT seeded on `CXCKitchen` shows on its own unit's reinstate list AND summary, is **absent** from another unit's list AND summary, and still shows under "All"; test KOT cleaned up.
- **Follow-up (realtime fix): kitchen card didn't update when the waiter replied to a change request — the kitchen had to reload.** `respond_kot_change` published `kot_change_resolved` (global → Frappe scopes an untargeted publish to the *waiter's* own room, not the kitchen's) and `kot_update_<branch>_All` (only "All" screens — and it sent just the KOT *name* to the new-KOT socket handler, which expects a KOT *doc*, so it mis-rendered / errored). A per-production kitchen screen (subscribed to `kot_update_<branch>_<production>`) got **nothing**. Fix: `respond_kot_change` now publishes **`kot_change_resolved_<branch>_<target>`** where target is the production unit (URY Production Unit mode) or each of the KOT's item departments (Menu Course mode, via new `_kot_kds_targets`), plus an `_All` channel; the buggy `kot_update_<branch>_All` ping was removed (global `kot_change_resolved` kept for back-compat). [kot.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/kot.vue) now builds `change_channel = kot_change_resolved_<branch>_<production>` alongside `kot_channel` in `fetchKOT`, and `mounted` subscribes to it → re-fetches the board (`fetchKOT` + `masonryLoading`) so the card shows the waiter's response + Accept button with no reload. **Deploy: `bench restart` + redeploy the `URYMosaic/` build** (no migrate). Verified on dev: `respond_kot_change` on a `CXCKitchen` KOT emits `kot_change_resolved_CXC Branch_All` + `kot_change_resolved_CXC Branch_CXCKitchen` (the exact channel the screen subscribes to) + set status Confirmed; test KOT cleaned up. (Live socket delivery is the user's browser test — the channel names match the already-working `kot_update` pattern, incl. branch names with spaces.)

### 2026-07-21 — Offline PWA Phase B: offline order outbox (queue offline, auto-send on reconnect, idempotent)
- **Goal (user, after Phase A shipped + verified live):** a waiter builds an order with no internet → it's held locally and **auto-fires to the kitchen on reconnect**. Agreed defaults: all order types; auto-queue on offline submit; clear cart on queue; keep failed orders for manual retry; a *pending* order is NOT in the kitchen until it syncs.
- **THE correctness core — idempotency.** Replaying a queued order on a flaky reconnect must not create a **duplicate**. `sync_order` → `get_order_invoice` did `frappe.new_doc("POS Invoice")` on every call for non-table orders (Take Away / Aggregator have no dedup; only table orders were incidentally deduped by the `restaurant_table, docstatus=0` lookup). Fix: a new **`POS Invoice.custom_idempotency_key`** (Data, hidden, `no_copy`, `search_index`; all 3 sources — [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py)). `sync_order` gained an `idempotency_key` param and, as its **first statement** (before any invoice resolution/validation), looks the key up — if an invoice already carries it, returns that invoice's `as_dict()` and does nothing else (no re-create, no re-fire KOT). On a new invoice it stamps the key. Two pure helpers `_find_invoice_by_idempotency_key` + `_should_stamp_idempotency` (never overwrite an existing key) in [ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py), unit-tested.
- **The key is sent on EVERY submit (online + queued), not just queued ones.** A client-generated UUID is generated per submit in [OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) and put in the payload for the ONLINE attempt too. Reason: if an online submit drops mid-flight (maybe it reached the server, maybe not), the order is then queued with the SAME key — so the drain either finds the already-created invoice or creates it, exactly once. Without the key on the online attempt, a server-created-then-queued order would double-create.
- **Frontend outbox** — new [pos/src/lib/outbox.ts](pos/src/lib/outbox.ts): a **localStorage** queue (`ury_order_outbox`; small queue, survives app close; Android-only so no iOS eviction concern — no IndexedDB needed) exposed via a zustand store. `enqueue` / `drain` (sequential; only auto-sends `pending`; a network failure mid-drain → back to `pending` for the next reconnect; a real server rejection → `failed` with the message, kept for manual retry) / `retry` / `discard`. `initOutbox()` (wired in [main.tsx](pos/src/main.tsx) after `initConnectivityWatch`) subscribes to the connectivity store and drains ~800ms after online returns (+ once on load if there's a backlog).
- **OrderPanel submit rewiring:** after the order is validated + built, if offline → `enqueue` (NEW orders only; updates to an existing draft need the live doc, so they show "editing needs a connection") + clear cart + "Order queued" toast. Online → `syncOrder`; a mid-flight network drop (SDK's `isOffline` flag from the Phase A hardening, or a response-less error) → `enqueue` the same payload (idempotent) instead of losing it; a real server error re-throws to the existing Price-Not-Set/generic handler.
- **UI** — new [pos/src/components/OutboxIndicator.tsx](pos/src/components/OutboxIndicator.tsx), a compact banner in [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx)'s flex column (next to OfflineBanner): shows "N waiting to send" (amber) / "Sending…" (blue) / "N failed" (red), expandable to a per-order list with **Retry**/**Discard** on failed ones + a "Try to send all now". Hidden when the queue is empty.
- **Verified:** `bench migrate` created the column; **8/8 unit tests pass** (`bench --site local.16.5 execute ury.ury.doctype.ury_order.test_ury_order.run_idempotency_tests`); a **live dedup check** confirmed the short-circuit — with a key stamped on `RS-2026-00024`, `sync_order(idempotency_key=key)` returned that same invoice (no new one) and a bogus key returned None (a normal order isn't wrongly deduped). `tsc -b` clean (except the pre-existing privateKey.ts placeholder); `yarn build` clean. **Deploy: `bench migrate` (new field) + `bench restart` (whitelisted `sync_order` signature changed) + redeploy the `pos/` build.**
- **Known limitations (v1, documented):** (1) offline UPDATES to an existing order aren't queued (need the live draft) — only NEW orders. (2) The outbox is per-device localStorage and is NOT cleared on logout (so a queued order isn't lost) — so if waiter A queues offline then waiter B logs in on the same device before reconnect, the drain runs under B's session while the payload's `owner` is A (rare shift-change edge; the order still sends, attributed per the payload). (3) A queued order can legitimately fail on drain (item now out of stock / price changed / stale opening entry) — it's surfaced as "failed" for the waiter to retry/discard, never silently dropped. (4) No Background Sync yet (would let a backgrounded/closed app replay on Android) — the app must be open + online to drain; fine for the waiter flow.
- **Follow-up (3 offline-testing fixes, frontend-only — redeploy the `pos/` build; NO migrate/restart):**
  1. **Default customer from the POS Profile (fixes "customer does not exist").** The POS hardcoded `Cash Customer` as the default in two spots ([pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts) `initializeApp` + `resetOrderState`); on a site whose default isn't literally that record, `sync_order`'s `frappe.get_doc("Customer", ...)` throws and (with Phase B) the queued order lands as **failed**. Now a `defaultCustomer(profile)` helper uses **`posProfile.customer`** (the ERPNext POS Profile `customer` field — already returned by `get_pos_profile_full`, no backend change) and falls back to `Cash Customer` only when the profile has none.
  2. **Waiter list cached for offline.** `get_waiters` is a `ury.*` GET but the picker only opens when ringing an order, so it's usually not in the SW cache offline → the cashier couldn't pick a waiter. New `loadWaitersWithCache()` in [waiter-api.ts](pos/src/lib/waiter-api.ts) (durable localStorage `ury_offline_waiters`, same pattern as menu categories); [WaiterSelectDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/WaiterSelectDialog.tsx) loads the full list once and **filters client-side** (so search works offline too); `initializeApp` pre-warms the cache at boot when `custom_use_waiter` is on.
  3. **Failed queued order → "Fix" it in the cart.** [OutboxIndicator.tsx](pos/src/components/OutboxIndicator.tsx) failed rows get a **Fix** button (alongside Retry/Discard) that calls a new store action `loadFromOutboxPayload(payload)` — rehydrates the cart (items, order type, table, customer, waiter, comment) from the queued payload, discards the outbox entry, and navigates to the POS so the waiter can correct what was wrong (e.g. pick a valid customer) and re-send as a fresh order.
  - **Verified:** `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. Live browser re-test is the user's (queue offline with a waiter → picker works offline; a failed order's Fix loads it into the cart; default customer no longer errors).

### 2026-07-21 — Offline PWA Phase A: cache the app so it loads with no internet + offline alert (React POS)
- **Goal (user):** waiters run the React POS (`pos/`) as an installed PWA on Android tablets; the venue's internet is flaky, so a reload/cold-open showed a blank screen (real downtime). Asked to (1) cache the whole app so it loads offline, (2) place orders offline, (3) alert the waiter when offline. Researched with 3 parallel Explore agents (PWA/serving map, order-submit path, web feasibility). Verdict: cache-to-load + offline alert = achievable; **an order physically reaching the kitchen offline is impossible** (`sync_order` does server-only naming/pricing/tax/KOT/stock) — it can only be queued and sent on reconnect. Via AskUserQuestion the user chose **Phase A only** (cache + alert, no backend changes) and **Android tablets only**. Phase B (offline order outbox + idempotent backend endpoint) is the documented next step, not built.
- **THE big constraint — service-worker scope.** There was **no service worker anywhere** before this (that's why offline reload died). The app HTML is at `/pos` but assets at `/assets/ury/pos/`; the only common prefix is `/`, so the SW must claim **root scope**. A SW served from `/assets/ury/pos/sw.js` would only control `/assets/ury/pos/` and never intercept the `/pos` navigation.
- **Serving the SW at root — the Frappe trap.** Serving from `/sw.js` needs the file at the origin root. **Frappe's `StaticPage` renderer REFUSES `.js` files** (`"js"` is in `UNSUPPORTED_STATIC_PAGE_TYPES`, [frappe/website/page_renderers/static_page.py](../frappe/frappe/website/page_renderers/static_page.py)), so a plain `www/sw.js` is NOT served. BUT the `TemplatePage` renderer serves any file ending in **`min.js` as RAW static source** (no Jinja) — [template_page.py](../frappe/frappe/website/page_renderers/template_page.py) `render_template()` `if self.template_path.endswith("min.js"): html = self.source`. And `build_response`→`set_content_type` derives the mimetype from the path (`mimetypes.guess_type("sw-min.js")` → `text/javascript`, a valid SW MIME). So the built SW is copied to **`ury/www/sw-min.js`** and served at **`/sw-min.js`** (root scope `/` covers `/pos`, no `Service-Worker-Allowed` header / nginx edit needed). `/pos/sw.js` is NOT usable — the `website_route_rules` `/pos/<path:app_path>` wildcard returns the POS shell HTML instead of the SW.
- **Stale-SW caching killed with a colocated pymodule.** Frappe's `@cache_html` caches rendered www pages in redis for 30 min → a redeployed SW would be served stale. Added **[ury/www/sw_min.py](ury/www/sw_min.py)** with `no_cache = 1` (colocated pymodule for `sw-min.js`; the `-`→`_` name mapping makes `ury.www.sw_min` a valid module — a `sw.min.js` name would break the module import because of the extra dot). SW is also registered with `updateViaCache: 'none'` so the browser bypasses HTTP cache on update checks.
- **Precache URLs must be ABSOLUTE.** vite-plugin-pwa (injectManifest) emitted RELATIVE precache URLs (`assets/index-*.js`), which workbox resolves against the SW's location — from `/sw-min.js` that wrongly gives `/assets/index-*.js` (404 → precache install fails). Fixed with a `manifestTransforms` in [pos/vite.config.ts](pos/vite.config.ts) that prefixes every URL with `/assets/ury/pos/` so they resolve correctly no matter where the SW is served.
- **The SW is root-scoped, so it must NOT touch the Desk.** Its routes only handle: `/pos*` navigations (NetworkFirst via a custom handler that also caches under a normalized `/pos` key so any sub-route reload has a fallback), `/assets/ury/pos/*` (NetworkFirst), and **only the POS's own API GETs** — `/api/method/ury.*` plus `/api/method/frappe.auth.get_logged_user` (NetworkFirst with `networkTimeoutSeconds` so weak connections fail over to cache fast). It deliberately does NOT cache general `/api/method/frappe.*` (Desk) calls, `/api/resource/*`, or `/assets/frappe/*`. Everything unmatched falls through to the network untouched. Confirmed the entire boot path (auth → terminal → profile → menu → courses → payment → opening entry) is all `ury.*` + `frappe.auth.get_logged_user`, so the scoped cache covers offline boot fully.
- **CSRF safety.** The `/pos` shell embeds a server-rendered `window.csrf_token`. The SW never PREcaches the shell HTML (a stale token would break POSTs) — it's NetworkFirst at runtime, so online always gets a fresh token and offline is read-only (Frappe GETs don't need CSRF). Confirmed the built SW contains none of `{index}` / `{next}` / `<!-- csrf_token -->` so Frappe's TemplatePage post-processing can't corrupt it.
- **Files.** New: [pos/src/sw.ts](pos/src/sw.ts) (the SW), [pos/src/lib/register-sw.ts](pos/src/lib/register-sw.ts) (registers `/sw-min.js`, prod + secure-context only), [pos/src/lib/connectivity.ts](pos/src/lib/connectivity.ts) (zustand `online` store + `navigator.onLine` events + a heartbeat that probes `/assets/ury/pos/manifest.json?__ping=` — the SW skips `__ping` so it measures TRUE reachability, not a cached 200), [pos/src/components/OfflineBanner.tsx](pos/src/components/OfflineBanner.tsx) (persistent bar + online/offline toasts, mounted in [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx) next to ShiftHoursBanner), [ury/www/sw_min.py](ury/www/sw_min.py). Modified: [pos/vite.config.ts](pos/vite.config.ts) (VitePWA injectManifest + manifestTransforms), [pos/package.json](pos/package.json) (deps + `copy-sw` build step + a `workbox-build` **7.3.0 resolution** because 7.4.x needs Node ≥20 and the bench has Node 18), [pos/src/main.tsx](pos/src/main.tsx) (registerServiceWorker + initConnectivityWatch), [pos/src/App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx) (mount banner + **skip the geofence gate when offline** — `validateGeofence` is a POST that would otherwise block offline boot), [pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) (offline pre-check on submit + hardened catch for a connection that drops mid-submit — frappe-js-sdk throws a bare TypeError with no `_server_messages` because there's no HTTP response; cart is preserved so the waiter can resend), [pos/tsconfig.app.json](pos/tsconfig.app.json) (`exclude` sw.ts — it's a WebWorker context compiled separately, conflicts with the app's DOM lib), [.gitignore](.gitignore) (`ury/www/sw-min.js` is build output like pos.html).
- **Verified (headless):** `yarn build` clean (SW compiles via injectManifest, precache 2 entries, copied to `ury/www/sw-min.js`); `tsc -b --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing `privateKey.ts` placeholder (unchanged by me, not in the Vite bundle); precache URLs confirmed absolute (`/assets/ury/pos/assets/index-*.js`); `mimetypes.guess_type("sw-min.js")` → `text/javascript`; SW route/cache markers present; SW free of TemplatePage-corrupting literals; Frappe serving path traced end-to-end (StaticPage rejects .js → TemplatePage min.js raw branch → text/javascript). **Serving is code-only (no schema, no migrate).** Deploy = redeploy the `pos/` build (regenerates `ury/www/sw-min.js`); `ury/www/sw_min.py` ships in the repo.
- **VERIFIED LIVE (user, HTTPS `landing.erpxpand.com`):** `curl -I /sw-min.js` → `Content-Type: text/javascript; charset=utf-8`, `Content-Length: 26114` (raw JS, not HTML), `X-Page-Name: sw-min.js`, `X-From-Cache: False`, `Cache-Control: no-store,no-cache,must-revalidate` (the `no_cache=1` pymodule → the SW is never HTTP-cached, so redeploys propagate immediately). SW registers; offline reload keeps the app + nav bar working. Pushed to branch `offline-pwa-phase-a`.
- **Follow-up fix (offline data — 2 gaps the user hit on the first offline reload):**
  1. **POS tab "failed to load menu categories".** `getMenuCourses` uses `db.getDocList` → the **`/api/resource/URY Menu Course`** REST path, which the SW's API route does NOT cache (it only caches `/api/method/ury.*`). Worse, [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx) `removeItem('menuCategories')` wipes the sessionStorage cache on every terminal resolve. Fix: a **durable localStorage fallback** in [pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts) `fetchCategories` (`ury_offline_categories`) — save on every successful online fetch, fall back to it when the fetch fails offline. Same durable-fallback added to `fetchMenuItems` (`ury_offline_menu:<profile>:<room>:<orderType>` + a `ury_offline_menu_last` catch-all) as belt-and-braces (menu items are `ury.*`/SW-cached, but this makes the POS bulletproof offline regardless of SW warm-up timing). Chose app-level localStorage over broadening the SW to `/api/resource/*` because that would also cache Desk resource reads (overreach) and is subject to SW warm-up timing; localStorage is Desk-safe and timing-independent, and survives the sessionStorage wipe.
  2. **Reports tab "cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'data')".** This is **frappe-js-sdk's own crash**: its axios error interceptor reads `error.response.data` unconditionally, but a true network failure has no `.response`, so the SDK throws that bare TypeError for ANY uncached `call`/`db` offline. Fix: [frappe-sdk.ts](pos/src/lib/frappe-sdk.ts) now wraps `call` + `db` in a `harden()` Proxy that normalizes offline / response-less TypeErrors into a clean "You're offline — this needs an internet connection" Error (`isOffline` flag) — global, so every feature shows a sensible message instead of the crash. Real server errors (403/500 with a response body) pass through untouched, so `_server_messages` parsing (extractFrappeServerError) still works.
  - **Verified (headless):** `tsc -b --noEmit` clean (only the pre-existing privateKey.ts placeholder), `yarn build` clean. Live offline behavior (categories now render, Reports shows the friendly offline message) is the user's browser re-test.

### 2026-07-16 — Cashier attribution fix (waiter was recorded as cashier) + cashier daily sales + Waiters→Orders open-to-pay
- **Bug: the waiter was being stamped as the `cashier`.** `getPosProfile` returns `cashier = frappe.session.user` and `sync_order` did `invoice.cashier = cashier` unconditionally — so a self-serve waiter placing an order got recorded as the cashier. The `cashier` field is meant to be **who took the sale**, not who placed it. **Fix (3 layers):** (1) `sync_order` only stamps `invoice.cashier` when the ringing user is NOT a self-serve waiter (`if not _self_waiter`); a waiter's order leaves cashier blank until billed. (2) `getPosProfile` returns `cashier = None` for a self-serve waiter (belt-and-braces so the client never even holds it). (3) **`qz_print_update` stamps `cashier = frappe.session.user`** when a real cashier prints/bills — so whoever processes the bill is the cashier of record. (`cashier` is a Data field holding the user id, confirmed on dev.)
- **Cashier daily sales now include waiter orders they billed.** `get_daily_sales` scoped by `pi.owner = %s` only, so a cashier who billed a *waiter-placed* order (owner = the waiter) never saw it. Both queries (invoices + payment_totals) now scope by **`(pi.owner = %s OR pi.cashier = %s)`** — his own rung orders (owner) AND anything he billed (cashier). Still submitted-only + per-user, so admins/captains see just their own (the cross-cashier report is separate).
- **Waiters page → click opens the order on the Orders page ready to pay.** Previously a click on a waiter's order hydrated it into the POS for *editing* and navigated home. A cashier wants to **print + take payment**, so the card click now calls a new orders-slice `openOrderByName(name)` (pre-sets search=name / status=Draft / today, fetches the doc, pre-selects it) and navigates to `/orders` with the right panel already open. The now-dead `handleEditOrder` (POS-hydrate path) was removed; the hover hint changed from "Edit" to "Open & pay". The Waiters page is cashier/captain-only (waiters are bounced by `RequireNotWaiter`), so this is always the cashier flow.
- **Verified on dev:** `cashier` is a `Data` user-id field; printing an invoice as cashier `adeyie21` stamped `cashier='adeyie21@gmail.com'` (was Administrator); `get_daily_sales` for that cashier then **included** the billed invoice via the OR-scope. `py_compile` + `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. **Deploy: `bench restart` (whitelisted changes) + redeploy the `pos/` build. No migrate** — `cashier` already exists.
- **Note / follow-up:** `openOrderByName` pre-searches the invoice id and pre-selects it (right panel opens), but if the Orders page's own mount-fetch races, the *list* highlight may lag a beat — the panel (what's needed to pay) is always correct. Historical invoices keep whatever `cashier` they had; the attribution is correct going forward.

### 2026-07-16 — KDS: draggable cards (persisted), tap-anywhere fix, and a two-way change/cancel/accept loop
- **Cards are draggable to reorder the board, and the order persists.** Native HTML5 DnD on each card (`draggable`, dragstart/over/leave/drop/end); the dragged-over card shows a dashed outline (`.ury-drag-over`). The chosen sequence is saved to `localStorage` per production (`ury_kds_order_<production>`) and re-applied by `applySavedOrder()` in `fetchKOT`, so a reload or the 30s poll doesn't shuffle it back. A NEW order (not in the saved list) sorts to the FRONT so it can't be buried.
- **"Tap for options" (and the whole card) now opens the dialog.** The click handler moved from the inner header `<div>` to the CARD root, so tapping anywhere — including the bottom strip — opens the actions. The card was already single-tap; the earlier version only wired the click to the header row, so the strip did nothing. The per-item strike-through toggle got `@click.stop` so it doesn't also open the dialog.
- **Kitchen→waiter loop is now two-way with three outcomes + a kitchen ACK.** Waiter answers a request with **Confirm**, **Update request**, or **Cancel order** (the old "Reject" is gone). Whatever she picks, the KOT parks in that state and the **kitchen must Accept it** before the card clears — the cook never misses the reply.
  - **Update** ([respond_kot_change](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py) `action="update"`) revises ONLY the item's special instruction — `_update_item_note` writes the new note to BOTH the `URY KOT Items.comments` (what the cook reads) AND the matching `POS Invoice Item.comment` (the record), so they can't drift. It never touches qty/items/price — re-ringing stays the cashier's job, exactly as the user asked ("update the special request, not by changing the whole order or removing the item").
  - **Cancel order** cancels the KITCHEN order only. On the kitchen's Accept, [kitchen_ack_change](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py) sets `order_status = "Cancelled by Waiter"` — which matches neither the active board's `Ready For Prepare` filter nor the served list's `Served`, so the card drops off the board (no schema change; `order_status` is free-text Data). Cancelling the INVOICE itself stays a captain action (`cancel_order`).
  - New `change_response` (Small Text) field carries the waiter's note back; the KDS card shows CONFIRMED / SPECIAL REQUEST UPDATED / ORDER CANCELLED panels and the tap-dialog surfaces an **Accept** (or **Accept & remove** for a cancel) button when a response is pending.
- **Waiter alert gained "Open this order"** ([Notifications.tsx](pos/src/pages/Notifications.tsx)) — pre-fills the Orders search with the invoice id and navigates to `/orders` (via `setOrderSearchQuery`), plus an inline **Update special request** field + optional note-back. (It does NOT deep-select the invoice on the Orders page — that needs the orders slice to accept a target; the search pre-fill lands her on the single row.)
- **Verified end-to-end on dev:** `bench migrate` created `change_response`; UPDATE → status `Updated`, note synced to BOTH the KOT item and the invoice item, kitchen ACK cleared it; CANCEL → status `Cancelled`, kitchen ACK set `order_status='Cancelled by Waiter'` and the KOT was **absent from the active board**. `py_compile` + `json.tool` + `tsc` + both `yarn build`s clean. **Deploy: `bench migrate` + `bench restart` + redeploy BOTH `pos/` and `URYMosaic/` builds.**

### 2026-07-16 (follow-up) — kitchen bell never rang (Check field has no "unset") + compact navbar
- **Bell was silent (user report).** The previous round gated ringing on `shouldRing() { return this.audio_alert !== 0 }`, reasoning that an "unset" `custom_kot_alert` should still ring so a fresh install has audio. **That state doesn't exist:** `custom_kot_alert` is a **Check** field, so it is ALWAYS `0` or `1` — never null. Every site that hadn't explicitly ticked it sat at `0` and got no sound at all, which is exactly what was reported. **Fix:** `shouldRing()` now returns `true` unconditionally — the bell is the out-of-the-box default and the POS Profile's `custom_kot_alert_sound` overrides WHICH sound plays, not whether it plays. **Consequence to know: there is currently no mute switch for the kitchen sound.** If one is wanted, add an explicit `custom_kot_mute` Check (don't re-use `custom_kot_alert` — its 0-default is the whole trap).
- **Compact navbar + toggle moved into it ([Header.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/Header.vue)):** padding `p-4` → `px-3 py-1.5`, logo `ml-20 w-40 h-15` → `w-24 h-auto` (no big left margin), refresh button tightened, and the **Active / Served** switch now lives in the navbar as a segmented pill instead of floating above the board. The board container also went `p-6` → `p-4`.
- **Where the Header renders changed.** It used to be a sibling of `<KOT>` in [App.vue](URYMosaic/src/App.vue); it's now rendered *inside* kot.vue, because the toggle drives kot.vue's `viewMode` and lifting that state up through App just to pass it back down is more plumbing than it's worth. Header takes a `viewMode` prop and emits `set-view`; kot.vue handles it via `onSetView`.
- **⚠️ kot.vue must stay SINGLE-ROOT.** Vue 3 allows multi-root (fragment) components, but `masonryLoading()` does `this.$el.querySelector(".grid")` — and on a fragment component `$el` is a comment placeholder, not an element, so masonry would silently stop working. The Header + board are therefore wrapped in one plain root `<div>`. Don't "tidy" that wrapper away.
- **Verified:** URYMosaic `yarn build` clean. **Deploy: redeploy the `URYMosaic/` build only** — no backend, no migrate.

### 2026-07-16 — KDS UX overhaul: tap-for-actions cards, serve animation, served list + reinstate, waiter badge, default bell
- **Cards are no longer a wall of buttons (user request).** The per-card **Serve** and **Request change** buttons are gone; tapping anywhere on a card opens an **action dialog** (Serve/Confirm · Request change · Cancel). The old click-to-flip overlay is removed entirely and its now-dead `rotateCard()` method deleted. The card body no longer repeats the order number (it's on the badge) — only the table, aggregator info and cancel-type remain.
- **Waiter badge** added beside the order-number badge (blue pill, truncated). Backend enriches both `kot_list` and `served_kot_list` payloads with `waiter_name` via a new `_kot_waiter_name(invoice)` helper (POS Invoice `custom_waiter` → URY Waiter `full_name`).
- **Serve now has visible feedback.** Previously the card just vanished. It now pulses green and collapses (`.ury-served-flash`, 620ms) before being removed. **And the board actually animates:** `masonryLoading()` used to construct a *brand new* Masonry on every call, which snaps every card into place — it now re-uses one instance (`reloadItems()` + `layout()`) with `transitionDuration: '0.4s'`, so the remaining cards visibly slide up into the gap.
- **Served list + reinstate (undo a mistaken serve).** New **Active orders / Served** toggle. New `served_at` (Datetime) field on URY KOT stamped by `serve_kot` — the legacy `start_time_serv` holds a *locale time string* ("3:45:12 PM") and can't be sorted. New whitelisted **`reinstate_kot(name)`** puts the KOT back to `Ready For Prepare`, clears `served_at`/`start_time_serv`/`production_time`, and **withdraws the waiter's "food ready" alert** by clearing the invoice's `custom_order_status` (an invoice can hold several KOTs, so any un-serve clears the invoice-level flag). Refuses to run twice.
- **BUG FOUND while testing `served_kot_list`:** it filtered AND sorted on `creation >= 3h ago`, so an order rung earlier in the shift but served just now **never appeared** — useless for the undo use case. Rewritten as raw SQL windowing and ordering on `COALESCE(served_at, creation)` DESC, so it's "last served on top" and pre-`served_at` rows still fall back to creation. Also stripped the leftover `print()` debug noise.
- **Default bell.** Bundled `URYMosaic/public/sounds/kitchen-bell.wav` (generated: struck fundamental + inharmonic partials, two overlapping strikes, ~1.9s) → served at `/assets/ury/URYMosaic/sounds/kitchen-bell.wav`. `playAlertSound(path)` uses the POS Profile's configured sound when set and falls back to the bundled bell otherwise, wrapped in try/catch with the existing "click anywhere to enable" hint on autoplay block. New `shouldRing()` treats `custom_kot_alert` as a MUTE switch: explicit `0` stays silent, but an **unset** value still rings — so a fresh install has audio out of the box.
- **Verified on dev:** `bench migrate` created `served_at`; serve → `served_at` stamped; served list returns it **top of list** with `waiter_name`; reinstate → back to `Ready For Prepare`, `served_at` cleared, invoice alert withdrawn; a second undo is blocked with `ValidationError`. `py_compile` + `json.tool` + URYMosaic `yarn build` clean; bell confirmed in the build output. **Deploy: `bench migrate` + `bench restart` + redeploy the `URYMosaic/` build** (`pos/` unaffected).

### 2026-07-16 — KDS: protruding order-number badge on each kitchen card
- **Feature (user request):** each KOT card now carries the order number in a **protruding pill** — a dark rounded badge with a white ring, positioned `absolute -top-4 left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2` so it sits half outside the card's top edge and is the first thing a cook sees scanning the board. The card's existing `margin-top: 28px` already left room, and the card has no `overflow-hidden`, so nothing clips it.
- **Shared label helper:** new `orderLabel(kot)` returns `daily_order_number ? kot.order_no : invoice.slice(-4)` — the same rule the inline "Order …" line already used. Both the badge and that line now call it so they can't drift apart.
- **⚠️ Side effect worth knowing:** the badge needs a positioning context, so `relative` was added to the card. The rotate-card overlay (`absolute inset-0 … rounded-2xl z-50`) previously had NO positioned ancestor except the board root, so it stretched across the **whole board** when a card was flipped; it now correctly covers just that card. Its `rounded-2xl` (matching the card radius) shows that was always the intent — so this is a latent bug fixed as a by-product, not a regression. Worth a glance when testing the flip/Serve overlay.
- **Verified:** `yarn build` (URYMosaic) clean. **Deploy: redeploy the `URYMosaic/` build only** — no backend, no migrate, and the `pos/` build is unaffected.

### 2026-07-16 — Item notes made discoverable + prominent, and a kitchen→waiter change-request / on-hold loop
- **Context:** audit confirmed per-item "special instructions" already flowed end-to-end (ProductDialog → `sync_order` → `URY KOT Items.comments` → KDS render), but on the dev DB **zero** orders had ever carried one — because the ONLY way to enter a note was to *double-tap* a menu card. Nobody discovers that.
- **Note discoverability ([OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx)):** every cart line now has a **note button** (MessageSquare) that opens a per-item note dialog, and any existing note renders inline on the line as an amber chip. Deliberately NOT blocked by the cashier item-restriction — a note changes no qty or price. New store action `updateItemComment(uniqueId, comment)` ([pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts)) sets it directly (NOT via `addToOrder`, which merges and bumps quantity). [CommentDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/CommentDialog.tsx) gained `title`/`label`/`placeholder` props so it serves both order- and item-level notes — **and a latent bug was fixed**: it stays mounted (renders null when closed), so `useState(initialComment)` kept stale text between opens; it now re-seeds on every open, which would otherwise have shown item A's note when opening item B.
- **Notes are now loud on the KDS ([kot.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/kot.vue)):** per-item notes render as a red-bordered `NOTE: …` block and the order-level note as an amber `ORDER NOTE: …` bar, instead of small grey text a cook scanning a busy board would miss.
- **NEW — kitchen→waiter change request with on-hold (user spec).** The kitchen often can't cook an order as rung (out of stock, can't honour an instruction). Chosen model (confirmed with the user): the kitchen raises a **TEXT request only** — it never edits quantities or prices, so the invoice is never touched by this flow. **Reject means "cook it as originally ordered"** (nothing is cancelled).
  - **Schema:** 7 new fields on the URY-owned **URY KOT** doctype (no custom fields needed): `change_status` (Select: ``/Awaiting Confirmation/Confirmed/Rejected), `change_request`, `change_item`, `change_requested_by/at`, `change_resolved_by/at` — all `allow_on_submit` since KOTs are submittable. `modified` bumped so it re-syncs.
  - **Backend ([ury_kot_display.py](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py)):** `request_kot_change(kot, message, item)` puts the KOT in **Awaiting Confirmation** and publishes `kot_change_request` to the invoice **owner AND the waiter's linked user** (shared `_kot_notify_users`, same pattern as `serve_kot`). `respond_kot_change(kot, action)` sets Confirmed/Rejected, guards against double-answering ("Already Resolved"), and publishes `kot_change_resolved` so the kitchen card comes off hold. `get_kitchen_change_requests()` returns pending requests scoped to the caller (owner OR `custom_waiter` for a self-serve waiter).
  - **KDS:** a **Request change** button per card opens a modal (optional item picker + free text). While Awaiting Confirmation the card shows a big amber **ON HOLD — AWAITING CONFIRMATION** panel and the **Serve button is disabled** ("On hold"); afterwards it shows CHANGE CONFIRMED (green) or REJECTED — make as originally ordered (red).
  - **POS Alerts ([Notifications.tsx](pos/src/pages/Notifications.tsx)):** pending requests render at the TOP of Kitchen Updates as amber cards with **Confirm** / **Reject**. [Footer.tsx](pos/src/components/Footer.tsx) folds them into the badge count and the alert ring (a held order is treated as MORE urgent than a served one), so the existing every-60s reminder nags until the waiter answers.
- **Verified on dev:** `bench migrate` created the columns (`has_column("URY KOT","change_status") == True`); the full loop ran green — request → `Awaiting Confirmation`; the invoice owner saw it via `get_kitchen_change_requests` (1 pending, item + message); confirm → `Confirmed`, pending → 0; a second response was blocked with `ValidationError`; test data cleaned up. `py_compile` + `json.tool` + `tsc` + **both** `yarn build`s (pos AND URYMosaic) clean. **Deploy: `bench migrate` + `bench restart` + redeploy BOTH the `pos/` and `URYMosaic/` builds** (the KDS changed too — easy to forget).
- **Follow-ups not built:** the waiter can't yet jump straight from the alert into editing that order (she navigates to it herself), and there's no history/audit list of past requests beyond the fields on the KOT.

### 2026-07-16 — Cashier/Captain lost menu access (Custom DocPerm override) + Waiters page hid waiter-placed orders
- **Symptom 1:** users with **ExPOS Cashier** or **ExPOS Captain** got "Failed to load menu categories" and no menu items — while **ExPOS Waiter** worked fine. Backwards from what you'd expect, since the waiter role is the newest and most restricted.
- **Root cause (the big one):** `getMenuCourses()` does a direct `db.getDocList('URY Menu Course')`, so it needs doctype read perm. **In Frappe, the moment ANY `Custom DocPerm` row exists for a doctype, the built-in `DocPerm` rows are ignored wholesale — they are NOT merged.** `URY Menu Course` shipped built-in rows for URY Manager/Cashier/Captain and had NO custom rows, so `_ensure_perm`'s "a built-in already grants this role → skip" shortcut left them alone. Then the new **URY Waiter** role (2026-07-14) got the doctype's *first* Custom DocPerm row — which instantly made Frappe ignore all three built-ins. Proof on dev: `has_permission("URY Menu Course","read")` was **False** for both a cashier and a captain user, with `custom rows = 1` (waiter) vs `builtin rows = 3`.
- **Fix ([permissions.py](ury/permissions.py)):** new **`_materialize_builtin_perms(doctype)`** copies any built-in `DocPerm` row that has no Custom counterpart into `Custom DocPerm` (idempotent; flag list resolved from the DocType meta via `_perm_flag_fields()` because the field set drifts between versions — `set_user_permissions` doesn't exist on v16 and a hardcoded SELECT blew up). `_ensure_perm` now: (a) checks whether the doctype is **already customised** and, if so, ignores the "built-in covers this role" shortcut entirely (built-ins aren't consulted anymore, so the role needs its own row); (b) calls `_materialize_builtin_perms` before it creates/updates any custom row, so taking over a doctype never silently drops a role. Runs on every `after_migrate` via `ensure_role_permissions()`, so existing sites self-repair.
- **Blast radius was much wider than the menu:** the repair pass on dev rescued bypassed built-ins on `POS Settings` (System Manager, Sales User, Accounts User), `URY POS Terminal`, `Company` (HR Manager/User), `Customer Group` + `Territory` (Sales User, Sales Manager, Sales Master Manager, Customer, Accounts User, Stock User…), `Account` (HR User/Manager) and more. Those roles had been silently losing access to those doctypes.
- **Symptom 2:** on the **Waiters** page a captain (and cashier) saw no orders under any waiter — but he's the one who collects the money from waiters and keys in the payment.
- **Root cause / fix:** `get_waiters_with_pending_orders` + `get_waiter_pending_order_count` filtered `pi.owner = frappe.session.user` (added 2026-06-11, when cashiers rang every order *on behalf of* waiters so owner == the viewer). Self-serve waiters (2026-07-14) own their own orders, so that filter hid every waiter-placed order from exactly the people the page exists for. **Dropped the owner filter — both are now branch-scoped only.** Verified on dev: 6 pending waiter drafts (all owned by Administrator) now show for a URY Captain AND a URY Cashier — 3 waiters, 6 orders, badge 6; previously 0.
- **Verified:** `py_compile` clean; `ensure_role_permissions()` re-run on dev flipped cashier + captain `URY Menu Course` read **False → True** and is idempotent on a second pass (`inserted: 0`). (`URY Menu Item` reads False for everyone — it's a child table, so `has_permission` never applies; the POS loads items through the whitelisted `getRestaurantMenu`.) **Backend-only → `bench migrate` (runs the permission repair) + `bench restart`. No pos rebuild.**

### 2026-07-16 — Waiter "My Sales" report (date range + PDF export)
- **Feature (user request):** waiters can now see the sales they've served, pick a date range, and export a PDF — all inside the PWA. New **My Sales** tab in the waiter nav (POS · Tables · My Orders · **My Sales** · Alerts).
- **Backend — new `get_waiter_sales(from_date, to_date, waiter=None)` ([ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):** returns `{waiter, waiter_name, from_date, to_date, summary{order_count,total_sales,average_order}, by_day[], invoices[]}`. Scope = **submitted** POS Invoices whose `custom_waiter` is this waiter (covers orders she rang herself AND orders a cashier rang for her); excludes returns and merged-source invoices. **Deliberately NOT branch-scoped** — a waiter's orders carry the branch of the table/till she rang on, which need not match her own URY User branch (same trap as the 2026-07-15 round-5 fix). Waiter resolution: a self-serve waiter ALWAYS gets her own record (the `waiter` arg is ignored, so she can't read anyone else's numbers); an elevated user (admin / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain) may pass an explicit `waiter`. Non-waiters get a friendly "Not a Waiter" throw. Defaults to today when no dates are given.
- **Frontend — new [WaiterSales.tsx](pos/src/pages/WaiterSales.tsx)** at `/my-sales` (route in [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx), nav entry in [Footer.tsx](pos/src/components/Footer.tsx)): quick presets (Today / Yesterday / Last 7 days / This month) + explicit from/to date inputs, three summary cards (orders, total, average), a per-day breakdown (only when the range spans >1 day), and the order list. `getWaiterSales` client added to [waiter-api.ts](pos/src/lib/waiter-api.ts).
- **PDF export:** the **PDF** button opens a print window containing a styled report (summary + daily breakdown + order table + generated timestamp) with `window.onload → window.print()` and auto-close — the same browser print → **"Save as PDF"** pattern the Reports page already uses. Text is HTML-escaped before injection. No server-side PDF dependency.
- **Route is open to any signed-in user** (not waiter-gated) because the scoping is server-side — a non-waiter simply sees the friendly "Not a Waiter" message. Only the waiter NAV shows the tab.
- **Verified:** `py_compile` + `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. On dev: `get_waiter_sales` for waiter "Afia" returned the right summary/by_day/invoice list off a real submitted invoice, and the no-waiter guard threw `ValidationError` as designed. **Deploy:** `bench restart` (new whitelisted method) + redeploy the `pos/` build (no migrate — no schema change).
- **Follow-ups if wanted:** per-item breakdown in the report, a waiter picker for captains/admins (backend already supports the `waiter` arg), and tips/commission columns.

### 2026-07-16 — Alerts: recurring every-minute reminder until served + "Served" button (was an X)
- **Recurring reminder (user request):** while the user has un-cleared "ready" kitchen notifications, the sound + vibration now **re-ring every 60s** until they're all served. Added to [Footer.tsx](pos/src/components/Footer.tsx) (global — the Footer is always mounted, so the reminder follows the user anywhere in the POS, not just on the Alerts page). A `notifCountRef` mirrors the live count so the 60s `setInterval` isn't a stale closure; it calls `playAlertSound()` + `vibrateAlert()` only when `count > 0`. Complements the existing instant ring on a NEW served order (the 15s poll) — new order rings immediately, then re-rings each minute until cleared. Count is per-user (`get_kitchen_notifications` is scoped to owner OR custom_waiter), so it only nags the person who owns the order.
- **"Served" button replaces the X ([Notifications.tsx](pos/src/pages/Notifications.tsx)):** the kitchen-update card's top-right X close icon is gone; each card now has a green **Served** button (CheckCircle) at the bottom that calls the same `clear_notification` (sets `custom_clear_from_notification=1`). Clearing drops the count → the recurring reminder stops on the next tick. Swipe-to-clear still works as a shortcut; toast copy changed to "Marked as served".
- **Note:** the recurring reminder currently applies to ANYONE with un-cleared ready-order notifications (waiter or cashier), since the Alerts page + Served button are shared. Trivial to scope to `isWaiterOnly` if it's too noisy for cashiers.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. Frontend-only → redeploy the `pos/` build. Browser test: kitchen marks an order ready → rings, and keeps re-ringing each minute; go to Alerts → tap **Served** → it clears and the ringing stops.

### 2026-07-16 — Orders filter sidebar → burger drawer + smaller Table & Order cards
- **Orders "Order Status" sidebar is now a drawer (< lg):** [OrderStatusSidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx) is a static left column on `lg+` but below `lg` becomes a slide-in drawer (`absolute inset-y-0 left-0 w-72`, `-translate-x-full` when closed → clipped by the Orders container's `overflow-hidden`) with a close (X) header. [Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) adds a **burger** (`Menu`) button in the toolbar (`lg:hidden`) that opens it, a backdrop tap-to-close, and closes the drawer when a filter is selected. Same `absolute`-inside-`relative` pattern as the round-earlier right-panel overlay, so it stays within the Orders area (doesn't cover header/footer) and frees the full width for the order cards in portrait.
- **Smaller Table cards** ([Table.tsx](pos/src/pages/Table.tsx)): grid `grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-4 xl:grid-cols-5 gap-4` → `grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-3 md:grid-cols-4 lg:grid-cols-5 xl:grid-cols-6 gap-2 sm:gap-3`; card padding `p-4`/`gap-y-4` → `p-2.5 sm:p-3`/`gap-y-2`, name `text-lg` → `text-sm sm:text-base` (truncate), details `text-sm space-y-2` → `text-xs space-y-1`, tighter action buttons.

- **Verified:** `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. Frontend-only → redeploy the `pos/` build (no migrate/restart). Browser test: Orders page on a tablet → order cards fill the width; tap the burger → filter drawer slides in, pick a filter → it closes; Tables + Orders show more, smaller cards.

### 2026-07-16 — Smaller menu cards (one more per row) + fly-to-cart animation + haptic on add
- **More, smaller cards (user request):** [MenuList.tsx](pos/src/components/MenuList.tsx) grid `grid-cols-3 sm:grid-cols-4 xl:grid-cols-5` → **`grid-cols-4 sm:grid-cols-5 lg:grid-cols-6`** (one more per row at each tier — 4 on phones, 5 on portrait tablets, 6 on landscape/desktop; added the `lg` step so landscape gets more than portrait). [MenuCard.tsx](pos/src/components/MenuCard.tsx) shrunk to fit: height `h-40 sm:h-52 xl:h-56` → `h-36 sm:h-44 xl:h-48`, image `h-16 sm:h-24` → `h-14 sm:h-20`, plus `active:scale-95` for a tactile press.
- **Fly-to-cart "swish" + vibration on add:** new [fly-to-cart.ts](pos/src/lib/fly-to-cart.ts). On a single-tap add ([POS.tsx](pos/src/pages/POS.tsx) `handleItemClick`) a small dot arcs from the tapped card to the cart and the cart bumps, plus a short `navigator.vibrate(25)` haptic. Layout-aware: the cart target is whichever `[data-cart-target]` is actually on-screen — the floating **FAB** in portrait (< lg) or the **side cart panel** in landscape (lg+); the off-screen mobile drawer is excluded via a viewport-bounds check. The cart "bump" only runs for a small target (FAB), never the wide side panel. Vibration is gesture-initiated (the tap), so it works on Android (iOS has no vibration API); the whole thing is wrapped in try/catch + feature checks so it can never block the actual add-to-cart. MenuCard's `onClick` now passes the tapped element (`e.currentTarget`) up through MenuList → POS as the animation source.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + `yarn build` clean. Frontend-only → redeploy the `pos/` build (no migrate/restart). Browser test: portrait tablet shows more/smaller cards, tapping one flies a dot into the FAB + a buzz; landscape shows 6 per row and the dot flies to the side cart.

### 2026-07-16 — Orders page portrait layout (order cards hidden) + optional Transaction ID for non-cash payments
- **Portrait layout bug:** on the Orders page the order cards didn't show in portrait (tablet vertical) but did in landscape. Root cause: the page is a 3-column horizontal flex — sidebar (`w-64`) | cards (`flex-1 min-w-0`) | **right detail panel (`w-96 shrink-0`, ALWAYS rendered)**. The sidebar + panel = 640px of fixed width even with no order selected, so on a ~768px portrait tablet the `min-w-0` cards column collapsed to almost nothing. **Fix ([Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx)):** the right panel is a static third column only on `lg+`; below `lg` it becomes a slide-in **overlay** (`absolute inset-y-0 right-0`, `translate-x-full` when no order selected → clipped by the parent's `overflow-hidden`, so no body scroll), with an `ArrowLeft` back button (`lg:hidden`) to return to the list. Cards now get the full width in portrait. Frontend-only.
- **Optional Transaction ID for non-cash payments (user request):** the Payment dialog now shows an optional **Transaction ID** input whenever the tender includes a non-cash mode (card / mobile money / bank transfer — i.e. any mode ≠ "Cash"). [PaymentDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx): `hasNonCashPayment` memo off `outgoingPayments`; the field renders in the left column and is passed as `transaction_id` to `make_invoice` (only when non-cash). Backend [make_invoice](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) gained a `transaction_id=None` kwarg and stamps `invoice.custom_transaction_id`. New **`POS Invoice.custom_transaction_id`** (Data, `in_standard_filter`) custom field in all three sources ([custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures). Covers the single + value-split flows (both go through `make_invoice`); item-split (separate flow) not wired this round. **Not yet displayed** in the POS UI/receipt — it's captured + queryable in the desk (standard filter); surfacing it on the right panel / print format is an easy follow-up.
- **Verified:** `py_compile` + `json.tool` + `tsc` + `yarn build` clean; `bench migrate` created the field (`has_column` True, Data / standard-filter). **Deploy:** `bench migrate` (new field) + `bench restart` + redeploy the `pos/` build. Browser test: portrait tablet → order cards show, tapping an order slides in the detail overlay with a back button; pay with a non-cash mode → Transaction ID field appears, entered value lands on `POS Invoice.custom_transaction_id`.

### 2026-07-15 (round 5) — THE waiter-My-Orders root cause: branch filter (getBranch) hid her own orders
- **Symptom:** sound + admin "Pick a Waiter" filter both worked, but a waiter STILL saw nothing in her own My Orders. The "simple" case was the broken one.
- **Root cause (confirmed with an end-to-end dev test):** `getPosInvoice`/`searchPosInvoice` filter `pi.branch = getBranch()`. `getBranch()` resolves the caller's branch from **their own URY User row**. A waiter's orders get their `branch` stamp from the **table / till she rang on** (via `get_restaurant_and_menu_name` / the POS Profile), which need NOT equal her own URY User branch — and if she isn't in any Branch's URY User table at all, **`getBranch()` throws `ValidationError` ("Branch Not Linked")**, which the whole list endpoint propagated. Either way her orders vanished. The admin's "Pick a Waiter" filter worked precisely because it runs under the ADMIN's branch (where the orders actually live) — that's why the harder-looking case worked and the simple one didn't.
