- **Workstream 2 — Returns master toggle (point 5).** New POS Profile `custom_enable_returns` (Check, default **0**). `_user_can_return_orders` in [ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) now evaluates this master switch BEFORE the existing captain restriction — when off, NOBODY can return (not even a captain), the backend rejects every `get_return_preview`/`create_pos_return`/`reverse_pos_return`. Frontend `canReturnOrders` ([role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts)) mirrors it, which hides the Return + Undo-Return buttons everywhere. `getPosProfile` returns the flag. Reversible by flipping the toggle — backend endpoints stay intact.
- **Workstream 3 — Captain-only transfer with receiver approval (point 7).**
  - **New doctype [URY Invoice Transfer](ury/ury/doctype/ury_invoice_transfer/) (XFER-{YYYY}-{MM}-{DD}-{####})** — invoice, status (Pending/Approved/Rejected/Cancelled), from_user, to_user, branch, opening_entry, requested_by/at, resolved_by/at, transfer_note, hop_number. Source of truth + audit chain. Plus denormalized `POS Invoice.custom_transfer_status` (Select) for the Orders page.
  - **`submit_pos_closing_entry` reworked:** captain-only. A non-captain closing with unpaid drafts is **hard-blocked** ("Pay or Cancel Required") — no transfer option. A captain instead creates **Pending** transfer records (the invoice owner does NOT change yet — it changes on approval). A draft only BLOCKS the close when it has no active transfer. The blocking decision is the pure **`_excluded_from_blocking(transfer_rows, current_user)`**: Pending excludes it (in flight + idempotency guard against double-transfer on a repeated close); Approved-to-someone-else excludes it (handed off); **Approved-to-ME does NOT exclude it** (I accepted it, it's my draft now). New POS Profile `custom_max_invoice_transfers` (Int, default 2) caps per-invoice hops (counts Approved transfers only; 0 disables transfers entirely).
  - **New endpoints:** `get_incoming_transfers` (Pending to me, branch-scoped, hides non-Draft invoices, returns getPosInvoice-shaped rows + `transfer_meta`), `get_incoming_transfer_count` (sidebar badge), `approve_transfer` (re-homes owner/cashier + clears terminal + stamps Approved), `reject_transfer` (routes the draft to a branch captain via `_get_captain_users_on_branch` — the "captain queue" is just rejected drafts owned by a captain). All assert Pending (else "Already Resolved") + caller is to_user/captain + invoice still Draft.
  - **Frontend:** "Incoming Transfers" Orders sidebar filter ([order-types.ts](pos/src/data/order-types.ts), gated on `custom_max_invoice_transfers > 0`) with a live indigo badge ([OrderStatusSidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx), 15s poll). [orders-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts) calls `get_incoming_transfers` for that filter. [Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) right panel shows Approve / Reject (reject = reason dialog) + an "Incoming transfer from X" banner for pending-incoming rows. [POSClosingDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx) hides the transfer dropdown for non-captains (uses server `can_transfer`) and shows a "pay or cancel" block. [transfer-api.ts](pos/src/lib/transfer-api.ts) new.
- **Workstream 4 — PIN expiry (point 8).** New `URY Biometric Settings.pin_expiry_days` (Int, default 0 = never). `pin_login` in [biometric/api.py](ury/ury/biometric/api.py): after a correct PIN, if past the window (measured from `pin_changed_at` → `pin_set_at` → `creation`; all-null = not expired so legacy users aren't locked out), the session IS created but the response carries `pin_change_required`. Pure `_pin_is_expired(enrollment, settings)` helper. Frontend: [BiometricLogin.tsx](pos/src/pages/BiometricLogin.tsx) PIN tab opens a forced [ResetPinDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ResetPinDialog.tsx) (new `forced` + `presetOldPin` mode — non-dismissible, old PIN preseeded from the just-entered PIN, reuses the authenticated `change_pin`) and redirects to `/pos` on success.
- **Schema plumbing:** every custom field added in all three sources ([custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) `get_custom_fields()` + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures). URY Invoice Transfer added to `WRITE_DOCTYPES` in [permissions.py](ury/permissions.py) and to [en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv). No backfill patch needed (the new fields have no legacy data; the new field gets created by `create_custom_fields` on migrate).
- **Follow-up — missing email account blocked the close.** User reported that closing a shift on a site with no default outgoing Email Account failed with `"Please setup default outgoing Email Account from Tools > Email Account"`. ERPNext's consolidation/notification path calls `frappe.sendmail` during the POS Closing Entry submit; with no outgoing account Frappe raises `OutgoingEmailError` MID-submit, rolling the whole close back. Catching it after `submit()` can't help (the transaction is already rolled back) — the send must be PREVENTED. Fix in [submit_pos_closing_entry](ury/ury_pos/api.py): a new `_has_outgoing_email_account()` helper (checks for an enabled `default_outgoing` Email Account OR site-config SMTP); when there's none, set `frappe.flags.mute_emails = True` around the insert+submit (restored in `finally`). Sites WITH an account keep emailing exactly as before (the mail just queues). The cashier doesn't care about the email — they just need to close. Only handles the synchronous-send case (which is what blocks the close); an enqueued consolidation's background-job email would run after submit returns and never blocks the close anyway.
- **Follow-up — phone number in `order_type` blocked the close (ROOT CAUSE: a misconfigured `fetch_from`).** User hit `"Could not find Reference Name: POS-CLO-2026-00001"` on close, twice. **The REAL root cause:** the `Sales Invoice-order_type` Custom Field was shipped with **`fetch_from = "customer.mobile_number"`** — a copy-paste from the `mobile_number` field directly above it in [setup.py](ury/setup.py)'s `get_custom_fields()`. With that fetch_from, EVERY Sales Invoice save auto-pulls the customer's PHONE NUMBER into `order_type` (a Select), failing validation. ERPNext's POS Closing Entry consolidation creates one Sales Invoice per customer, so a single customer with a phone number rolls the WHOLE shift close back. The POS Invoices were NEVER corrupt — that's why the dev site had zero bad `order_type` rows and the first repair attempt did nothing (the Sales Invoice re-fetches from the customer's mobile regardless of the POS Invoice value). The visible "Could not find Reference Name" is a SECOND error masking the first: `create_merge_logs` catches the order_type error and calls `closing_entry.set_status("Failed")` → `add_comment` → `LinkValidationError` on the already-rolled-back closing entry. **The crucial trap:** `setup.py`'s `create_custom_fields` runs on EVERY migrate and kept re-stamping the bad `fetch_from`, even though `custom_field.json` (fixtures, install-only) had it correctly `null`. **Fix:** (1) [setup.py](ury/setup.py) Sales Invoice `order_type` — removed the bad `fetch_from` (set to `""` explicitly to force `create_custom_fields` to CLEAR it) + removed a stray duplicate `"options": "URY Restaurant"`; (2) new `_fix_sales_invoice_order_type_fetch()` in [install.py](ury/install.py), called from after_install + after_migrate — explicitly clears the Custom Field's `fetch_from` and `frappe.clear_cache(doctype="Sales Invoice")`, because `create_custom_fields` doesn't reliably clear an existing value (it only sets keys present in the spec). **Defense-in-depth (kept, but not the fix):** `_repair_invalid_order_types(paid)` + `clear_document_cache` normalize any genuinely-corrupt POS Invoice `order_type` before consolidation; `_wrap_close_error` + `_root_cause_message` unmask the real error behind the "Could not find Reference Name" cascade (this is what surfaced the true cause for diagnosis); a write-time guard in [sync_order](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) rejects out-of-range `order_type`. **Verified:** ran `_fix_sales_invoice_order_type_fetch` on dev → cleared `customer.mobile_number`; a FULL `bench migrate` keeps `fetch_from=[]` (blank) afterward; idempotent re-run is a no-op. Tests: `RootCauseMessageTests` (3) + `OrderTypeValidityTests` (2) added → `run_invoice_transfer_tests` now 13. **Lesson:** when a Custom Field misbehaves on every migrate despite a correct `custom_field.json`, check `setup.py get_custom_fields()` — it's the dual source that runs on migrate, and a stray `fetch_from` there silently corrupts a field forever. Also: an out-of-place `fetch_from` is the FIRST thing to check when a field keeps getting an unexpected value (here, the customer's phone), not "data corruption".
- **Follow-up — "Valuation Rate Missing" blocked the close (next error after the order_type fix).** Once the `fetch_from` fix let consolidation past the order_type validation, the close hit a NEW real error: `"Valuation Rate for the Item ABC is required ..."` / `"Row #N: Item X has zero rate but 'Allow Zero Valuation Rate' is not enabled."`. **Why:** ERPNext defers a POS Invoice's stock ledger entries to the CONSOLIDATED Sales Invoice created at shift close. URY menu items are stock-tracked (`update_stock=1`) but often have NO valuation rate (a restaurant doesn't carry an inventory cost per cooked item), so the consolidated invoice's `on_submit` → `update_stock_ledger` → `get_valuation_rate` throws and rolls the whole close back (masked again behind "Could not find Reference Name"). **Fix:** new `before_submit` hook on Sales Invoice ([ury_sales_invoice.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_sales_invoice.py) `allow_zero_valuation_on_pos_stock`, wired in [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) `doc_events`) sets `allow_zero_valuation_rate=1` on each item row when the invoice is POS-side (`is_consolidated` or `is_pos`) AND moves stock (`update_stock`). Narrow scope: a NO-OP for items that already have a valuation rate (the flag only matters when the rate is zero), and it never touches manual / purchase / non-stock Sales Invoices. The stock entry posts at zero cost instead of blocking the close. Alternative for sites that DO want COGS: set a valuation rate on the Item master or make the menu items non-stock (`is_stock_item=0`). **CRITICAL deploy note:** `hooks.py` changes only take effect after a full `bench restart` — `bench clear-cache` is NOT enough (the worker has the old hooks module in memory). Verified the hook registers via `frappe.get_hooks("doc_events")["Sales Invoice"]["before_submit"]`.
- **Follow-up — `/pos` login user-picker showed no users (guest search returned empty).** On a site with biometric **disabled**, the `/pos` login page's "Sign in as" search returned nothing, so cashiers/captains/admin couldn't pick themselves to log in. **Root cause:** `search_users_for_login` ([biometric/api.py](ury/ury/biometric/api.py)) early-returned `[]` when the biometric `enabled` flag was off — but the BiometricLogin page is the DEFAULT `/pos` sign-in for guests whether or not fingerprint/PIN is on, and **password login always works**. Also, the search role filter (`URY_LOGIN_ROLES` = Cashier/Captain/Manager) excluded the Administrator (which has no URY role). **Fix:** (1) dropped the `enabled` gate so the picker always works; (2) new `URY_LOGIN_SEARCH_ROLES` (adds System Manager) + an explicit `OR u.name='Administrator'` so admins are searchable; (3) when biometric is OFF, the search reports `has_fingerprint=0`/`has_pin=0` so the page offers **password-only** (pin_login/biometric_login still require the feature enabled, so we don't advertise tabs that would fail). The raw-SQL query already bypassed guest DocType perms (the user's "guests can't read the user list" hunch wasn't it — the enabled-gate was). Verified with biometric still disabled: `query='a'` → 8 users incl. Administrator, all with has_pin=0/has_fingerprint=0. Backend-only, no frontend rebuild (the page already drives tabs off `has_*`). **Note:** to get PIN/fingerprint login, the site must enable URY Biometric Settings → `enabled` (PIN works without fingerprint hardware; only the fingerprint tab needs the reader + agent).
  - **Login picker scroll polish:** the user-results list in [BiometricLogin.tsx](pos/src/pages/BiometricLogin.tsx) `UserPicker` had no height cap, so a long result set (up to 15) grew the centered card past the viewport and scrolled the WHOLE page. Capped it (`max-h-80 overflow-y-auto overscroll-contain`) so the list scrolls internally and a scroll at its edge doesn't chain to the page. Frontend change — `yarn build` in `pos/`.
- **Follow-up — POS Invoice ignored the POS Profile's tax template + tax category.** Orders went out UNTAXED even though the POS Profile had `taxes_and_charges` (VAT on Sales) + `tax_category` configured. **Why:** [get_order_invoice](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) only set `taxes_and_charges` from `URY Restaurant.default_tax_template`, which was blank — it never looked at the POS Profile. Recent invoices had `taxes_and_charges=''` and 0 tax rows. **Fix:** new `_apply_pos_profile_taxes(invoice, pos_profile)` helper reads the POS Profile's `taxes_and_charges` + `tax_category`, sets them on the invoice (profile takes precedence; blank profile fields keep the restaurant default for back-compat), and calls ERPNext's `invoice.append_taxes_from_master()` to populate the tax rows when empty (`set_taxes()` only auto-fills brand-new docs; this also fixes a re-synced older draft). Guarded against double-appending (`not invoice.get("taxes")`). Wired into BOTH `sync_order` (at creation) and `make_invoice` (at payment, so a draft created before the change still gets taxed). Verified on dev: helper turned an empty invoice into `taxes_and_charges='VAT on Sales-2026 - CXC'`, `tax_category='Sales VAT-2026'`, 3 tax rows (NHI 2.5 / GETF 2.5 / VAT 15). Backend-only — `bench restart`, then ring a new order and confirm the tax shows on the bill.
- **Follow-up — "Close Previous Session" dropped the cashier on the Frappe desk instead of the in-POS close.** The "Previous POS Not Closed" dialog (shown when the daily-close rule is on and a previous-day entry is still open) had a `closing` branch in [POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx) that still did `window.open('/app/pos-opening-entry/<name>', '_blank')` — opening the desk document. Its sibling `existing-entry` branch had ALREADY been fixed (2026-04-09) to close in-POS via `POSClosingDialog`, but the `closing` branch was missed. **Fix:** rewrote the `closing` branch to mirror the existing-entry one — a `showClosingDialog` state + "Close Previous Session" opens `<POSClosingDialog openingEntry={unclosedEntry} .../>` in-POS (reload on success). Also hardened [POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx): the two paths that set `type:'closing'` with `unclosedEntry:null` (legacy bare-"Failed" string + the catch branch) now fall back to `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminal)` to resolve the entry name, so the in-POS close always has a target instead of a disabled button. `yarn build` + `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Verification:**
  - `bench --site local.16.5 migrate` — clean (the 5 "failed" fields it lists — arrived_time / total_spend_time / no_of_pax — are PRE-EXISTING legacy fieldtype mismatches, not from this batch; my 4 new fields are among the 80 OK). Schema verified present via mariadb (doctype + 3 custom fields + pin_expiry_days).
  - **54 unit tests pass:** `run_split_invoice_tests` (11 — allocation math), `run_invoice_transfer_tests` (8 — the `_excluded_from_blocking` decision incl. idempotency + the to_user distinction), `run_biometric_tests` (35, +8 new `PinExpiryTests` — date math, fallback chain, boundary). Direct-runner pattern per rule 6.
  - `yarn build` in pos/ — clean (945 kB bundle). `npx tsc --noEmit` — no type errors.
  - **Still to do (user, in browser):** the UI flows can't be exercised headlessly. Walk: (1) value split 60/40 → two itemized receipts with grand total + Receipt N; (2) item split → allocate to 2 bills with different customers → pay sequentially → two separate invoices, two receipts, table freed, no KOT re-fire; (3) returns hidden by default, flip `custom_enable_returns` → Return reappears; (4) non-captain close with drafts → "pay or cancel" block; captain close → Pending transfer, close succeeds, end shift again → no duplicate; receiver Incoming Transfers → Approve (lands in their orders) / Reject (goes to a captain); hit the hop limit → blocked; (5) set `pin_expiry_days=1`, backdate `pin_changed_at` → PIN login forces a new-PIN dialog. **The user must restart bench** so the running workers pick up the new whitelisted methods before browser testing.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Item split is irreversible** (the source is cancelled, the bills submitted). There's no "un-split" — by design, like a normal payment. If a cashier mis-allocates, they'd return the bills (once returns are enabled) and re-ring. Worth a confirm step in the UI if it bites.
  - **KOT re-point degrades the Pending-KOTs filter** for item-split bills: a re-pointed KOT now lives on a SUBMITTED bill, and the `Pending KOTs` filter only matches `docstatus=0` drafts, so it won't show there. Acceptable — split happens at pay time after the bill is printed.
  - **Transfer owner-change-on-approve (not on create)** is what makes the receiver-approval semantics work: while Pending the draft stays owned by the captain (and shows in the captain's orders with a "Pending Incoming" flag); the receiver sees it only via the Incoming Transfers filter (driven by the doctype, not owner). The `_excluded_from_blocking` `to_user` distinction is the subtle bit — `Approved-to-me` must still block ME (I own it now) while `Approved-to-other` must not block the original captain. Both are unit-tested.
  - **A never-approved Pending transfer lingers** (owned by the captain). The hop cap bounds churn; the captain can pay/cancel/re-transfer it. A future scheduled auto-route or expiry could clean these up — out of scope.
  - **Value-split receipts are N separate printouts** (matching "two itemized payment receipts"), each headed by the grand total + its portion. If the user wants ONE combined receipt instead, the change is localized to `printSplitReceipts` (print one annotated copy listing all portions). Flagged for the browser-walk feedback.
  - **`custom_max_invoice_transfers` default is 2.** Existing profiles get 2 via `getPosProfile`'s `raw is None → 2` fallback even before re-save, so transfers are ON by default for existing branches. Set to 0 to disable.

### 2026-04-27 — Biometric / PIN login: full feature in 5 phases (URY Finger Agent + login revamp + PIN self-service)
- **Goal:** let URY cashiers sign in via fingerprint (ZK9500) or 6-digit PIN, with password as fallback. Captain/admin enrolls each cashier; cashier picks themselves on the login page and just places their finger.
- **Architecture (final, after a full day of dead ends):**
  - **Backend** — new doctypes `URY Biometric Settings` (singleton) + `URY Biometric Enrollment` (per-user). New whitelisted API in [ury/ury/biometric/api.py](ury/ury/biometric/api.py) with 9 endpoints: `get_public_settings` (guest), `search_users_for_login` (guest), `get_enrollment_template_for_login` (guest, rate-limited), `biometric_login` (guest), `pin_login` (guest), `enroll_biometric` (captain+), `change_pin` (auth), `admin_reset_enrollment` (captain+), `record_password_login` (auth). PINs hashed with `pbkdf2_sha256` via Frappe's `passlibctx`. Per-user PIN failure counter + lockout window. Per-(IP, user) rate limits via `frappe.cache()`.
  - **URY Finger Agent ([ury-finger-agent/](../ury-finger-agent/))** — new tiny local Java HTTP service that runs on each cashier PC alongside QZ Tray. Wraps the official ZKFinger Java SDK (`ZKFingerReader.jar`) which JNI-calls the native `libzkfp.dll` installed in `C:\Windows\System32` by the ISSOnline driver. Listens on `http://127.0.0.1:9994`. We OWN the wire format end-to-end — no fighting with vendor protocols. Runs on QZ Tray's bundled OpenJDK 11 JRE so the user doesn't need to install Java separately. Single self-contained 39 KB JAR + `start.bat` + `install.bat` (Windows Startup-folder shortcut auto-start) + `uninstall.bat`.
  - **Frontend** — `BiometricClient` + `useFingerprintReader` hook talk to the agent via REST. Three new pages/components: `BiometricEnrollment` (captain wizard at `/pos/biometric-enrollment`), `BiometricLogin` (cashier login at `/pos`, replacing Frappe's stock `/login`), `ResetPinDialog` (self-service PIN change in avatar dropdown). All built without `react-query`/`zod`/etc — vanilla React + plain `fetch`.
- **The dead ends that shaped this design (~6 hours of investigation):**
  - **Server-side template matching impossible.** `libzkfp.so` on the cloud Frappe server fails Init with `ZKFP_ERR_INIT (-2) = init capture library failed` because the SDK was designed assuming a USB device is present. Even DBMatch (which logically only needs templates, not a device) fails because Init failed. This invalidated the original "client captures, server matches" plan.
  - **ZKBioOnline (port 22001-22003) WebSocket protocol** — discovered live via a dialect explorer page we built. The wire IS `{function, module: "fingerprint", msgid, …}` with valid functions `open` / `close` / `register` / `cancelregister` / `verify`. But the locally-installed ZKBioOnline.exe (5.2.0.36, Oct 2020) crashes with `STATUS_STACK_BUFFER_OVERRUN (0xc0000409)` when its Oct-2020-vintage DLLs are mixed with the newer ZKFinger 5.3 capture set we needed to copy in. Reverted the DLL copy after confirming the version mismatch was unrecoverable without a fresh ZKBioOnline 5.3 build (which doesn't appear to exist publicly).
  - **ISSOnline (port 24008/24308)** — also discovered to be a REST API not a WebSocket. The HTTP shape `POST /ISSOnline {function, module: "fingerprint", msgid}` returns `ret: -2000` ("Unsupport") for every command we tried. The actual capture/enroll/verify commands are exposed via ActiveX (IE-only) — that's why the user's other attendance app says "Only Supports IE 64-bit browser". `GET /ISSOnline` did return a live status JSON (`{enroll_count: 3, server_version: "2.0.66", license_mode: "TRIAL"}`) confirming the service is healthy, just not browser-reachable for the operations we need.
  - **Lesson — when fighting a proprietary driver protocol, build your own agent.** Once we pivoted to the Java agent it took ~30 minutes to ship a working `/open` + `/enroll/start` + `/verify` REST surface. The earlier 6 hours were spent reverse-engineering wire formats we didn't actually need.
- **Schema additions:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_biometric_settings/](ury/ury/doctype/ury_biometric_settings/) — singleton, fields: `enabled`, `match_threshold` (default 80), `min_template_bytes` / `max_template_bytes` (template-size sanity check), `pin_max_attempts` / `pin_lockout_minutes`, `biometric_login_rate_limit_*`, `template_lookup_rate_limit_*`, and `issonline_ws_url` (legacy field name, repurposed as the agent URL — default now `http://127.0.0.1:9994`). Description block in the form has download buttons for the agent zip + ISSOnline driver + USB driver.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_biometric_enrollment/](ury/ury/doctype/ury_biometric_enrollment/) — per-user, autoname `field:user`. Stores `fingerprint_template` (Long Text, base64), `fingerprint_template_length`, `primary_finger`, `pin_hash` (hidden, no_copy, print_hide), `failed_pin_attempts`, `pin_locked_until`, and crucially `last_login_method` (Select: Biometric / PIN / Password) — used by the login page's smart-default tab.
- **Permissions ([ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py)):** added `URY Biometric Settings` + `URY Biometric Enrollment` to `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` (cashier read; needed for the self-service PIN reset dialog) and `URY Biometric Enrollment` to `CAPTAIN_EXTRA_WRITE_DOCTYPES` (captain + manager read+write+create+delete for enrollment management).
- **Translations ([ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv)):** added `URY Biometric Settings → ExPOS Biometric Settings` and `URY Biometric Enrollment → ExPOS Biometric Enrollment` so desk shows the rebrand.
- **Public assets ([ury/public/drivers/](ury/public/drivers/)):** ship 3 downloadables linked from the settings form:
  - `ury-finger-agent.zip` (41 KB) — the agent JAR + start/install/uninstall scripts + README.
  - `issonline-fingerprint-reader-driver-2.0.66.0.exe` (28 MB) — the ZKTeco web driver installer (provides the native `libzkfp.dll` in System32).
  - `fingerprint-reader-driver-5.3.0.30.exe` (11 MB) — standalone USB driver if needed separately.
- **Login flow (BiometricLogin page):**
  1. Guest hits `/pos` → `App.tsx` renders `BiometricLogin` (replaced the old `UnauthenticatedLanding` that linked to Frappe's `/login`).
  2. Cashier types name/email → autocomplete via `search_users_for_login` (220ms debounced, returns `{name, full_name, email, has_fingerprint, has_pin, last_login_method}`).
  3. Pick user → tabs render. Tab pre-selected based on `last_login_method` (or first-available method on first-ever login).
  4. **Biometric tab** — auto-arms as soon as the reader is ready (no button click needed). Phases: capturing → matching → authenticating → success. Errors pause auto-arm with a clear "Try again" button. Reuses `useFingerprintReader` from Phase 2.
  5. **PIN tab** — 6-digit input with auto-advance and auto-submit at 6 digits. Backend rate-limits with friendly "N attempts remaining" / lockout messages.
  6. **Password tab** — standard email + password form, posts to Frappe's `auth.loginWithUsernamePassword`. After success, calls `record_password_login` to update `last_login_method` so smart-default keeps working.
  7. After ANY successful login, `window.location.replace('/pos')` → App.tsx detects new session → normal POS flow.
- **Logout flow (cleanup at the end):** all 6 redirect points that previously sent users to Frappe's `/login?redirect-to=%2Fpos` now redirect to `/pos`, where `App.tsx`'s guest detection renders `BiometricLogin` directly. Touched: [Header.tsx](pos/src/components/Header.tsx) (main avatar Logout), [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx) (geofence Sign Out), [auth-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/auth-slice.ts) (auto-redirect on no session + auth check error), [POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx) (hard sign-out from gate-error screen). Removed the now-dead `UnauthenticatedLanding` function from App.tsx. The "Use the standard login page instead" small link at the bottom of `BiometricLogin` is the deliberate escape hatch that still goes to Frappe's `/login` — kept for admin recovery scenarios.
- **PIN self-service ([ResetPinDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ResetPinDialog.tsx)):** new modal in the user-menu dropdown for any signed-in user. Three PIN-cell inputs (current / new / confirm). Backend `change_pin` verifies the old PIN, hashes the new one, resets failure counter + lockout. The dialog is visible to every user; the backend friendly-rejects users who don't have an enrollment yet.
- **Critical install gotchas (documented in agent README too):**
  - **`libzkfp.dll` lives in `C:\Windows\System32`** (64-bit) — installed by the ISSOnline driver. Java's JNI loads it by name. The agent's `start.bat` passes `-Djava.library.path="%SystemRoot%\System32;%ZKFINGER_DIR%"` so Java finds it AND its transitive deps (zkfinger10.dll etc in `C:\Program Files (x86)\FPOnline\bin\ZKFinger\`).
  - **QZ Tray's bundled JRE is "minimal" and excludes the `jdk.httpserver` module.** That's why the agent ships its own `MiniHttp` (~200 lines, only uses `java.base`) instead of `com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpServer`. Verified via `java --list-modules`.
  - **Batch file parens trap.** `C:\Program Files (x86)\…` containing literal parens repeatedly broke `if`/`for` parsing in start.bat. Fixed by using explicit `goto :label` flow + avoiding chained `if defined X if exist Y` patterns.
  - **The user's reader had to be ZK-branded.** Their HP X360 also has a built-in Synaptics fingerprint sensor (VID_06CB) which ZKBioOnline crashes trying to load. Their actual external ZK9500 is VID_1B55&PID_0124 — once we hit the right device, everything worked.
- **Verification:**
  - 27 backend unit tests pass (`bench --site local16.5 execute ury.ury.biometric.test_biometric.run_biometric_tests`) — covers PIN format validation, PIN hashing, template validation (size + base64 + URL-encoded variant), enrollment create/update/re-enroll, non-URY-user rejection, PIN-required-on-create, admin reset, admin delete, wrong-PIN bumps counter.
  - Live end-to-end test on the user's machine: real ZK9500 enrollment via the captain wizard (3 scans, master template generated server-side via SDK's DBMerge), template stored in DB, then biometric login (capture → 1:1 match via agent → backend session creation) — confirmed by user "everything works with no errors".
  - PIN login + password login + smart-default tab switching all confirmed working.
  - PIN reset modal confirmed working (wrong PIN returns error + clears cells, correct old + new + confirm updates the hash).
  - Logout from any of the 4 entry points lands on `BiometricLogin` (not Frappe's `/login`).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Fingerprint templates are biometric data.** Currently stored as plain base64 in `URY Biometric Enrollment.fingerprint_template`. ZK templates are vendor-encoded (not reversible to a fingerprint image) but if any client requires encryption-at-rest under data-protection compliance, add AES-GCM with a site-wide key. Field is already marked `no_copy=1, print_hide=1, report_hide=1` so it doesn't leak into reports/exports.
  - **PIN entropy is low (6 digits ≈ 20 bits).** Rate limiting + lockout are the only thing keeping brute force impractical. Default: 5 failed attempts → 15 min lockout. Configurable in `URY Biometric Settings`.
  - **The agent runs as the user** (not as a Windows service). It needs to read the device which is bound to the logged-in user's session. Auto-start via Startup folder is the right pattern. Running as a service (e.g. via NSSM) is possible but adds complexity for no real benefit on a per-cashier-PC model.
  - **The captain's terminal is where enrollment scans happen** — meaning the cashier walks up to the captain's PC to enrol. The cashier's OWN terminal doesn't need a working reader at enrollment time. Once enrolled, the cashier can log in from any terminal that has the agent running.
  - **`last_login_terminal` is stamped on every login** but not used yet. Future feature: terminal-bound enrollment (cashier can only log in from terminals matching their assigned terminal). For now, any-terminal login is intentional.
  - **The `issonline_ws_url` field name is now legacy** — it stores an HTTP URL (the agent), not a WebSocket URL. Renamed via the form label to "URY Finger Agent URL" but kept the underlying field name for backward compatibility with sites that already had it set. The hook normalises `ws://` → `http://` at runtime so old values keep working.
  - **Test suite uses the `run_biometric_tests()` direct-runner pattern** from CLAUDE.md rule 6 — bypasses bench run-tests since ERPNext's test bootstrap is still broken on this site (see 2026-04-15 entry).

### 2026-04-16 — Reports page batch 2: Dashboard admin scope + Merges report + Transfers report
- **Context:** Follow-up to batch 1 after the user walked the new reports. Three asks: (1) Dashboard still scoped to `owner = session.user` — admins saw their own rings instead of the branch, (2) no audit trail for the order-merge + table-merge flows that shipped in the March/April rounds even though URY Order Merge Log + URY Table Merge Log doctypes exist, (3) no visibility on the shift-close draft-transfer flow from 2026-04-10 — admin couldn't answer "who dumped what onto whom during close".
- **Dashboard role-scoping ([get_dashboard_stats](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - Endpoint now branches on `_user_can_see_admin_reports()`. Cashier scope stays as `pi.owner = session.user`; admin/captain/manager scope flips to `pi.branch = getBranch()` — the dashboard becomes a branch-wide lens for admin. Response carries `is_admin: 0|1` + `scope: 'user'|'branch'` so the UI can render a scope badge honestly (frontend shows "Branch-wide" vs "My orders" pill).
  - Base query rewritten as one SQL (was three separate queries for total_sales / total_orders / total_customers, each with their own scoping clause that had to be kept in sync). Single query returns all core totals plus `returns_count` and `returns_amount` — returns now get their own dashboard tile (red accent, shows count + absolute amount).
  - Totals exclude `is_return=1` rows (returns have a dedicated tile) AND merged-source invoices (`custom_merged_into IS NULL`) — the master carries the combined total so counting the sources again would double-count.
  - Admin-only blocks appended to the response: `order_type_breakdown` (Dine In / Take Away / Delivery / Phone In / Aggregators, each with count + amount), `payment_mode_breakdown` (per-MoP totals from the Sales Invoice Payment child table), `active_cashiers` (per-owner totals with full_name + invoice count + grand total, ordered by grand_total DESC). Cashier dashboard stays lean — it only sees the 4 core tiles + returns + top selling items.
- **New `get_merge_report(from_date, to_date, terminal)` endpoint:**
  - Admin-only. Returns `{order_merges: [...], table_merges: [...], summary}` for the given window. Each merge row carries master name (POS Invoice or URY Table), source count (via correlated COUNT subquery on URY Order Merge Source / URY Table Merge Source), `sources_total` for order merges (sum of the pre-merge grand totals snapshotted on URY Order Merge Source), actor + timestamp + status + unmerge metadata.
  - `DATE(merged_at) BETWEEN %s AND %s` scoping — an Active merge that was created outside the window but is still open today doesn't show up (scoping is "merges that happened in this window", not "merges that are in some state today"). If admin wants the open/active view they can set from_date = to_date = today and filter on `status === 'Active'` in the UI.
  - Summary tile counts: `order_merge_count` (total in window), `order_merge_active` (currently still merged), same for tables. Drives four stat tiles at the top of the page.
- **New `get_transfer_report(from_date, to_date, terminal)` endpoint:**
  - Admin-only. Surfaces every POS Invoice that `submit_pos_closing_entry` transferred to another cashier at shift-close. Detection uses the `remarks LIKE 'Transferred from %%'` pattern (double-`%` because this is inside an f-string SQL template). Regex parsing on the Python side splits each row's `remarks` into `from_cashier` and `opening_entry` — defensive fallback to `None` for malformed remarks so a bad row doesn't crash the whole report.
  - User-full-name caching in a local dict so we don't re-query `tabUser` for every row when the same original cashier appears in dozens of transfers. Current live data shows 26 transfers across 3 distinct from→to pairs; cache saves 23 redundant lookups.
  - Scoping uses `pi.modified BETWEEN from 00:00:00 AND to 23:59:59` — `modified` is when the transfer stamp was set (via `frappe.db.set_value(..., update_modified=True)` in the close flow), which is a better proxy for "when the transfer happened" than `posting_date` (could be backdated). Terminal filter uses the same defensive null fallback as the Orders page.
  - Summary carries `count`, `total_amount` (sum of grand_total across transferred invoices), and `distinct_pairs` (unique from→to combinations). Frontend shows three stat tiles.
- **Frontend ([Reports.tsx](pos/src/pages/Reports.tsx) + [reports-api.ts](pos/src/lib/reports-api.ts)):**
  - `DashboardStatsExtended` type replaces the local `DashboardStats` — same field names plus the admin-only extras. Exported from `reports-api.ts` so it's the one source of truth.
  - `ReportTab` union extended with `'merges' | 'transfers'`. Two new admin-only tab buttons (GitMerge + ArrowRightLeft icons) appear in the tab bar next to Top/Bottom Items, gated on `canSeeAdminReports(user)`.
  - Dashboard render: new scope badge at top ("Branch-wide" or "My orders" pill with explanation). Returns tile renders conditionally (only when `returns_count > 0`) to keep the dashboard clean when nothing was returned that day. Admin-only grid below the core tiles: Order Type Breakdown + Payment Mode Breakdown side-by-side, Active Cashiers Today table below.
  - `<MergeReportView>` — two stacked tables (Order Merges + Table Merges), each with the same actor / timestamp / status columns. Active rows get blue/purple badge; Unmerged rows get gray badge + "undone by X at Y" sub-line. Stat tiles at the top show count + active-count for both.
  - `<TransferReportView>` — one table sorted by transfer time DESC. Each row shows invoice + customer + table on the left, From cashier → To cashier with an arrow icon, opening entry shift name, transfer time, current invoice status (Paid badge green, Draft gray), and amount. Three stat tiles at top: total count, total amount moved, distinct from→to pairs.
  - New `shortDateTime` helper at the bottom of the file formats "Apr 14, 6:13 PM" style — shared across merge + transfer views for consistent compact timestamps.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `bench --site local16.5 execute` on all 3 endpoints — real data: dashboard branch-scoped shows Today total ₵30 in one Take Away order with 1 active cashier (Patrick Anteh), merge report shows 3 order + 3 table merges with proper actor names (Captain Owusu, Administrator), transfer report shows 26 invoices transferred across 3 from→to pairs totaling ₵1,420.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 751.11 → 766.00 kB (~15 kB for new views + extended dashboard render + reports-api types).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Backend regex parsing of `remarks`** is fragile — if a future close-flow rewrite changes the remarks format, the transfer report breaks silently (the report returns `from_cashier: null` for every row but doesn't error). A cleaner design would be a dedicated `URY Draft Transfer Log` doctype written by `submit_pos_closing_entry` at the same time it stamps the remarks. Deferred — the remarks approach is zero-migration and works on every existing shift-close already in the database. Upgrade path is easy when/if we want the structured log.
  - **Dashboard `returns` tile** shows absolute amount (no negative sign) with a "−" prefix on the formatted number. Currency formatter doesn't know about sign; the minus is a string prefix. If `returns_amount` ever comes back genuinely negative (shouldn't — the SQL uses `ABS()`) the UI would render "−−X" which is ugly but not broken. The `ABS()` is defensive belt-and-braces.
  - **`order_type_breakdown` COALESCEs to `'Unknown'`** when `pi.order_type` is null. In practice URY orders always have a type set at sync time, but defensive coalesce means a pre-existing bad row doesn't hide from the admin's dashboard.
  - **Payment Mode Breakdown join on Sales Invoice Payment** — that's ERPNext's native child table, not URY's. Works today because POS Invoice submits payment rows into it. If a future flow writes payments to a different table (unlikely but possible), the breakdown goes silent. No indicator on the UI — just shows empty. Acceptable risk.
  - **Merge report shows source_count via correlated subquery** — one subquery per log row. For a site with thousands of merges this gets expensive. Mitigation: the date range keeps N bounded (typical range is 7-30 days with at most a few merges/day). If it ever shows up in profiling, denormalize: add a `source_count` Int column on URY Order Merge Log + URY Table Merge Log, stamp it at merge time, backfill via a patch.
  - **Transfer report's `distinct_pairs` count** collapses `(from=A, to=B)` + `(from=A, to=B)` to one — good for "is this a systematic cashier dumping pattern". If admin wants "total unique FROM cashiers" or "total unique TO cashiers" instead, those need separate aggregations. Not in scope; easy follow-up.
  - **Active cashiers table sorted by grand_total DESC** — same sort the Sales by Cashier report uses for consistency. The grand_total includes returns (is_return=0 filter is on the aggregation); if a cashier rang 10 sales + 1 return their grand_total here is sales-only. Matches the rest of the dashboard's returns-excluded accounting.

### 2026-04-16 — Reports page batch 1: Sales by Cashier / Category, Top & Bottom Items, My Shift Summary
- **Context:** Reports page at `/pos/reports` had only 2 tabs (Dashboard + Daily Sales) and both scoped exclusively to `owner=frappe.session.user` — cashiers saw their own day, but a captain or admin had no cross-cashier view. User asked for "more important reports" split into cashier-visible and captain/admin-visible tiers. First batch of 4 agreed up-front; later batches (Hourly Sales heatmap, Discounts/Voids, Open Tabs Aging, KOT Print Health) are deferred.
- **New backend endpoints** (all in [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py), all branch-scoped via `getBranch()`, all with optional `terminal` scoping that honors the defensive null fallback used by the Orders page):
  - **`_user_can_see_admin_reports(user=None)`** — role gate helper. Returns True for Administrator + `System Manager` / `URY Manager` / `URY Captain`. All three admin-only endpoints call this and `frappe.throw` with `PermissionError` if the caller doesn't qualify — so a malicious frontend spoofing the endpoint name can't escalate past server-side checks. Role list matches `canSeeAdminReports` on the frontend side in [role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts).
  - **`_reports_date_range(from_date, to_date)`** — normalizes a `(from, to)` pair with a default of last-7-days (today-6 → today). Used by all 3 range-based endpoints so the default window is consistent across the UI and the API (e.g. hitting the endpoint directly via bench execute with no args returns a useful range).
  - **`get_sales_by_cashier(from_date, to_date, terminal)`** — groups by POS Invoice `owner`, joins to `tabUser` for full names. Per-cashier columns: `invoice_count`, `sale_count` (excludes returns), `return_count`, `grand_total`, `net_total`, `return_amount`, `discount_amount`, `average_order_value`. AOV computed over sale_count only (not total invoice count) so a cashier with 10 sales + 1 return has AOV = sum_of_sales / 10 — returns don't deflate the value artificially. Ordered by `grand_total DESC` so the "who sold the most" cashier lands first. Excludes merged-source invoices (`custom_merged_into IS NULL`).
  - **`get_sales_by_category(from_date, to_date, terminal)`** — groups POS Invoice Items by department via the URY Menu Item → URY Menu Course → `custom_department` join. Critical detail: the join uses a `GROUP BY umi.item` subquery (`SELECT umi.item, MIN(umc.custom_department)`) so an item that appears on multiple URY Menu Items doesn't duplicate in the outer join. Defaults unclassified items to `'Food'` via `COALESCE` — matches `_classify_kot_item_department`'s default department so the reports page and the print routing agree on what belongs where. Server stamps a `percentage` field on each row (0-100) so the UI doesn't have to re-compute on every re-render. Excludes returns (`pi.is_return = 0`) so a refunded drink doesn't skew the drinks department total.
  - **`get_top_bottom_items(from_date, to_date, limit=10, terminal)`** — runs the same grouped SQL twice (once `ORDER BY total_qty DESC`, once `ASC`) and returns `{top: [...], bottom: [...]}`. `HAVING SUM(qty) > 0` clause on the bottom list excludes items that never sold — "slow movers" means low-velocity, not zero-velocity. `limit` clamped to `[1, 50]` server-side so a bad value can't DoS the query.
  - **`get_my_shift_summary(terminal)`** — thin wrapper around `preview_pos_closing_entry`. Looks up the caller's open POS Opening Entry (scoped by `user=frappe.session.user` + optional `custom_terminal`), calls the existing preview helper, and compacts the response to drop `transfer_candidates` + `draft_invoices` (those are closing-dialog concerns, not reporting). Normalizes field names to reports-friendly spellings (`paid_count` not `invoice_count`, `total_qty` not `total_quantity`, `total_tax` not `total_taxes_and_charges`). Returns `{has_open_shift: 0}` when the caller has no open entry — the UI renders a friendly "No open shift" card in that case. Visible to every role.
- **Frontend:**
  - **New [pos/src/lib/reports-api.ts](pos/src/lib/reports-api.ts)** — thin SDK wrappers around the 4 new endpoints + the response types (`SalesByCashierResponse`, `SalesByCategoryResponse`, `TopBottomItemsResponse`, `ShiftSummaryResponse`). `ReportDateRange` type captures the `(from_date, to_date, terminal)` triple shared by the 3 range endpoints.
  - **[role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts)** — new `canSeeAdminReports(user)` helper, same allowed-role list as `canSeeAllTerminalOrders` but named for the reports use case so the intent at each call site is clear. Cashiers return false; captains/managers/admins return true.
  - **[Reports.tsx](pos/src/pages/Reports.tsx)** — expanded from 2-tab to 6-tab, `ReportTab` union type. Cashiers see the first 3 tabs (Dashboard + Daily Sales + My Shift); admins see all 6. Tab buttons extracted into a shared `<TabButton>` sub-component to keep the markup tight. Date filter area is conditional: single-date picker for Dashboard/Daily Sales, from/to range picker for the 3 admin range-reports, no picker at all for My Shift (live data). Each report gets its own render sub-component:
    - `<ShiftSummaryView>` — 4 stat tiles (paid count, grand total, unpaid drafts, tax) + per-mode payment reconciliation table with opening + collected + expected total. "No open shift" empty state renders a friendly call-to-action pointing back to the POS opening dialog.
    - `<SalesByCashierView>` — per-cashier table sorted by grand total, with columns for invoices, returns, discount, AOV, net, grand. Header shows the grand total + cashier count + date range.
    - `<SalesByCategoryView>` — bar-chart-like horizontal rows with a colored dot per department (green/Food, blue/Drinks, amber/Other) + a progress bar filled to each department's percentage. Server-computed percentages drive the bar width so the UI is pure render.
    - `<TopBottomView>` — two side-by-side `<ItemRankCard>`s (Top Sellers green, Slow Movers red) with a numbered ranking + item name + qty sold + order count + revenue.
  - **Terminal scope** — all 4 reports pull `terminalName` from the POS store so a captain on Bar 1 can see the whole branch's sales but can ALSO pick "Bar 1" via the same scoping mechanism the Orders page uses (defensive null fallback: invoices created before terminal stamping was added still show up on every terminal of their branch).
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `bench --site local16.5 execute` on all 4 new endpoints — each returns real data from the existing POS Invoice rows on the dev site: 3 cashiers over 7 days totaling ₵210, 100% in Drinks department (Fanta 5 qty ₵150 + Sprite 3 qty ₵60), Administrator's current shift with 16 drafts + ₵1100 held.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 736.40 → 751.11 kB (~15 kB for 4 new reports + shared sub-components).
  - **Still to do:** browser walk — (a) log in as URY Cashier → only Dashboard, Daily Sales, My Shift tabs visible; (b) log in as URY Captain → all 6 tabs visible; (c) change the from/to range → admin reports refetch; (d) click My Shift while shift is open → see live tender breakdown; (e) log out of the shift → My Shift shows "No open shift" empty state.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The `get_my_shift_summary` field-name normalization is a small debt item.** `preview_pos_closing_entry` uses ERPNext-y names (`invoice_count`, `total_quantity`, `total_taxes_and_charges`) because it's also the data source for the POS Closing Entry submit. The reports endpoint renames them (`paid_count`, `total_qty`, `total_tax`) to match the rest of the reports schema. Two places now depend on the preview helper's shape — if its field names ever change, the normalization here breaks silently. Small risk, keeping it for consistency.
  - **The `SalesByCategory` query uses `MIN(custom_department)` as the tiebreaker** when an item appears on multiple URY Menu Items with different course departments. Drinks < Food < Other alphabetically, so an item tagged both Food AND Drinks (rare, but possible if the admin set up their menus sloppily) gets classified as Drinks. Acceptable for reporting — the number is a read-only insight, not an accounting entry. If it matters, admins can clean up the source data.
  - **The `average_order_value` column on Sales by Cashier** excludes returns from the denominator. Rationale: a cashier who rings 10 sales + 1 return has economic activity of 11 transactions but meaningful sales of 10; including the return in the divisor would make the AOV look artificially low on a busy-returns day. This is a choice, not a law — if admins prefer "gross transaction count" AOV the divisor should change to `invoice_count`.
  - **No charts / heatmaps in this batch.** Hourly Sales, Payment Mode Over Time, and similar time-series views require a chart library; Reports currently uses plain HTML tables + progress bars. Adding a chart lib (e.g. Recharts or Chart.js) is a future decision for when the 4-10 more reports hit the page. Keeping it lib-free for now keeps the bundle tight.
  - **`TopBottomView` shares one `ItemRankCard` component** for both top and bottom — the only difference is the badge tone (green vs red). Saves a copy-paste and means any future polish (hover states, click to drill down) ripples to both.
  - **Reports are read-only.** None of the new endpoints write anything — no Frappe document creation, no `frappe.db.set_value`. That's by design; Reports should never modify ledger state. If a future report needs an action button (e.g. "Reprint This Receipt"), it should call into an existing action endpoint, not add write logic to a reports endpoint.
  - **Future batches (queued in the Reports design doc discussion):** Hourly Sales heatmap, Payment Mode Analytics over time, Discounts & Voids report, Open Tabs Aging (drafts sitting open > N hours), KOT Print Health (% KOTs that fell through to the bill-printer fallback — sanity check on the new print routing). Each adds one endpoint + one tab; batch size depends on how much tuning feedback comes back on these four.

### 2026-04-16 — Print revamp Round 3: urypos/ legacy Vue POS deprecation
- **Context:** Third and final round of the print system overhaul. Round 1 shipped the unified POS Profile config + URY Printer doctype + KOT department routing + URY Production Unit switch. Round 2 was deferred (Round 2 will kill the `window.open('/printview')` fallback + add the in-POS print progress overlay — documented in the Round 1 Fixes log entries, not in scope here). Round 3's sole purpose is to delete the urypos/ Vue v1 POS source tree and the ~9 exclusive backend methods it depends on, since support officially ended December 2025 per README and the React POS (`pos/`) has full feature parity.
- **What was deleted:**
  - **[urypos/](urypos/)** — 53 tracked source files removed via `git rm -rf`: Vue 3 + Pinia stores, components, router, views, privateKey.js placeholder, vite/tailwind/postcss configs, package.json/yarn.lock, .vscode config, and public assets. Build output under `ury/public/urypos/` and `ury/www/urypos.html` stays gitignored — deployed sites need a manual `rm -rf ury/public/urypos ury/www/urypos.html` after pulling this commit (documented in the release note so admins don't miss it).
  - **Five whitelisted methods from [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py):**
    - `getRoom()` — returned `{branch, room}` tuples for the current user. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Table.js:92`. React POS uses `getRooms` (plural) via `table-api.ts` which is a DIFFERENT function and untouched.
    - `getInvoiceForCashier(status, cashier, limit, limit_start)` — a thin wrapper around `getPosInvoice` that only existed so the Vue POS at `urypos/src/stores/recentOrder.js:113` could call it with positional args. React POS calls `getPosInvoice` directly with named args.
    - `get_select_field_options()` — returned POS Invoice.order_type Select options. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Menu.js:170`. React POS reads the options from its own `ORDER_TYPES` constant in `data/order-types.ts`.
    - `getCashier(room)` — captain/sub-cashier lookup from the pre-2026-04-08 multi-cashier model that got dismantled when POS Opening Entry became per-terminal. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Table.js:148`. The Multiple Rooms + main cashier chain it reads is the deprecated captain model.
    - `getAggregatorMOP(aggregator)` — returned Aggregator Settings mode of payment. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/recentOrder.js:383`. React POS reads aggregator MOPs via a different per-invoice path.
  - **Four whitelisted methods from [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py):**
    - `pos_opening_check()` — legacy pre-per-terminal POS open check that used the deprecated Multiple Rooms join. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Auth.js:164` and the desk form script.
    - `table_transfer(table, newTable, invoice)` — same-room-only table move. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Table.js:435` and the desk form script. React POS has its own table-transfer flow in table-api.ts.
    - `captain_transfer(currentCaptain, newCaptain, invoice)` — captain-to-captain POS Invoice waiter reassignment, also from the deprecated multi-cashier model. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Table.js:460` and the desk form script.
    - `customer_favourite_item(customer_name)` — top-3 items a customer has ordered before. Only called by `urypos/src/stores/Customer.js:224` and the desk form script.
  - **[ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.js](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.js)** — the 1,289-line URY Order desk form script. It was the other caller of `table_transfer`, `captain_transfer`, `customer_favourite_item`, and `pos_opening_check` via Frappe desk-side `frappe.call({method: ...})` buttons. Deleted in full because every one of its custom buttons called into a deleted Python method. The URY Order doctype JSON stays (the Python module still hosts `sync_order`, `make_invoice`, `get_order_invoice`, `cancel_order` which the React POS uses) — only the desk form customization is gone. URY Order is a fully-vestigial doctype anyway (zero callers anywhere in the codebase create or query its records), so losing the desk form has no practical impact.
  - **`getBranchRoom` import** stripped from [ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) since `pos_opening_check` was its sole local caller. The function itself still exists in [ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) but is now orphaned — left in place for now since it's a 2-line defensive return and removing it is cosmetic. Future cleanup round.
- **What was rerouted:**
  - **[ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py)** — `website_route_rules` no longer maps `/urypos/<path:app_path>`. Replaced with a new `website_redirects` rule: `/urypos → /pos` + `/urypos/(.*) → /pos/\1` with `match_with_query_string=True`. Old bookmarks and deep-links 301-redirect to the React POS instead of 404'ing.
  - **[ury/fixtures/custom_html_block.json](ury/fixtures/custom_html_block.json)** — the "POS V1" entry (the desk workspace shortcut that linked to `/urypos`) removed. The "URY POS" entry linking to `/pos` stays. Next `bench migrate` does NOT delete the Custom HTML Block "POS V1" on existing sites (Frappe only imports fixtures on initial install). Admins on existing sites need to manually delete the "POS V1" Custom HTML Block from the desk if they want the shortcut gone; it'll redirect to `/pos` anyway via the website_redirects rule, so leaving it there produces the right UX automatically.
  - **[package.json](package.json)** — `ury-pos-install` and `ury-pos-build` scripts deleted. `postinstall` and `build` now only run `ury-mosaic-install + ury-posv2-install` and `ury-mosaic-build + ury-posv2-build` respectively. `yarn install` at the repo root no longer tries to install urypos's node_modules; `yarn build` no longer tries to build urypos.
- **What was NOT deleted (deliberate):**
  - **The URY Order doctype JSON + ury_order.py module** — the Python module still contains `sync_order`, `make_invoice`, `get_order_invoice`, `cancel_order`, and helpers that the React POS calls every time a cashier rings or pays an order. Deleting the module would break the React POS immediately. The doctype JSON stays so the module's `class URYOrder(Document): pass` import chain resolves.
  - **`ury/public/urypos/` + `ury/www/urypos.html`** — both are gitignored build artifacts. They physically exist on the filesystem on deployed sites and need a manual `rm -rf` after this commit merges. Included in the release note at the bottom of the commit message.
  - **The URY branch `v1`** referenced in the README's "previous version" callout stays. Admins who explicitly want the old version of the separate POS repo can still find it at that tag; this commit only removes the in-repo `urypos/` folder (the rewrite that lived inside URY after the separate-repo era). README is unchanged.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py), [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py), [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) — OK (no broken imports or references to deleted methods).
  - `json.load` on [custom_html_block.json](ury/fixtures/custom_html_block.json) + [package.json](package.json) — OK.
  - **`bench --site local16.5 migrate`** — clean, no errors, no orphan Custom HTML Block removal (fixtures only import on install, not migrate — admins will see POS V1 still exist in the desk until they delete it manually, but the link now 301-redirects to `/pos` so the UX is right).
  - **`bench --site local16.5 execute ury.ury.api.test_ury_print_routing.run_print_routing_tests`** — 48/48 tests still pass. None of the deleted methods had tests (they were pre-test-suite era code) so there's nothing to remove from the test file.
  - **`yarn build` in `pos/`** — clean, bundle 736.40 → 736.48 kB (no change from deletions — React POS never imported from `urypos/`).
  - **`yarn build` in `URYMosaic/`** — clean, bundle 234.05 kB unchanged.
  - **Top-level `yarn build`** — orchestrates URYMosaic + pos only, no urypos step anywhere in the chain. Clean.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **`ury/public/urypos/` cleanup on deployed sites:** admins run `rm -rf ury/public/urypos ury/www/urypos.html` on their bench after pulling. If they don't, the files linger but are harmless — nothing references them and the `/urypos` route now 301-redirects to `/pos` at the Frappe layer before the static file would ever be served.
  - **Legacy Vue dependency footprint:** removing `urypos/` saves about 228 MB of `node_modules` on dev machines after a fresh `yarn install`. CI builds lose one install + one Vite build step (~15-20s wall time).
  - **`urypos/` build output under `ury/public/urypos/assets/index-*.js`** — gitignored, never touched by git. The user will have stale build artifacts on their bench until they `rm -rf`; if some future release tries to serve `/urypos/` via the website_redirect, the redirect fires BEFORE Frappe hits the static asset layer, so the stale files never serve.
  - **`fav_items(customer)`** in [ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) is a SIBLING of `customer_favourite_item` with similar logic but a different param shape. NOT deleted — left alone because I didn't trace its callers this round, and the name doesn't appear in the 9-method list from the round-3 scope in the Fixes log entry for 2026-04-16 Round 1. If a future cleanup determines it's unused, it can go.
  - **The URY Order doctype itself is a candidate for deletion in a future round.** Zero callers create or read its records, but the whitelisted `sync_order`/`make_invoice` etc. functions still need somewhere to live. Moving them to `ury_pos/api.py` or creating a dedicated `ury_pos/orders.py` module would let the URY Order doctype go, shrinking the footprint further. Deferred.
  - **URY Order `ury_order.json`** still lists the `URY Captain` and `URY Manager` roles in its permissions — harmless since nothing creates docs, but cosmetic. Delete in a future cleanup round.

### 2026-04-16 — Print revamp Round 1 Phases A-D: hold Drinks, pending sidebar, KDS routing mode switch, URY Production Unit printer wiring
- **Four bundled workstreams on top of the 2026-04-16 URY Printer doctype landing.** All driven by the same conversation: after the user got the unified config working, three follow-up asks came up — (1) food should print at order time but drinks should HOLD until bill print, (2) cashiers need a sidebar filter with live badge showing invoices that still have unprinted KOT parts, (3) clients with existing URY Production Unit setups need a toggle to keep using the legacy per-production KOT flow, (4) in that legacy mode the URY Production Unit's printer_settings child table has to actually drive print routing instead of being ignored. Phases A, B, C, D below address each in turn. A single branch `print-revamp-round-1-frontend` carries all four.
- **Phase A — Per-department print tracking + fire held KOTs at bill print.**
  - **Schema:** new `URY KOT.custom_printed_departments` field (Small Text, default `"[]"`, `allow_on_submit=1`) — JSON array of department names (`["Food"]`, `["Food","Drinks"]`, etc). Added directly to [ury_kot.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.json) since URY KOT is URY-owned. Plus a new POS Profile Check field `custom_auto_print_drinks_kot` (default 0) — admin can flip ON to auto-fire drinks at order time for busy-bar workflows (default OFF = hold).
  - **Backend — tracking helpers in [ury_print.py](ury/ury/api/ury_print.py):**
    - `_get_printed_departments(kot_doc)` — parse the JSON field defensively (missing / empty / malformed / default `"[]"` / list-vs-dict shape → all return empty set).
    - `_compute_all_departments_for_kot(kot_doc)` — classify every item in `kot_items` by course department and return the full set.
    - `record_printed_departments(kot_name, departments)` — merge new depts into the JSON field; if `all_departments.issubset(merged)`, also flip `kot_printed=1`. Idempotent, uses `frappe.db.set_value(..., update_modified=False)` so URY KOT's submit hooks don't re-fire.
    - `mark_kot_departments_printed(kot_name, departments)` — `@frappe.whitelist()` wrapper for the frontend. Accepts a JSON string or an already-parsed list.
    - `filter_plan_for_auto_print(plan, pos_profile_doc)` — drops Drinks from the plan unless `custom_auto_print_drinks_kot=1`. Pure function — returns a new list, doesn't mutate.
  - **Backend — print path wiring ([ury_kot.py `multi_print_kot`](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py)):** After the resolver returns a plan, the new `filter_plan_for_auto_print` drops held departments. Successfully-printed departments get tracked into a local list and handed to `record_printed_departments` once at the end. Any Drinks entry that auto-print holds skips the print call but keeps its items on the KOT record — those items remain "pending" via the `kot_printed=0 AND NOT Food in custom_printed_departments` invariant.
  - **Backend — [get_latest_kot](ury/ury_pos/api.py):** query filter extended with `custom_printed_departments: ["in", [None, "", "[]"]]` so the polling listener never sees a partially-printed KOT twice. Subtracts already-printed departments from the plan before building `print_jobs`. Also returns the legacy `print_jobs` shape with per-department filtered HTML.
  - **Backend — [print_pending_kots_for_invoice(invoice)](ury/ury_pos/api.py):** new whitelisted endpoint. Validates the invoice exists + is not PU mode, walks every `kot_printed=0` URY KOT for the invoice, rebuilds the FULL plan (no auto-print filter), subtracts already-stamped departments, returns filtered `print_jobs` with a `kot_name` back-ref so the frontend can stamp per KOT after firing. Errors on individual renders are logged + skipped — a broken Drinks render shouldn't block the bill print.
  - **Frontend — [kot-listener.ts](pos/src/lib/kot-listener.ts):** new `markKotDepartmentsPrinted(kotName, departments)` helper that POSTs to `ury.ury.api.ury_print.mark_kot_departments_printed` with JSON-encoded departments + CSRF header. The main polling loop stamps just the departments that successfully printed (not the ones that threw). New `firePendingKotsForInvoice(invoice)` exported helper — the Orders page calls this before firing the bill print to re-fire any held KOTs.
  - **Frontend — [Orders.tsx `handlePrintOrder`](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx):** calls `firePendingKotsForInvoice(selectedOrder.name)` before `printOrder(...)`. On success, shows an info toast "Fired held KOT(s): Drinks" and bumps a `pendingKotRefreshVersion` counter so the sidebar badge re-polls immediately.
- **Phase B — "Pending KOTs" sidebar filter + live badge.**
  - **Frontend type:** [order-types.ts](pos/src/data/order-types.ts) gains `"Pending KOTs"` in the `OrderStatusType` union and adds it to `BASE_ORDER_STATUS_TYPES` (visible to every cashier by default).
  - **Backend — [getPosInvoice + searchPosInvoice](ury/ury_pos/api.py):** `status_map` and the search path gain a new `Pending KOTs` branch. Translates to `docstatus=0` (Draft) + a correlated EXISTS subquery against `tabURY KOT` where `kot.invoice = pi.name AND kot.kot_printed = 0 AND kot.docstatus != 2`. Charged-to-room drafts excluded via the same predicate as the other pseudo-statuses.
  - **Backend — new [get_pending_kot_count(terminal, posting_date)](ury/ury_pos/api.py)** whitelisted endpoint. Same branch + terminal + posting_date scoping as the list endpoint, but deliberately NO cashier filter — pending KOTs are a kitchen/bar concern, not a per-cashier ledger. A captain arriving mid-shift sees every held ticket on the terminal regardless of who rang it. Returns `{count: int}`.
  - **Frontend API:** [invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts) gains `getPendingKotCount(terminal?, posting_date?)` — returns 0 on any error so the badge never breaks the sidebar.
  - **Frontend — [OrderStatusSidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx):** new `pendingKotCount` state + `refreshPendingCount` callback polling every 15 seconds AND on terminal/date changes AND on a parent-driven `refreshVersion` counter bump. The "Pending KOTs" row renders an orange badge with the count when > 0, and the FileText icon also turns orange. Badge has a `title` attribute showing "N orders with un-printed KOT".
  - **Frontend — [Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx):** new `pendingKotRefreshVersion` state bumped after `firePendingKotsForInvoice` returns any fired KOTs so the sidebar updates immediately instead of waiting for the 15s tick.
- **Phase C — POS Profile `custom_kds_routing_mode` switch + URYMosaic routing.**
  - **Schema:** new `POS Profile.custom_kds_routing_mode` Select field (options `Menu Course\nURY Production Unit`, default `Menu Course`), dual-source-of-truth in [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures list.
  - **Backend — [process_items_for_kot](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_generate.py):** branches on the new mode. In Menu Course mode, skips the `URY Production Unit` lookup and dumps every item into a single fallback KOT with `production=None`. In Production Unit mode, keeps the existing per-production loop unchanged.
  - **Backend — [kot_list(target=None)](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_display.py):** rewritten. Accepts an optional `target` URL segment (`Food`, `Drinks`, `Other`, `All`, or a production-unit name in legacy mode). Reads `custom_kds_routing_mode` from the branch's POS Profile. In Menu Course mode with a specific target, walks each KOT's `kot_items` and filters rows by `_classify_kot_item_department(row) == target`, drops KOTs that end up empty, stamps `kotjson["production"] = target` so the Vue client's existing `v-if="kot.production === production"` filter passes unchanged. `All` is a no-op filter that still stamps `production = "All"`. Legacy URY Production Unit mode returns the full branch list exactly like before. Response now also carries `kds_routing_mode` so the client can surface the current mode.
  - **Backend — [URYKOT.kotDisplayRealtime](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py):** rewritten. In Production Unit mode, one `publish_realtime` call to `kot_update_<branch>_<production>` (unchanged legacy behavior). In Menu Course mode, fans out one publish per distinct department present in the KOT's items (`kot_update_<branch>_<Food|Drinks|Other>`) PLUS an unconditional publish to `kot_update_<branch>_All` so a single-screen "show everything" KDS gets one ping per KOT regardless of department.
  - **Frontend — [URYMosaic kot.vue](URYMosaic/src/components/kot.vue):** `fetchKOT` passes `target: this.production` (the URL segment). `kds_routing_mode` stored on the component. No structural changes to the masonry grid or the per-kot filter — the backend stamping on `kotjson.production` keeps the v-if chain working.
  - **Unit tests:** +5 KDS filter scenarios in [test_ury_print_routing.py](ury/ury/api/test_ury_print_routing.py) cover the per-department filter predicate (Food target keeps Food rows, Drinks target keeps Drinks rows, All target is a pass-through, Other matches Other, negative case drops all rows). Written as pure-function tests so they don't need real URY KOT / POS Profile / Branch fixture chains.
- **Phase D — URY Production Unit printer wiring + hidden POS Profile fields + translations.**
  - **Schema change:** `URY Printer Settings.printer` native Link field `options` flipped from `Network Printer Settings` → `URY Printer` directly in [ury_printer_settings.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_printer_settings/ury_printer_settings.json). Affects all three parents of the child table: POS Profile, URY Production Unit, and URY Room.
  - **Backfill patch [backfill_ury_printer_settings_links.py](ury/patches/v0_2/backfill_ury_printer_settings_links.py):** new `[post_model_sync]` patch that walks `SELECT DISTINCT printer FROM tabURY Printer Settings` and ensures every referenced name has a matching URY Printer record. Clones `server_ip`/`port`/`printer_name` from the legacy `Network Printer Settings` record when one exists, else creates a bare record with `localhost:631` + the name as the CUPS printer. Guarded with `frappe.db.has_column("URY Printer Settings", "printer")` and `frappe.db.exists("DocType", "URY Printer")`. Idempotent.
  - **Backend — [multi_print_kot](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py):** reads `custom_kds_routing_mode` upfront. In URY Production Unit mode, forces `plan = []` so the resolver's Menu-Course-only plan is skipped and the function falls through to the legacy URY Production Unit child table path. Also: the legacy `print_kot` helper is rewritten to call `ury_print_via_cups` (the new URY Printer CUPS layer) instead of Frappe's `print_by_server` — that keeps the Production Unit path cloud-compatible and consistent with the Menu Course path. Failed prints log via `frappe.log_error` with title "URY KOT production-unit print failed" (was silent before).
  - **Backend — [get_latest_kot](ury/ury_pos/api.py):** new "URY Production Unit QZ path" branch runs BEFORE the Menu Course path. Reads `kot_doc.production`, queries `URY Printer Settings` child rows with `parent=<production>, parenttype='URY Production Unit', custom_kot_print=1`, honors the `custom_block_takeaway_kot` flag per row (skips takeaway-blocked rows when the order is a takeaway), renders full KOT HTML (no department filtering — the KOT already only holds items for this production), and returns `print_jobs` stamped with `production_unit_mode: 1`. Each print job's `department` field carries the production name as a sentinel — in PU mode it's purely informational, not used by the tracking path.
  - **Backend — [print_pending_kots_for_invoice](ury/ury_pos/api.py):** short-circuits with an empty `print_jobs` list when the POS Profile is in PU mode. Rationale: PU mode has no "held" concept — every KOT auto-fires at order time through its production's own printers. The Orders page's "fire pending KOTs before bill print" step becomes a no-op.
  - **Frontend — [kot-listener.ts](pos/src/lib/kot-listener.ts):** destructures `production_unit_mode` from the response. When `production_unit_mode === 1`, iterates `print_jobs` normally but calls the OLD whole-KOT `mark_kot_printed` endpoint once after the loop (instead of `markKotDepartmentsPrinted`). A single failed row doesn't block marking the whole KOT — PU mode has no retry tracking, mark once and move on.
  - **POS Profile field hiding:** seven custom fields (`custom_kitchen_kot_printer`, `custom_bar_kot_printer`, `custom_parcel_kot_printer`, `custom_drinks_kot_route`, `custom_food_kot_route`, `custom_takeaway_kot_route`, `custom_auto_print_drinks_kot`) now carry `depends_on: eval:doc.custom_print_mode != 'Disabled' && doc.custom_kds_routing_mode != 'URY Production Unit'`. In PU mode the admin only sees `custom_print_mode`, `custom_bill_printer`, `custom_print_fallback_mode`, `custom_kds_routing_mode`. Dual-sourced in both [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) and [setup.py](ury/setup.py) per the dual-source-of-truth rule.
  - **Translations audit — [en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv):** added `URY Production Unit → ExPOS Production Unit`, `URY P and L Materials → ExPOS P and L Materials`, `URY Table Merge Log → ExPOS Table Merge Log`, `URY Table Merge Source → ExPOS Table Merge Source`. Fixed typos: `URY Printer Settings → ExPOS Printer Expenses` (wrong word) → `ExPOS Printer Settings`; `URY Setup → ExPos Setup` → `ExPOS Setup`; trailing-comma normalization on every line. Audited every URY-prefixed doctype in [ury/ury/doctype/](ury/ury/doctype/) against the CSV — every URY-owned DocType now has a translation row.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on every edited Python file — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` + `ury_kot.json` + `ury_printer_settings.json` — OK.
  - **`bench --site local16.5 migrate`** — installed the new POS Profile / URY KOT fields, ran both new patches (`backfill_ury_printer_settings_links` + updated `migrate_print_config`), permissions baseline unchanged, pycups detected green.
  - **`bench --site local16.5 execute ury.ury.api.test_ury_print_routing.run_print_routing_tests`** — 48/48 tests pass in 1.2s (+5 new Phase C KDS filter tests + 11 new Phase A tracking tests — `_get_printed_departments`, `_compute_all_departments_for_kot`, partial-vs-full coverage invariant, KDS filter per department).
  - **`yarn build` in `pos/`** — clean, bundle 735.56 kB → 736.40 kB (~1 kB added for Phase B sidebar wiring + PU listener branch).
  - **`yarn build` in `URYMosaic/`** — clean, bundle 234.05 kB (target param + kds_routing_mode state).
  - **User confirmed end-to-end in browser on 2026-04-15**: (a) food prints at submit, drinks held, Print Invoice fires drinks then bill, pending badge drops to 0; (b) `/URYMosaic/Food`, `/URYMosaic/Drinks`, `/URYMosaic/All` each render the right subset in Menu Course mode; (c) switching POS Profile to URY Production Unit mode hides the seven non-applicable fields, ringing an order in PU mode now fires KOTs to the URY Production Unit's printer_settings rows (NOT the POS Profile's Kitchen printer — that was the final bug to fix).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The final PU-mode "still printing to POS Profile's Kitchen printer" bug** was a three-branch-point fix: `multi_print_kot` (for CUPS mode), `get_latest_kot` (for QZ mode), AND `print_pending_kots_for_invoice` (for the bill-print trigger) ALL needed an explicit `kds_mode == 'URY Production Unit'` check. The first pass only fixed schema + legacy CUPS helper — missed the two QZ-related code paths because the resolver `resolve_kot_print_plan` unconditionally returned a plan based on POS Profile fields whenever `custom_print_mode != 'Disabled'`, masking the mode difference. Lesson: any code path that builds a print plan must consult `custom_kds_routing_mode`, not just `custom_print_mode`. If a fifth print-plan builder ever gets added, this needs the same gate or it reintroduces the bug.
  - **Auto-print Drinks OFF default** — the user explicitly asked for this ("we dont want the drinks to be printed during the order and we dont want the bill to be printed as well"). Drinks department KOTs are CREATED on the URY KOT record at order submit time, but only FOOD auto-fires. Drinks (and nothing else — Food and Other always fire) wait for the cashier to hit Print Invoice at the end of the meal. The Check field flips this back to the "fire everything at submit" behavior for busy-bar workflows where the bartender needs the ticket immediately. Not wired to a Client Script — admin just checks/unchecks + saves.
  - **`custom_printed_departments` field is on the URY KOT record**, not on individual KOT items. The reasoning: a KOT is one row in the DB, and the "fully printed" decision is `all_item_departments ⊆ printed_departments`. Per-item tracking would let us detect individual item reprints but would bloat the schema and complicate the "is this invoice pending?" query. The JSON-array-in-a-text-field compromise is fast enough for the polling load (max a few dozen unprinted KOTs at any time) and readable in the desk without needing a custom form.
  - **Phase B sidebar badge polls every 15 seconds** — chosen as "fresh enough to trust, doesn't hammer the DB". The EXISTS subquery is indexed on `kot.invoice` so it's fast even at scale. Combined with the explicit `refreshVersion` bump after Print Invoice, the badge is usually accurate within 1-2 seconds of any state change.
  - **Phase C URYMosaic client** is deliberately minimal — the backend stamps `kot.production = target` on every returned KOT so the existing `v-if="kot.production === production"` filter just works. No new Vue logic, no new routes. If a future feature wants to show the department name visually on the card (e.g., a "FOOD" badge), it needs a proper front-end tweak.
  - **Phase D backfill patch deliberately does NOT delete legacy `Network Printer Settings` records.** They linger as unreferenced data. Any admin who wants to clean them up can delete them manually in the desk. Deleting them automatically during migrate would be destructive and could break other apps (ERPNext's own POS flows, POS Awesome, etc) that still depend on the doctype.
  - **`bill` Check on URY Printer Settings is still there** but currently it's only consumed by the legacy POS Profile path (`migrate_print_config.py` reads it to pick a bill printer during migration). In the new URY Production Unit path it's a visual hint only — the actual bill printing still goes through POS Profile's `custom_bill_printer` via the React POS's `printOrder`. If a client wants the production-unit bill printer to ALSO receive bill prints in QZ Tray mode, that'd need a new fan-out step in `printOrder` — punted as Round 2B if the ask ever comes up.
  - **The Round 2 scope (`window.open('/printview')` replacement + retry overlay)** was NOT done in this phase batch. Current Round 1 state is "unified config + department routing + PU mode toggle" — the `window.open` fallback still fires when QZ Tray is unreachable and the user sees the new tab. Round 2 will fix that. Round 3 deletes `urypos/` legacy (up next after push).
