  - **KOT Thermal Print format** — the user created a `ury/ury/print_format/kot_thermal_print/kot_thermal_print.json` fixture directly in the desk during testing. Ships as part of this commit since it's the print format the new config relies on for cheap thermal printers. Not wired into any backend code — admins assign it as the default print format on their URY Printer Settings rows.

### 2026-04-16 — URY Printer doctype replaces Network Printer Settings for POS Profile link target (pycups catch-22)
- **Symptom:** User hit the 2026-04-16 print revamp Round 1 schema landing and then tried to create a `Network Printer Settings` record in the desk. Two errors in sequence: (a) `pycups` missing → "Please contact your system manager to enable this by installing pycups!" — fixed by adding `pycups>=2.0.1` to [requirements.txt](requirements.txt) + a defensive check in [ury/install.py](ury/install.py) `_check_cups_dependency` that prints platform-aware install commands (`libcups2-dev` for Debian, `cups-devel` for RHEL, etc). (b) After installing pycups, `cups.Connection()` threw `RuntimeError: failed to connect to server` — because there was no CUPS daemon at `localhost:631` and the user's browser form couldn't populate the required `printer_name` Select field without one.
- **Root cause (architectural):** ERPNext's `Network Printer Settings` doctype has `printer_name` as a **required Select field** whose options are populated dynamically by the "Get Printers List" button — and that button calls a whitelisted method that connects to a live CUPS server via `pycups`. So you literally cannot create a `Network Printer Settings` record on a Frappe site where CUPS is unreachable. For **cloud-hosted URY deployments** (the normal case — all URY customer sites are cloud-hosted Frappe), CUPS is fundamentally unreachable because the cloud server can't talk to the restaurant's on-premise printer network. The previous Round 1 schema that linked POS Profile's printer fields to `Network Printer Settings` inherited this dead-end: admins couldn't even CREATE the records the new unified config expected them to link to.
- **Fix — new URY-owned `URY Printer` doctype:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_printer/ury_printer.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_printer/ury_printer.json) + `.py` + `__init__.py`. Fields: `printer_name` (Data, reqd, autoname `field:printer_name`, unique) — **free text**, admin types whatever the cashier's local OS calls the printer. `description` (Small Text). `disabled` (Check). A collapsible "CUPS Direct Settings (optional)" section with `server_ip` (Data, default "localhost") + `port` (Int, default 631) + a yellow-box HTML note explaining that CUPS Direct mode doesn't work on cloud-hosted Frappe and QZ Tray is the way. Permissions baseline: System Manager + URY Manager = full write. URY Captain = read + write + create. URY Cashier = read.
  - **All 4 POS Profile print link fields flipped** from `"options": "Network Printer Settings"` to `"options": "URY Printer"` in [custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) and mirrored in [setup.py](ury/setup.py). The field values (what gets stored) are now URY Printer names; the field semantics (bill, kitchen KOT, bar KOT, parcel KOT) are unchanged.
  - **New helper `ury_print_via_cups()`** in [ury/ury/api/ury_print.py](ury/ury/api/ury_print.py) replaces the call to `frappe.utils.print_format.print_by_server` for the new unified path. It reads `server_ip` / `port` / `printer_name` from a **URY Printer** record (not Network Printer Settings), opens a `cups.Connection()` directly, renders the doc to PDF via `frappe.get_print(..., as_pdf=True)`, writes a temp file, and prints via `conn.printFile(cups_printer_name, ...)`. Returns `(ok, err_msg)` so the caller applies the `custom_print_fallback_mode` policy. Defensive: handles missing pycups, missing URY Printer, unreachable CUPS, failed PDF render, and failed print — all returned as `err_msg` so the fallback logic kicks in cleanly.
  - **`_try_print_kot` in [ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py)** switched from `print_by_server` to `ury_print_via_cups`. The legacy path (old `URY Printer Settings` child table → Network Printer Settings names) still uses `print_by_server` via the internal `print_kot` helper, so existing installs keep working until migration.
- **Migration patch ([ury/patches/v0_2/migrate_print_config.py](ury/patches/v0_2/migrate_print_config.py)) rewritten:**
  - **Two stages.** Stage 1 creates `URY Printer` records from any legacy `Network Printer Settings` names referenced by existing POS Profiles (via `custom_table_order_printer`, `custom_parcel_order_printer`, or the `printer_settings` child table). The helper `_ensure_ury_printer(nps_name)` looks up the legacy record if one exists to copy `server_ip`/`port`/`printer_name`, OR creates a bare URY Printer record with just the name if no legacy doc is found. Returns `(name, created_flag)` so the stats are honest. Idempotent — skips existing URY Printer records.
  - Stage 2 writes the new POS Profile fields (`custom_print_mode`, `custom_bill_printer`, `custom_kitchen_kot_printer`, `custom_parcel_kot_printer`) with **URY Printer** names (not Network Printer Settings names). Stage 1 guarantees every reference has a matching URY Printer record so the Link field validates cleanly. Bar KOT stays blank — admin picks post-migrate.
  - **Both stages idempotent + guarded against missing columns/doctypes** via `frappe.db.has_column` and `frappe.db.exists("DocType", "URY Printer")` checks. A mis-ordered `patches.txt` or a fresh install with the doctype not yet synced degrades to a no-op instead of crashing migrate.
- **pycups dependency declaration:**
  - Added `pycups>=2.0.1` to [requirements.txt](requirements.txt) with a comment listing the OS-package prerequisites (`libcups2-dev` for Debian/Ubuntu, `cups-devel` for RHEL/Fedora, `libcups` for Arch) because pycups is a C extension that can't build without them.
  - **New `_check_cups_dependency()`** hook in [ury/install.py](ury/install.py), wired into `after_install` + `after_migrate`. Runs `import cups`; on success prints a green "pycups detected" line; on failure detects the Linux distro from `/etc/os-release` and prints a multi-line **yellow** warning (non-fatal — migrate doesn't crash) with the exact `sudo apt-get install -y libcups2-dev`, `./env/bin/pip install pycups`, `bench restart` command sequence for that distro. Also supports RHEL/Fedora/Arch/macOS and falls back to a generic hint for unknown platforms. The warning is deliberately loud + actionable so admins don't have to guess at the installation recipe.
- **Translations ([ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv)):** `URY Printer → ExPOS Printer`.
- **Permissions ([ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py)):** `URY Printer` added to `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` (cashier read) and `CAPTAIN_EXTRA_WRITE_DOCTYPES` (captain + manager full write). `ensure_role_permissions()` picks it up automatically on the next migrate.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on all edited Python files — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` + `ury_printer.json` — OK.
  - `bench --site local16.5 migrate` — URY Printer doctype synced, 2 new Custom DocPerm rows for URY Captain / URY Manager, pycups detected green, migration patch ran cleanly (0 profiles to migrate — there were none on the test site with legacy print config).
  - **All 27 existing unit tests still pass** (`bench --site local16.5 execute ury.ury.api.test_ury_print_routing.run_print_routing_tests`) — the tests use string printer names as stand-ins so they don't care what doctype the string is validated against.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Frontend impact: zero.** The React POS still reads `posProfile.custom_bill_printer` etc as plain strings. Whether the string is a Network Printer Settings name or a URY Printer name doesn't matter to the client — it just passes the name to QZ as the target.
  - **Legacy `Network Printer Settings` records are not deleted** by the migration. They linger as unreferenced data. If an admin wants to clean them up, they can delete them manually in the desk or with a script. Deleting them automatically during migrate would be destructive and could break other apps (ERPNext, POS Awesome, etc) that still depend on the doctype.
  - **The `cups_note` HTML field in URY Printer** is a Frappe `HTML` fieldtype — renders inline in the form. Admins see the yellow-box warning right next to the CUPS settings so they know when NOT to use them. Small but meaningful UX win.
  - **CUPS Direct mode on self-hosted deployments** now has the ENTIRE print chain under URY's control — from POS Profile → URY Printer → `ury_print_via_cups()` → `cups.Connection()`. No dependency on ERPNext's `print_by_server` which was always brittle. If CUPS fails, the fallback policy kicks in and routes to the bill printer (or fails silently / loudly per the profile setting).
  - **The `ury_print_via_cups` helper is NOT whitelisted.** It's an internal helper called from `_try_print_kot`. If a future use case needs it exposed as an API (e.g. a manual "reprint this KOT" button that bypasses the normal flow), wrap it in a thin `@frappe.whitelist()` shim.
  - **Why this wasn't part of the original Round 1 scope:** I assumed `Network Printer Settings` was safe to reuse because Frappe ships it out of the box. The catch-22 only became visible when the user tried to create a record and hit the "Get Printers List" dead-end. Classic "assume the built-in primitive works" trap. The lesson: when a feature depends on an ERPNext doctype that has a REQUIRED Select field populated by a whitelisted method, think through whether that method will actually work on the user's deployment BEFORE wiring it into URY's data model.

### 2026-04-16 — Print system revamp Round 1: unified POS Profile config + department-based KOT routing
- **Round 1 of a 3-round print system overhaul.** User's top complaints: (a) first-time POS Profile printer setup is too confusing (scattered across 10+ custom fields, a `printer_settings` child table, and per-venue overrides on URY Room / URY Production Unit); (b) silent print failures — the old `multi_print_kot` had a bare `except: pass` that swallowed every printer error; (c) KOTs print the whole order to every station, so the bar sees burgers and the kitchen sees beers. Round 1 tackles (a) and the "mixed-order noise" part of (c). Round 2 will kill the `window.open('/printview')` fallback and add the in-POS animation overlay + proper print-state tracking. Round 3 will delete `urypos/` legacy.
- **Investigation:** Two Explore subagents mapped the current print architecture and the urypos/ legacy surface area. Findings drove the design in this round (see the multi-paragraph recap inside Round 1's discussion — 5 architectural smells identified, 4 urypos blast-radius items catalogued).
- **Design decisions (pre-agreed with user):**
  - **QZ Tray is the default print mode.** All deployments are cloud-hosted Frappe → the server can't reach on-premise printers directly. QZ Tray runs in the cashier's browser and talks to the local printer network. CUPS Direct is available as a second option for self-hosted deployments where the server has direct CUPS access.
  - **Department classification lives on URY Menu Course**, not per-item. Admins tag each course Food / Drinks / Other once; all items in that course inherit. Simpler than per-item tagging and naturally maps to how restaurants organize their menus.
  - **Mixed KOTs split by department.** A KOT with 2 Beers + 1 Burger produces two print jobs — the bar gets only the beers, the kitchen gets only the burger. Filtering happens in-memory via `frappe.copy_doc(kot)` with a trimmed items list; the same print format is reused, no new template needed.
  - **Fallback behavior:** when a target printer is offline/missing, the POS Profile's `custom_print_fallback_mode` decides. Default = "Fallback to Bill Printer" — reroutes the print to the bill printer with a "DRINKS - TARGET PRINTER OFFLINE" header so the receiving station still sees the order. Alternative modes: "Fail Silently" (drop the job), "Fail with Alert" (drop + loud toast to cashier).
  - **Desk form redesign is schema-only for Round 1.** A collapsible Section Break + field descriptions handle the "friendlier config" ask. A proper Client Script with test-print buttons / routing diagrams is a Round 2 polish item.
- **Schema ([ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures list, all three per the dual-source-of-truth rule):**
  - **POS Profile** gains 10 new fields inside a collapsible "Printers & Routing" section:
    - `custom_print_routing_section` (Section Break, collapsible)
    - `custom_print_mode` (Select, default "QZ Tray") — options `QZ Tray\nCUPS (Direct)\nDisabled`
    - `custom_bill_printer` (Link → Network Printer Settings) — `mandatory_depends_on: eval:doc.custom_print_mode != 'Disabled'`
    - `custom_kitchen_kot_printer`, `custom_bar_kot_printer`, `custom_parcel_kot_printer` (Link → Network Printer Settings, optional)
    - `custom_drinks_kot_route` (Select: `Bar\nKitchen\nBill`, default "Bar")
    - `custom_food_kot_route` (Select: `Kitchen\nBar\nBill`, default "Kitchen")
    - `custom_takeaway_kot_route` (Select: `Parcel\nKitchen`, default "Parcel")
    - `custom_print_fallback_mode` (Select: `Fallback to Bill Printer\nFail Silently\nFail with Alert`, default "Fallback to Bill Printer")
    - All data fields depend on `eval:doc.custom_print_mode != 'Disabled'` so they hide when printing is off.
  - **URY Menu Course** gains `custom_department` (Select: `Food\nDrinks\nOther`, default "Food", `in_list_view: 1`, `in_standard_filter: 1`) so admins can see and filter by department in the list view.
- **Backend ([ury/ury/api/ury_print.py](ury/ury/api/ury_print.py)):**
  - **New section "Unified print routing (2026-04-16)"** added at the end of the file (~200 lines). Doesn't touch the existing `network_printing`, `select_network_printer`, `print_pos_page`, `print_kot_on_create` functions — those stay as legacy back-compat paths.
  - **`_get_course_department_map()`** — cached per-request map of `{course_name: department}` via `frappe.local._ury_course_dept_cache`. One fetch regardless of how many KOT items are classified.
  - **`_classify_kot_item_department(item)`** — returns the department for a single KOT item by looking up its `course` in the map. Missing / unknown course defaults to "Food".
  - **`_split_kot_items_by_department(kot_doc)`** — buckets a KOT's items into a `{department: [items]}` dict. Empty departments are NOT in the output; only non-empty buckets.
  - **`_resolve_printer_for_department(pos_profile_doc, department, order_type=None)`** — the core router. Reads the new `custom_*_kot_route` fields, maps each route to its printer field (Bar → `custom_bar_kot_printer`, Kitchen → `custom_kitchen_kot_printer`, etc), and returns the printer name. Cascades: if the preferred printer is empty, falls through Kitchen → Bill. Honors the takeaway override (Food + Take Away → Parcel).
  - **`resolve_kot_print_plan(kot_doc, pos_profile_name, order_type=None)`** — the public planner. Returns `[{printer, department, items, fallback}]` — one entry per department that has items. When `custom_print_mode` is unset or "Disabled", returns `[]` so the caller falls back to the legacy path.
  - **`apply_print_fallback(plan_entry, pos_profile_doc)`** — reads the `custom_print_fallback_mode` and returns `(should_print, printer, header)`. For "Fallback to Bill Printer" it returns the bill printer + a `"DRINKS - TARGET PRINTER OFFLINE"` (or similar) header string. The caller stamps that header on `doc.flags.fallback_header` before rendering.
- **Rewiring ([ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_kot/ury_kot.py)):**
  - **`multi_print_kot` rewritten** with a new-config-first resolution: calls `resolve_kot_print_plan`. If the plan is non-empty (unified config is set), iterates each plan entry, makes a filtered copy of the KOT via `frappe.copy_doc(self)` with only that department's items, stamps `doc.flags.kot_department` for the print format to pick up, and prints via a new `_try_print_kot` helper. On failure → applies fallback via `apply_print_fallback` + logs + publishes a realtime `kot_print_failed` event so the React POS can show a toast.
  - **Legacy path preserved.** When `resolve_kot_print_plan` returns `[]` (mode is unset), the old nested Production-Unit → Room → POS-Profile printer lookup runs unchanged. The one difference: the legacy `print_kot` helper's bare `except: pass` is replaced with `except Exception: frappe.log_error(...)` so silent failures at least end up in the Error Log. This is a tiny bit of Round 2 hardening that was too dangerous to leave in place for Round 1.
  - **New helpers** at module scope: `_try_print_kot(kot_doc, printer, format) -> (ok, err)` — single choke point for every print attempt; `_publish_print_failure(kot_doc, dept, printer, err)` — best-effort realtime publish so the frontend can surface a toast.
  - **`reprint_kot` ([ury/ury/api/ury_kot_reprint.py](ury/ury/api/ury_kot_reprint.py))** — same new-config-first pattern. When `custom_print_mode` is set, resolves the target printer via `_resolve_printer_for_department(profile, "Food", order_type)`. Honors takeaway route for Parcel orders. Falls back to the legacy `custom_table_order_printer` / `custom_parcel_order_printer` chain when unset.
- **getPosProfile exposure ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):** the 9 new POS Profile fields are added to the response dict so the React POS store picks them up. The frontend only reads `custom_print_mode` directly (to decide QZ vs CUPS vs Disabled on the client side); all the routing is server-side.
- **Frontend types ([pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts)):** `PosProfileLimited` + `PosProfileCombined` both gain the 9 new fields with proper union types (`'QZ Tray' | 'CUPS (Direct)' | 'Disabled'`, `'Bar' | 'Kitchen' | 'Bill'`, etc). Propagated through `getCombinedPosProfile()`. No component-level changes in Round 1 — the React POS's print call sites ([pos/src/lib/print.ts](pos/src/lib/print.ts) + [print-qz.ts](pos/src/lib/print-qz.ts)) keep their existing behavior and will be rewritten in Round 2 along with the "no new tab" flow.
- **Migration ([ury/patches/v0_2/migrate_print_config.py](ury/patches/v0_2/migrate_print_config.py)):** one-time `[post_model_sync]` patch that walks every POS Profile whose `custom_print_mode` is still unset and auto-populates the new fields from the legacy config. Rules: `qz_print=1 → custom_print_mode="QZ Tray"` else `"CUPS (Direct)"`. Bill printer = first `bill=1` row of the legacy `printer_settings` child table, else `custom_table_order_printer`. Kitchen = `custom_table_order_printer`. Parcel = `custom_parcel_order_printer`. Bar stays blank — admin picks after migrate. Routes inherit their field defaults. Idempotent (skips profiles with `custom_print_mode` already set). Guarded with `frappe.db.has_column("POS Profile", col)` so a mis-ordered patches.txt degrades to a noisy no-op instead of crashing migrate.
- **Unit tests ([ury/ury/api/test_ury_print_routing.py](ury/ury/api/test_ury_print_routing.py)) — 27 scenarios, all passing.**
  - Invoked via `bench --site <site> execute ury.ury.api.test_ury_print_routing.run_print_routing_tests` (the same "bypass bench run-tests" pattern from the shift system tests — ERPNext test bootstrap is still broken on this site, see 2026-04-15 entry).
  - **Coverage breakdown:**
    - Item classification (5 tests): food / drinks / other courses, unknown course defaults to Food, item with no course defaults to Food.
    - Bucket splitting (3 tests): single department, mixed Food + Drinks, empty KOT.
    - Printer resolution (9 tests): Drinks → Bar, Food → Kitchen, Food + Takeaway → Parcel, Parcel-empty cascades to Kitchen, Takeaway route override, Drinks with Bar empty cascades to Kitchen, Drinks with Bar AND Kitchen empty cascades to Bill, Drinks explicitly routed to Bill, Other uses Food route.
    - End-to-end plan (5 tests): unset mode → empty plan (legacy fallback), Disabled mode → empty plan, Food-only KOT → 1 plan entry, mixed KOT → 2 plan entries each with filtered items, takeaway override.
    - Fallback policy (5 tests): bill fallback returns printer + header, header format for Food, Fail Silently returns no print, Fail with Alert returns no print, bill-empty-too returns no print.
  - **Test fixtures** use `types.SimpleNamespace` (not `frappe._dict`) for the fake KOT doc because `_dict.items` shadows the built-in `dict.items()` method. Flagged in-file so whoever writes the next round's tests doesn't hit the same gotcha. Also: `URY Menu Course` autoname is `field:course`, NOT `name` — the test fixture helper sets `course=...` explicitly.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on all edited Python files (`api.py`, `ury_print.py`, `ury_kot.py`, `ury_kot_reprint.py`, `setup.py`, `hooks.py`, `migrate_print_config.py`, the test file) — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean.
  - **`bench --site local16.5 migrate`** — applied new fields + ran the migration patch cleanly.
  - **27/27 unit tests pass** in 0.5s on the real Frappe test harness.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Round 2 scope** (next round): kill the `window.open('/printview')` new-tab fallback — replace with a hidden iframe + `document.execCommand('print')` so cashiers never see a new tab or OS print dialog flash. Add the print progress overlay (blue-accent animation with per-printer status, green/red flash on success/failure). Add per-KOT print state fields (`custom_print_attempts`, per-printer status JSON) so the Orders page can show "Printed to: Bar ✓, Kitchen ✗ (retry)" with a retry button. Rewrite `select_network_printer` to read the new config and route through `resolve_kot_print_plan`. Bonus: the Client Script for POS Profile with a "Test Print" button next to the bill printer.
  - **Round 3 scope** (final): delete `urypos/` legacy + its ~9 exclusive backend methods (`getAggregatorMOP`, `getCashier`, `getInvoiceForCashier`, `getRoom`, `get_select_field_options`, `table_transfer`, `captain_transfer`, `customer_favourite_item`, `pos_opening_check`). Soft-redirect `/urypos/*` → `/pos` in hooks.py `website_route_rules`. Update `custom_html_block.json` to remove the "POS V1" link. Build artifacts at `ury/public/urypos/` + `ury/www/urypos.html` stay gitignored but need manual cleanup on deployed sites — document the `rm -rf` in the release notes.
  - **The `_ury_course_dept_cache` is per-request**, attached to `frappe.local`. One fetch per request regardless of how many KOT items get classified. No cross-request caching because course department changes need to take effect on the next KOT without a cache flush. If N+1 ever shows up in profiling, promote to a longer-lived `frappe.cache()` with a bump on URY Menu Course save.
  - **The `filtered_doc` used for department splitting is a shallow copy via `frappe.copy_doc`.** The items child rows are fresh copies; the print format renders the trimmed subset. The filter does NOT touch the real KOT in the DB — it's purely an in-memory transform for the duration of the print call.
  - **The print format currently doesn't render `doc.flags.kot_department`** — it's stamped but ignored. A Round 2 follow-up adds a header line to the KOT print format template so the receiving station sees "BAR KOT" or "KITCHEN KOT" at the top of each printout. Low-priority polish; not functionally load-bearing.
  - **The existing print format that multi_print_kot uses is read from the legacy `URY Printer Settings.custom_kot_print_format`** via a fallback query. If an admin migrates to the new config without ever having populated the legacy printer_settings table, `kot_print_format` will be `None` — Frappe's `print_by_server` tolerates None (uses the default print format), so the print still works but might look ugly. Round 2 will add a `custom_kot_print_format` field directly on POS Profile so this isn't a back-door dependency.
  - **The `custom_takeaway_kot_route`** only offers `Parcel` vs `Kitchen`. If a restaurant wants takeaway drinks to go to the Bar, they have to change `custom_drinks_kot_route` globally — the drinks route doesn't fork by order type. This was deliberate (keeps the config manageable) but if someone asks for per-order-type drinks routing, the next step is to add a `custom_takeaway_drinks_route` field and wire it into `_resolve_printer_for_department`'s Drinks branch.
  - **The `apply_print_fallback` header is currently only a plain string** stored in `doc.flags.fallback_header`. Nothing in the current KOT print format actually reads this flag — when Round 2 updates the format to render the department header, it should also check for the fallback header and render it in BIG RED LETTERS so the bill-printer-that's-now-doing-bar-duty is obvious to whoever picks up the paper.

### 2026-04-15 — Cross-midnight shift support + layout fixes + unit tests
- **Four bundled items** driven by the first production tests of the URY Shift system from 2026-04-14.
- **Item 1 — POS Opening Entry creation bypassed the shift gate.** The 2026-04-14 gate wired into `posOpening()` only fired on the "should we show the dialog?" check. The React POS's actual entry creation goes through `db.createDoc('POS Opening Entry', payload)` (Frappe's REST resource endpoint), which bypasses the whitelisted `posOpening` method entirely. Result: cashier outside their shift window saw the opening dialog appear, filled it in, and the entry was created regardless. **Fix:** added `enforce_shift_gate(doc, method)` to [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py) `validate` that runs `_enforce_shift_gate_for_open(doc.custom_terminal)` on fresh inserts. Only fires on `doc.get("__islocal")` so editing an existing entry doesn't re-fire. Uses `frappe.session.user` (the user actually creating the record), not `doc.user`, so admins creating entries for cashiers via the desk still bypass cleanly.
- **Item 2 — Frontend swallowed backend gate errors.** [POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx) `checkPOSStatus` had a catch-all that replaced any `checkPOSOpening` failure with `setValidation({type: 'opening'})` — "assume POS not opened so the user gets an actionable screen". That assumption dates from before the shift gate existed. Once the gate started throwing, the error was caught and discarded; the cashier just saw the opening dialog. **Fix:** wrapped the `checkPOSOpening` call in its own try/catch, parsed the error via `extractFrappeServerError`, and stored it as a new `gateError` state slot. New render branch shows a dedicated red card with `Clock` icon, the parsed title + message, and two buttons (Try Again, Sign Out). Falls through to the dialog only if the error state isn't set — other error branches behave as before.
- **Item 3 — ShiftHoursBanner layout distortion.**
  - **Resolver bug:** `_resolve_active_shift_for_user_ury` had three loop branches (in_window, running, upcoming) but no fourth for "ended today". At 12:00 Mon with a Mon 9:00-11:50 shift, none of the branches matched and the resolver returned `None`. `get_shift_status` reported `no_shift` → banner never saw `after_end` → no banner. **Fix:** added an `ended` candidate slot to both URY and HRMS resolvers. Priority: live > ended > upcoming. The existing `get_shift_status` `elif now_secs > end_secs: status = "after_end"` branch now actually fires.
  - **Layout bug:** `POSOpeningProvider` rendered `<ShiftHoursBanner />` as a sibling of `{children}` inside a React fragment. The children were `<div className="flex flex-col h-screen ...">` — meaning the banner sat OUTSIDE the h-screen container. Body height became `~40px + 100vh`, pushing the Footer below the visible area. Compounded by [POS.tsx](pos/src/pages/POS.tsx) and [Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) using `h-screen` (100vh) inside their `flex-1` parents — latently broken (the inner pages were always overflowing the parent's box) but hidden by the parent's `overflow-hidden` until the banner made the overflow visible. **Fix:** (a) removed the banner mount from `POSOpeningProvider`'s happy-path return; (b) added `<ShiftHoursBanner />` as the first flex child in [App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx) inside the `flex flex-col h-screen` column so it takes its natural height and the rest of the column shrinks; (c) changed [ShiftHoursBanner.tsx](pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx) classes from `sticky top-0 z-40` to `shrink-0 z-30` (no longer needs sticky inside a flex column); (d) changed [POS.tsx](pos/src/pages/POS.tsx), [Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx), [Sidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/Sidebar.tsx) `h-screen` → `h-full` so the inner pages participate in the flex sizing. All of these were pre-existing latent bugs — the banner change just exposed them.
  - **Right panel bug:** `<OrderPanel />` (POS page) and the Orders page right details panel were `position: fixed` with `h-[calc(100vh-4rem)]` — hardcoded "header is 4rem and there's nothing else above". When the banner pushed everything down by ~40px, the panels stayed anchored at viewport-top + 4rem but their height was still `100vh - 4rem`, so the bottom 40px (with the Add Order button) dropped below the viewport. **Fix:** dropped `fixed right-0 z-10 h-[calc(100vh-4rem)]` on both panels, replaced with `h-full shrink-0 z-10`. Both panels are already inside a flex row, so they become natural flex children — shrink/grow with their parent automatically, never need viewport-height math again. Dropped the `pr-96` hack on the menu/list area (was reserving 24rem on the right for the formerly-fixed panel); replaced with `min-w-0` so flex can shrink the area below content size.
- **Item 4 — Cross-midnight shift support.** The 2026-04-14 cut explicitly rejected cross-midnight shifts via `URY Shift._validate_time_window`, forcing admins to split overnight shifts into two records. User asked for proper support.
  - **[URY Shift.validate](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift/ury_shift.py)** — removed the `end_time <= start_time` rejection. Only zero-length shifts (`end == start`) are rejected now. Cross-midnight is auto-detected: any shift with `end_time < start_time` is treated as spanning midnight. No new schema field — the detection is implicit from the times.
  - **[URY Shift Assignment._validate_no_overlap](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/ury_shift_assignment.py)** — overlap check rewritten to handle cross-day. New helper `_compute_occupancy_ranges(shift_doc, days_of_week_rows)` expands each shift into a list of `(weekday_index, start_sec, end_sec)` occupancy ranges. Non-crossing shifts produce ONE range per assigned weekday; cross-midnight shifts produce TWO ranges per assigned weekday — one on the start day (start_sec → 86400) and one on the next weekday mod 7 (0 → end_sec). The overlap validator then does pairwise intersection on shared weekday indexes. So a Monday assignment of Shift A (18:00-06:00) correctly conflicts with a Tuesday assignment of Shift B (03:00-11:00) on the shared weekday 1 range `(1, 0-21600)` ∩ `(1, 10800-39600)`.
  - **[api.py `_resolve_active_shift_for_user_ury`](ury/ury_pos/api.py)** — full rewrite. Switched from "seconds-since-midnight today" to actual `datetime.datetime` objects anchored to specific days. The resolver now considers TWO candidate days per lookup: TODAY (for shifts that start today) AND YESTERDAY (for cross-midnight shifts that started yesterday and may still be running into today). For each candidate it builds `_start_dt` / `_end_dt` / `_open_window_*_dt` / `_late_close_dt` datetime objects — for a cross-midnight shift, `end_dt` is naturally on the following calendar day. The dispatcher picks the best candidate (in_window → return immediately, else priority live > ended > upcoming). Public fields `start_seconds` / `end_seconds` stay as raw time-of-day (mod 86400) so the banner can display "Ended at 06:00" correctly. Internal `_start_dt` fields are used by `get_shift_status` and `_enforce_shift_gate_for_open` for the actual time comparisons.
  - **[api.py `_resolve_active_shift_for_user_hrms`](ury/ury_pos/api.py)** — same datetime rewrite + yesterday lookup. HRMS Shift Types with `end < start` now work through the same cross-midnight path.
  - **[api.py `get_shift_status`](ury/ury_pos/api.py) + `_enforce_shift_gate_for_open`** — both switched from `now_secs` vs `open_window_start_seconds` comparison to `now` vs `_start_dt` / `_end_dt` / `_open_window_*_dt`. Response shape to the frontend is unchanged (still has `start_time`, `end_time`, `status`, `reason`, etc).
  - **Testing hook:** added `_now_dt=None` optional parameter to `_resolve_active_shift_for_user_ury`. When passed, the resolver derives both "today" AND "now" from it, bypassing `frappe.utils.getdate()` and `_now_local()`. Lets unit tests freeze time without monkey-patching.
- **Item 5 — Unit tests for cross-midnight.** Per the new CLAUDE.md rule 6 (write unit tests for non-trivial features), added [ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/test_ury_shift_assignment.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/test_ury_shift_assignment.py) — 24 scenarios covering URY Shift validate, `_compute_occupancy_ranges`, URY Shift Assignment overlap validation, and the resolver at multiple time points across a cross-midnight shift (before open window, in open window, running before midnight, running after midnight, after end, next-day-far-after, etc).
  - **Test runner workaround:** `bench run-tests --app ury` is blocked by two pre-existing ERPNext bootstrap issues — URY's `ury_customer.before_insert` hook requires a mobile number which ERPNext's test-fixture Customer creation doesn't supply, AND ERPNext tries to create a "Standard Buying" Price List that already exists on this site (DuplicateEntryError). Rather than untangling ERPNext's bootstrap, I added a `run_cross_day_tests()` wrapper at the bottom of the test file that calls `unittest.TextTestRunner(verbosity=2)` directly, bypassing Frappe's test discovery. Invoked via `bench --site <site> execute ury.ury.doctype.ury_shift_assignment.test_ury_shift_assignment.run_cross_day_tests`. Future URY test files should follow the same pattern until someone untangles the ERPNext bootstrap.
  - **Side fix:** added `frappe.flags.in_test` bypass in [ury/ury/hooks/ury_customer.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_customer.py) `validate_mobile_number` so ERPNext's test bootstrap can create Customers. The production path (React POS customer creation) always supplies a mobile number, so the bypass is safe.
  - **Side fix:** removed stale `reqd: 1` on `URY Shift Assignment.days_of_week`. The field description and resolver both treated "empty days_of_week = every day of the week" as valid, but the schema still had the reqd flag, so saving an assignment without days_of_week threw a MandatoryError. Now matches design intent.
  - **Test coverage breakdown** (24 scenarios): URY Shift.validate (2) · occupancy ranges (5) · overlap validation (5, including cross-midnight vs cross-midnight on same start day, cross-midnight vs next-day morning, same-day non-overlap OK, etc) · resolver (12, including before-window upcoming, in-window immediate return, running before/after midnight, ended, far-after-end returning None, non-crossing yesterday NOT considered, live > ended priority, every-day-cross-midnight, tolerance extension).
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on every edited Python file — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 731.35 → 732.50 kB.
  - **`bench --site local16.5 execute ury.ury.doctype.ury_shift_assignment.test_ury_shift_assignment.run_cross_day_tests` — all 24 tests pass in 1.214s.** 0 failures, 0 errors.
  - User confirmed the banner layout + right panel distortion fixes work manually after the rebuild. Cross-midnight support was validated by the test suite rather than manual browser testing.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Lesson: write unit tests up front.** The cross-midnight logic has too many time-based edge cases to rely on manual testing — 24 scenarios, each at a specific minute of a specific day, most of them would be annoying to reproduce in the browser. The test suite validated all of them in ~1 second. CLAUDE.md rule 6 now codifies this as a general expectation for non-trivial features.
  - **`bench run-tests --app ury` is broken** on sites with existing ERPNext data. The `run_*_tests()` direct-unittest wrapper pattern (see the test file) is the current workaround. Someone eventually needs to: (a) figure out whether ERPNext's test bootstrap really needs to create a Standard Buying Price List when one already exists, and (b) make the customer mobile_number hook tolerate empty values during test bootstrap. Both are non-URY fixes. Until then, every URY test file should ship with its own `run_*_tests()` wrapper.
  - **Shifts that cross a day boundary twice (e.g. 48-hour shifts)** are not supported. A shift's start_time and end_time are both time-of-day values; the resolver assumes the shift ends at most 24 hours after start. A multi-day shift would need a different data model. Not in scope.
  - **DST transitions are not handled specially.** The resolver uses naive `datetime` objects anchored to dates from `frappe.utils.getdate()`. During a DST transition, a shift that normally runs 18:00-06:00 might run 11 or 13 hours instead of 12 — but the resolver still treats `end_dt` as the fixed `06:00 next day` regardless of DST. For restaurants in tz-unaware regions or with DST-insensitive operations, this is fine. Cross-DST shift scheduling is deferred.
  - **Yesterday is always consulted, not two-days-ago.** If a weird 30-hour shift ever exists, yesterday's assignment wouldn't cover it. The resolver only looks at today's + yesterday's weekday assignments. Documented; revisit if someone needs 24h+ shifts.
  - **The test user `cross-day-cashier@ury-test.local` and branch `_URY Cross Day Test Branch`** are created once in `setUpClass` and left on the site (not torn down). This is intentional — they're inert fixtures that don't affect anything else. If you want to clean them up, delete via the desk: `/app/user/cross-day-cashier@ury-test.local` and `/app/branch/_URY Cross Day Test Branch`.

### 2026-04-14 — Collapsible drafts list + URY Cashier desk lockdown + URY Shift system (schema + backend + frontend)
- **Three bundled features.** All three drove off the same conversation: (a) the Close Shift dialog's "drafts blocking close" table got crowded when many drafts existed, (b) URY Cashier-only users were able to wander into the Frappe desk and see things they shouldn't, (c) the per-POS-Profile `custom_shift_hours` was a clumsy proxy for "when can the cashier open and when should they close" — managers wanted to set actual shifts and have the system gate POS opening + close-reminder against them.
- **Feature 1 — Collapsible drafts ([pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx)):**
  - New `useState` `draftsExpanded` (default `false`). The orange "N unpaid orders blocking close" header becomes a clickable button with a chevron icon (`ChevronRight` collapsed → `ChevronDown` expanded). Body of the alert always shows count + total + "Show details" link; the table only renders when expanded.
  - **Transfer dropdown stays visible at all times** — a cashier can pick a target without expanding. Expansion is only needed if they want to verify the row list.
  - Expanded table now allows max-height 220px (was 140px) and gets a `sticky top-0` thead so headers don't scroll out on long lists.
- **Feature 2 — URY Cashier desk lockdown:**
  - **New JS asset** [ury/public/js/cashier_desk_redirect.js](ury/public/js/cashier_desk_redirect.js): tiny script wired via `app_include_js` in [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py). Loaded on every desk page. Reads `frappe.user.has_role(...)` to detect a URY Cashier user with no elevated role (Administrator / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain) and immediately calls `window.location.replace('/pos')`. The replace (not assign) keeps the desk out of browser history.
  - **First attempt used `User.home_page`** for the smoother server-side post-login redirect path (a User.validate hook + a backfill patch). On modern Frappe (v15+ in our `fb-dev16-5` bench), `User.home_page` was removed from the doctype — `bench migrate` crashed with `Unknown column 'home_page' in 'SELECT'` when the patch ran. Frappe replaced it with `default_app` (a Select field that requires registering URY POS as a Frappe app via `add_to_apps_screen` to populate the options). That's more invasive than warranted, so the entire server-side setter was scrapped: the User.validate hook, the backfill patch, and the `User` entry in `doc_events` were all removed. The JS-only approach is the sole mechanism now.
  - **Trade-off:** there's a brief flash of the desk on direct `/app` access AND on the post-login redirect (Frappe's default lands on /app, then the JS fires). About half a second on a normal connection. Acceptable for the threat model — cashiers are still kept out, just with a flash on the way.
  - **Bypass behavior:** anyone with Administrator / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain skips the redirect entirely. Captains specifically can still hit the desk to manage shifts and other URY admin doctypes.
- **Feature 3 — URY Shift system (the big one):**
  - **Three new DocTypes** under `ury/ury/doctype/`:
    - **`URY Shift`** ([ury_shift.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift/ury_shift.json) + [ury_shift.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift/ury_shift.py)): template with `shift_name` (autoname `field:shift_name`), `branch` (Link → Branch, required), `start_time` / `end_time` (Time, required), three tolerance Ints (`tolerance_minutes_before` default 15, `tolerance_minutes_after_start` default 30, `tolerance_minutes_after_end` default 60), `description`, `disabled`. The `validate` method rejects `end_time <= start_time` to keep overnight-crossing shifts out of scope (they'd need a `crosses_midnight` flag and more careful weekday math; documented in code).
    - **`URY Shift Day`** ([ury_shift_day.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_day/ury_shift_day.json) + `.py`): tiny `istable=1` child with one `day` Select field (Monday\nTuesday\n…\nSunday). Used by URY Shift Assignment as the weekday pattern.
    - **`URY Shift Assignment`** ([ury_shift_assignment.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/ury_shift_assignment.json) + [ury_shift_assignment.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_shift_assignment/ury_shift_assignment.py)): naming `format:SHIFT-ASSIGN-{YYYY}-{####}`. Fields: `user` (Link → User), `shift` (Link → URY Shift), `branch` (fetch from `shift.branch`), `status` (Active/Inactive default Active), `effective_from`/`effective_to` Dates, `days_of_week` Table → URY Shift Day, `notes`. Empty `days_of_week` means "every day" (documented in description). Validate hook does three things:
      1. **`_validate_user_role`**: rejects assignments to users without any URY cashier role (URY Cashier / URY Captain / URY Manager) — meaningless otherwise.
      2. **`_validate_effective_dates`**: `effective_to >= effective_from`.
      3. **`_validate_no_overlap`**: walks all OTHER Active assignments for the same user, computes effective-date overlap, weekday overlap (with empty=every-day handling), AND shift time-window overlap, and throws with a clean error message naming the conflicting assignment + shift if all three intersect. Two non-overlapping shifts on the same user (e.g. Morning 06-14 + Evening 16-22) are explicitly allowed.
  - **POS Profile schema** ([custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures, all three per the dual-source-of-truth rule):
    - **New `custom_shift_system_mode`** (Select, default `Disabled`, options `Disabled\nURY Shift\nHRMS Shift Type`): the master switch. "Disabled" falls back to the existing `custom_shift_hours` legacy reminder. "URY Shift" uses URY Shift Assignment + URY Shift. "HRMS Shift Type" uses ERPNext HRMS Shift Type + Shift Assignment via the Employee linked to the user.
    - **`custom_shift_hours`** depends_on changed to `eval:!doc.custom_shift_system_mode || doc.custom_shift_system_mode == 'Disabled'` — hidden when shift system is on so admins can't accidentally double-configure.
    - **`custom_block_orders_after_shift_end`** depends_on changed to require BOTH `custom_shift_hours > 0` AND shift system mode = Disabled. The shift system intentionally does NOT support hard-blocking new orders at end-time (per user choice — soft reminder only, option (a) from the design questions).
  - **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py))** — new section "URY Shift system" (~330 lines added):
    - **`_shift_system_mode(pos_profile_name)`** — defensive read of `custom_shift_system_mode`, defaults to "Disabled".
    - **`_is_shift_admin(user)`** — Administrator + System Manager always bypass the gate. Per user requirement #8.
    - **`_now_local()`**, **`_seconds_in_window()`**, **`_time_to_seconds()`**, **`_seconds_to_hhmm()`** — small datetime/time helpers. The `_time_to_seconds` helper handles `datetime.time`, `timedelta`, AND ISO time strings because Frappe returns Time fields as different Python types depending on context.
    - **`_resolve_active_shift_for_user_ury(user, branch)`** — URY backend resolver. Pulls all Active URY Shift Assignments matching today's effective date range, filters by branch (when supplied), then for each candidate checks weekday membership in the `days_of_week` child table (empty = every day). For each match, loads the URY Shift template, computes the open window (`start - tolerance_before`, `start + tolerance_after_start`), and dispatches to one of three states: live (in open window — return immediately), running (started but past open window), or upcoming (still in the future). Returns the first live result or falls back to live/upcoming. Returns None if no assignment matches today.
    - **`_resolve_active_shift_for_user_hrms(user, branch)`** — HRMS backend resolver. Defensive: if `Shift Assignment` or `Shift Type` doctypes don't exist (hrms not installed), returns None. Looks up the Employee record linked to this user via `Employee.user_id`, then queries Active submitted Shift Assignments for that employee with today's date in range. For each, loads the Shift Type and applies the same dispatch logic. Maps HRMS field names to URY's tolerance shape (`begin_check_in_before_shift_start_time` → `tolerance_minutes_before`, `allow_check_out_after_shift_end_time` → `tolerance_minutes_after_end`, hardcodes a 30-min open window after start since HRMS doesn't model that).
    - **`_resolve_active_shift_for_user(user, pos_profile_name, branch)`** — top-level dispatcher. Picks the URY or HRMS path based on the profile's `custom_shift_system_mode`.
    - **`get_shift_status(terminal=None)`** — new whitelisted endpoint. Returns a dict the React POS uses to drive the banner: `{mode, bypass, has_shift, shift_name, branch, start_time, end_time, now, can_open, status, reason, tolerance_*}`. The `status` field is one of `disabled | bypass | no_shift | before_window | in_window | running | after_end | outside`. The banner renders only when `status === 'after_end'`. The opening dialog uses `can_open` to decide whether to show the open form.
    - **`_enforce_shift_gate_for_open(terminal)`** — server-side guard called from `posOpening`. For non-admin users with a non-Disabled shift mode, resolves the user's shift and throws a clean `frappe.throw` with a clear "Outside Shift Window" or "No Shift Assigned" error if they can't open right now. The error has a structured title so the React POS catches it via `extractFrappeServerError` and shows it in the existing opening dialog error branch.
    - **`posOpening(terminal=None)`** — augmented to call `_enforce_shift_gate_for_open` after computing `needs_open == 1` but before returning. Means: cashiers see the shift gate error the moment they hit the POS if they're outside their assigned window.
    - **`getPosProfile()`** — now exposes `custom_shift_system_mode` in the response so the frontend can pick the right banner mode without an extra round-trip.
  - **Frontend:**
    - **[pos/src/lib/shift-api.ts](pos/src/lib/shift-api.ts) — new file** with `getShiftStatus(terminal)` helper + `ShiftStatus` / `ShiftSystemMode` / `ShiftGateStatus` types matching the backend response shape exactly.
    - **[pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts)** — `PosProfileLimited` and `PosProfileCombined` gain `custom_shift_system_mode`. Propagated through the merge.
    - **[pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx](pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx)** — full rewrite. Now branches on `posProfile.custom_shift_system_mode`:
      - **Legacy mode (Disabled):** unchanged. Polls `getCurrentPosOpenEntry`, computes elapsed hours from `period_start_date`, fires when `elapsed >= shift_hours`.
      - **Shift system mode (URY Shift / HRMS Shift Type):** polls `getShiftStatus(terminal)` once a minute. Banner fires when `status === 'after_end'`. Title shows the assigned shift name (e.g. `Shift "Morning" is over.`) and detail shows `Ended at HH:MM. Time to close the POS.`. The hard-block flag is NOT used in this mode — soft reminder only, matching the user's design choice. Errors from `getShiftStatus` are caught + logged + the banner state cleared (don't break the POS if the endpoint hiccups).
      - Both modes share the same Close Shift button + dismiss button + POSClosingDialog mount.
  - **Permissions** ([ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py)): `URY Shift` + `URY Shift Assignment` added to `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` so all URY roles (Cashier, Captain, Manager) can read them. Captain + Manager additionally get `read+write+create+select+report` via `CAPTAIN_EXTRA_WRITE_DOCTYPES`. Cashiers stay read-only — they can see their own assigned shift in the React POS banner, but can't create or edit shift records. The next `bench migrate` runs `ensure_role_permissions()` which idempotently adds the Custom DocPerm rows.
  - **Translations** ([ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv)): added `URY Shift → ExPOS Shift`, `URY Shift Assignment → ExPOS Shift Assignment`, `URY Shift Day → ExPOS Shift Day` so the desk shows the rebranded names.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on every edited Python file (`api.py`, `hooks.py`, `setup.py`, `permissions.py`, the three new doctype `.py` files, the new patch, the new `ury_user.py` hook) — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` + the three new doctype JSONs — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 729.70 → 731.35 kB (1.6 kB for the new `shift-api.ts` lib + the `ShiftHoursBanner` branching).
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (creates the three new doctypes via Frappe's DocType sync, the new POS Profile field via fixtures + setup.py, and the new permissions baseline rows; runs the cashier home_page backfill patch). Then walks the matrix:
    - **Collapsible drafts:** open a shift with several unpaid drafts → End Shift → drafts banner is collapsed by default with "Show details" → click → table appears → click again → table collapses → transfer dropdown stays visible throughout.
    - **Cashier desk lockdown:** create a fresh user with ONLY URY Cashier role → log in as that user → expect to land on `/pos` (not `/app`) → in another tab type `/app` directly → expect to flash and immediately redirect to `/pos`. Switch to a captain / manager / admin → desk loads normally.
    - **Shift system off (default):** existing flow unchanged. POS Profile shows Shift Length (Hours) field as before. Banner uses the legacy elapsed-hours math.
    - **Shift system on (URY Shift):** flip POS Profile `custom_shift_system_mode` → "URY Shift" → confirm Shift Length (Hours) field hides. In the desk, create a `URY Shift` (e.g. Morning, 06:00-14:00, branch X) → create a `URY Shift Assignment` linking your test cashier to that shift starting today, with Monday-Friday in `days_of_week`. Log in as the cashier:
      - Before 05:45 (15 min before open) → `posOpening` throws "Outside Shift Window" with the friendly start_time message.
      - Between 05:45 and 06:30 (open window = start - 15 min, start + 30 min after start) → POS opening dialog appears, can submit cleanly.
      - At 14:01 (1 minute after end) → the orange shift banner shows "Shift Morning is over. Ended at 14:00. Time to close the POS." with a Close Shift button.
      - Click Close Shift → POSClosingDialog opens → submits cleanly.
    - **Shift assignment overlap guard:** create a second URY Shift Assignment for the same cashier overlapping Morning's time window → save → expect "Shift Overlap" error naming the conflicting assignment.
    - **Shift assignment non-overlap allowed:** create a second assignment for the same cashier with an Evening shift (16:00-22:00) on the same days → save → expect success.
    - **Shift system on (HRMS):** if hrms is installed, set mode to "HRMS Shift Type" → create an Employee linked to the cashier user, assign a Shift Type via the standard HRMS flow → repeat the gating tests. If hrms is NOT installed, the resolver returns None and the cashier sees "No Shift Assigned" — admin can either install hrms or switch back to URY Shift.
    - **Admin bypass:** as Administrator, no shift gate ever fires regardless of mode. As System Manager (without URY roles), same.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The shift system is recurring-weekly only.** Per-date one-off overrides (a cashier swapping a shift just for one day) are NOT supported in this round. The design accommodates them later: add a `URY Shift Override` doctype that points at a specific date and takes precedence over the weekly assignment for that day. Punted because it adds another resolver branch and the user's restaurants don't need it yet.
  - **Overnight shifts that cross midnight are explicitly unsupported.** The URY Shift `validate` hook rejects `end_time <= start_time`. If a restaurant needs a 22:00-04:00 evening shift, the workaround is to split it into two records (Evening 22:00-23:59 + Late 00:00-04:00) and assign both to the cashier. A future round could add a `crosses_midnight` flag and handle the seconds-since-midnight rollover in the resolver — left as a TODO when someone actually needs it.
  - **HRMS path is best-effort.** It maps the most common HRMS Shift Type fields (`begin_check_in_before_shift_start_time` → before-tolerance, `allow_check_out_after_shift_end_time` → after-end-tolerance) and hardcodes a 30-min after-start window since HRMS doesn't model that explicitly. If a site's HRMS workflow uses Shift Request / Shift Assignment Tool features, those should still work — URY only reads the Active Shift Assignment row, not the workflow that produced it.
  - **Tolerance defaults are conservative.** 15 min before / 30 min after start / 60 min after end. Restaurants vary wildly here — a fast-casual chain might want zero tolerance and a fine-dining house might want an hour-long pre-open window. The defaults are configurable per shift, so admins can tune them per template without touching code.
  - **The shift gate fires at `posOpening` time, not at every page load.** This means a cashier who's already past the open window but already opened the shift doesn't keep getting blocked — they ring orders normally, see the close-shift banner when end_time hits, and close at their leisure. The next morning, when they try to open a new shift, the gate fires again.
  - **The cashier desk lockdown JS has a brief flash** (the desk loads, then the JS replaces the location). This is unavoidable without a full server-side request hook. The User.home_page setter handles the smooth case (login flow), and the JS catches the edge case (bookmarked /app). If the flash is annoying enough, a future round can add a real `before_request` middleware that returns a 302 for cashier users hitting /app — but it requires a Werkzeug response object and is invasive enough to skip unless asked.
  - **The collapsible drafts pattern** can be reused on the iHotel folio side (when a charged invoice has many items) and the Returns dialog (long item lists). Worth pulling out into a shared `<CollapsibleSection>` component if a third use case shows up.
  - **Why did I not gate the close shift on shift end_time?** Because the user explicitly chose option (a) — soft reminder, no hard block. The cashier may be mid-payment when end_time fires; a hard close would be hostile. The banner is a reminder, the cashier closes when they're ready.
  - **The validate hook on URY Shift Assignment trusts `frappe.get_roles()`** which includes inherited roles via Role Profile. So a user assigned to a Role Profile that grants URY Cashier indirectly will pass the role check. Worth noting if anyone debugs "why does this assignment validate when the user doesn't have the role directly."

### 2026-04-13 — iHotel integration: charge POS orders to a guest's room (schema + backend + full frontend)
- **Feature:** a separate Frappe app `iHotel` is installed on the same bench to manage rooms, guests, and folios. Cashiers now have a first-class path to charge a POS order to a hotel guest's open folio — the order is tagged against `(Customer → Guest → iHotel Profile.status=Open)`, a `Folio Charge` child row is appended on the profile with the full order total, and the POS Invoice stays in `docstatus=0` forever (a "charged draft"). iHotel's own checkout flow will post the real GL entries when the guest settles the folio — URY deliberately does not double-book the accounting.
- **Design decisions (pre-agreed with user):**
  - Master switch on POS Profile (`custom_ihotel_enabled`), not on POS Settings — different branches can opt in independently.
  - A mandatory "default charge type" (Link → iHotel `Charge Type`) lives on the profile too; required when the toggle is on, used as the `Folio Charge.charge_type` on every row the POS writes.
  - **Unified customer picker with Walk-in / Hotel Guest tabs.** The hotel flow re-uses the existing debounced customer search; after the cashier picks a customer, a separate panel appears listing only the rooms that customer has open (via `Customer → Guest.customer → iHotel Profile.guest + status=Open`). No room-not-found guessing — the dropdown IS the validation.
  - **Take Away + Room is explicitly supported.** Dine-in and take-away orders both go through the same flow.
  - **Payment dialog gains a third tab** — "Charge to Room" — alongside the existing Single Payer and Split Bill tabs. Only visible when the draft has a hotel room tagged; clicking it posts the folio charge and marks the invoice as a charged draft.
  - **"Charged draft" pattern** (this was the key tradeoff). An earlier sketch considered creating a separate "iHotel POS Invoice" doctype to avoid the GL posting entirely. I pushed back — instead, the POS Invoice stays as a regular POS Invoice but its `docstatus` never leaves 0. The invoice has `custom_charge_to_room=1` + `custom_hotel_room` + `custom_ihotel_profile` stamped so the Orders page can show it under a new "Room Charges" filter and iHotel's folio has the line item. When the guest settles at checkout, iHotel's own flow does the accounting — URY just authoritatively says "this happened" via the Folio Charge row.
- **Schema ([ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures, all three per the dual-source-of-truth rule):**
  - POS Profile: `custom_ihotel_section` (Section Break, collapsible), `custom_ihotel_enabled` (Check, default 0), `custom_ihotel_charge_type` (Link → `Charge Type`, `mandatory_depends_on: eval:doc.custom_ihotel_enabled`).
  - POS Invoice: `custom_charge_to_room` (Check, `in_standard_filter`, read-only), `custom_hotel_room` (Link → `Room`, `depends_on: eval:doc.custom_charge_to_room`), `custom_ihotel_profile` (Link → `iHotel Profile`, same depends_on).
  - The POS Invoice fields sit right after `custom_merged_into` in `setup.py`'s existing POS Invoice section — initial attempt created a DUPLICATE `"POS Invoice":` dict key in `get_custom_fields()`, Python happily returned only the last one, and migrate would have silently skipped the three fields. Caught before ship. Always grep for the target doctype key in `setup.py` before adding new fields.
  - [ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv) unchanged — the new fields don't reference any URY-owned DocType in their user-visible strings.
- **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - **`_ihotel_enabled(pos_profile_name)`** — helper that reads the Check field defensively. Defaults to False when the profile doesn't have the field yet (fresh migrate case).
  - **`get_guest_rooms_for_customer(customer)`** whitelisted — returns the list of Open iHotel Profiles for guests linked to this customer. One SQL query joining `Guest.customer` → `iHotel Profile.guest` with `status='Open'`. Returns `[{profile, room, guest, guest_name, check_in_date, check_out_date}]`. Empty list means no current stays — the picker shows a friendly "no open rooms" message.
  - **`validate_hotel_room_for_customer(customer, hotel_room)`** whitelisted — idempotent re-check called at charge time. Throws a ValidationError when the room has no open stay or the guest on that room is linked to a different customer. The frontend's filtered dropdown already prevents the "pick a wrong room" case; this is the server-side safety net.
  - **`charge_invoice_to_room(invoice, hotel_room)`** whitelisted — the big one. Validates invoice is `docstatus=0`, not already charged (`custom_charge_to_room != 1`), and the profile has iHotel enabled. Reads `custom_ihotel_charge_type` from the profile and throws "Charge Type Not Set" if missing. Re-validates `(customer, hotel_room)` via the helper above. Builds a description string `"POS <invoice_name>: 2x Item, 1x Item"` from the invoice items. Appends a row to the `iHotel Profile.charges` child table with `charge_date=nowdate`, `charge_type` (from profile), `description`, `quantity=1`, `rate=amount=invoice.grand_total`, `reference_doctype="POS Invoice"`, `reference_name=invoice.name`. Then stamps the invoice via `frappe.db.set_value` (bypasses the full validate cycle, just flips the flags): `custom_charge_to_room=1`, `custom_hotel_room`, `custom_ihotel_profile`, `invoice_printed=1` (cashier doesn't need to print to pay because there's nothing to collect), `custom_order_status='Room Charged'`. Frees the restaurant_table and calls `auto_unmerge_table_if_active` so merged tables come apart cleanly. Returns `{invoice, ihotel_profile, folio_charge_row, amount}`. The charged draft lingers on docstatus=0 — iHotel owns the accounting from here.
  - **`getPosProfile()`** — now exposes `custom_ihotel_enabled` and `custom_ihotel_charge_type` in the response so the frontend's role helpers and CustomerSelect have one source of truth.
  - **`getPosInvoice()` / `searchPosInvoice()`** — `status_map` gains a new **"Room Charges"** bucket with the filter `("Draft", "AND pi.custom_charge_to_room = 1")`. The existing **Draft** and **Unbilled** buckets now explicitly exclude charged drafts via `AND (pi.custom_charge_to_room IS NULL OR pi.custom_charge_to_room = 0)` so cashiers don't see them twice. `searchPosInvoice` mirrors the same filter for search-typed queries. SELECT list on both queries gains `pi.custom_charge_to_room, pi.custom_hotel_room, pi.custom_ihotel_profile` so the list cards can render the "Room N" badge without an extra round-trip.
  - **`_get_shift_invoice_breakdown()`** (feeds Close Shift preview) — draft query now also excludes charged drafts so they don't block the close with a "Transfer Required" error. Rationale: a charged draft isn't a real unpaid draft — there's nothing to collect, the money moves through the guest's folio. It'll linger on docstatus=0 past the close and that's fine.
- **Backend (sync_order — [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py)):**
  - New optional `hotel_room=None` kwarg on `sync_order()`. When set, stamps `invoice.custom_hotel_room = hotel_room or None` on the draft after `invoice.restaurant_table`. **This is intent-only, not the actual charge.** The cashier picks a room in CustomerSelect → gets tagged on the draft → survives page reloads via the rehydration path in `loadTableOrder`. The real charge (Folio Charge row + `custom_charge_to_room=1`) only happens when the cashier clicks "Charge Room X" in the Payment dialog.
- **Frontend:**
  - **[pos/src/lib/ihotel-api.ts](pos/src/lib/ihotel-api.ts) — NEW** — thin SDK wrapper with `GuestRoomOption`, `RoomValidation`, `ChargeToRoomResult` types and `getGuestRoomsForCustomer`, `validateHotelRoomForCustomer`, `chargeInvoiceToRoom` helpers. File docstring explains the three-way flow (find rooms → validate → charge).
  - **[pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts)** — `PosProfileLimited` and `PosProfileCombined` types gain `custom_ihotel_enabled` + `custom_ihotel_charge_type`. `getCombinedPosProfile()` propagates both through the limited → full merge.
  - **[pos/src/lib/order-api.ts](pos/src/lib/order-api.ts)** — `POSInvoice` type (used by `get_order_invoice`) gains `custom_charge_to_room`, `custom_hotel_room`, `custom_ihotel_profile`. `SyncOrderRequest` gains optional `hotel_room`.
  - **[pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts)** + **[pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts)** — `POSInvoice` on both sides gains the three custom fields so the Orders page cards/rows/right-panel can render the Room Charged badge and the Payment Dialog can read the hotel room off the selected order.
  - **[pos/src/store/pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts)** — two new state fields: `hotelRoom: string | null` (the selected iHotel `Room` name) + `ihotelProfile: string | null` (the matching open profile). New action `setHotelRoom(room, profile)`. **`setSelectedCustomer`** now clears `hotelRoom`/`ihotelProfile` whenever the customer changes — a room is only valid for the customer it was resolved against, so any customer change invalidates the link. `loadTableOrder` **rehydrates** both fields from `order.custom_hotel_room` / `order.custom_ihotel_profile` so reopening a charged draft re-enters hotel mode with the right room pre-filled. `clearTableOrder` and `resetOrderState` both clear the pair.
  - **[pos/src/data/order-types.ts](pos/src/data/order-types.ts)** — `OrderStatusType` gains `"Room Charges"`. New `ROOM_CHARGES_STATUS_TYPE` array. `getOrderStatusTypes()` now takes an optional `ihotelEnabled` arg and conditionally includes the Room Charges filter when iHotel is on for the profile.
  - **[pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx)** — passes `posProfile?.custom_ihotel_enabled` into `getOrderStatusTypes` so the sidebar shows "Room Charges" only when the feature is enabled.
  - **[pos/src/components/CustomerSelect.tsx](pos/src/components/CustomerSelect.tsx)** — the big UX piece:
    - New **Walk-in / Hotel Guest** segmented toggle at the top, only rendered when `ihotelEnabled && !isUpdatingOrder` (once an order has items on it, the cashier can't flip the mode mid-flight — matches the feel of the selectedOrderType guard).
    - New local `pendingCustomer` state — in hotel mode, picking a customer from the search dropdown stores them as pending (NOT in the store) so the rooms effect can fire. Only a committed room selection sends `(customer, room, profile)` to the store.
    - New effect fetches `getGuestRoomsForCustomer(targetCustomer.id)` whenever the mode + pending/selected customer changes. Empty list = "no open hotel stays" friendly error; network error = raw error message.
    - New `awaitingRoomPick` render branch shows the customer name + an orange room list. Each room is a button with the room number, guest name, and check-in date. Clicking commits `setSelectedCustomer + setHotelRoom` atomically.
    - New `committedHotelSelection` render branch replaces the blue walk-in card with an amber "Customer + Room X" card + a Change button that fully resets (clears customer, room, pending, rooms cache).
    - "Add New Customer" is hidden in hotel mode — brand-new customers by definition don't have an open iHotel Profile linked. They need to check in first (via the iHotel desk) before the POS can charge to their room.
    - Mode reacts to store state via a `useEffect` — if `loadTableOrder` rehydrates a charged draft, the picker automatically enters hotel mode with the right room pre-selected on reload.
  - **[pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx)** — pulls `hotelRoom` from the store and passes it through as `hotel_room: hotelRoom || undefined` in the `syncOrder` payload. One-liner change; persistence is all backend.
  - **[pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx)** — third tab + routing:
    - New `paymentMode: 'single' | 'split' | 'room'` state replacing the old boolean `splitEnabled`. `splitEnabled` is now a derived const (`paymentMode === 'split'`) so all the existing split-bill render branches still work without rewrites.
    - New `canChargeToRoom` memo: `ihotelEnabled && hotelRoom` — the Room tab is hidden entirely when the draft has no room tagged.
    - When `canChargeToRoom`, the old 2-way Split toggle is replaced with a **3-way segmented control** (Single / Split / Room) with three icons. When off, the old 2-way toggle stays in place so non-hotel flows are unchanged.
    - New **Charge to Room panel** renders when `paymentMode === 'room'`: a big amber card with `BedDouble` icon, the room name, guest label (passed as `hotelRoomLabel` prop, defaulted to customer_name from the selected order), the full grand total, and plain-text explanation of the "charged draft" pattern so the cashier knows what they're about to do. The discount section and single/split input grids are hidden in room mode.
    - `handlePayment` branches on `paymentMode === 'room'` — calls `chargeInvoiceToRoom(invoice, hotelRoom)` instead of `make_invoice`, shows a tailored success toast, and closes the dialog + refreshes the Orders list. Errors still flow through `extractFrappeServerError`.
    - Pay button label switches to `"Charge $X to Room Y"` and the button turns amber in room mode.
    - `canSubmit` allows submit in room mode unconditionally (the backend validator is the safety net; there's no balance math to wait for).
    - `setSplitEnabled(enabled)` is now a no-op wrapper that maps to `setPaymentMode(enabled ? 'split' : 'single')` so the existing `splitEnabled && payers.length === 0` seed effect still runs correctly.
  - **[pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx)** — three changes:
    - List view, card view, and right-panel header each get a new **"Room N"** Badge (amber, `BedDouble` icon) rendered when `order.custom_charge_to_room === 1`. Title attribute shows the full "Charged to Room X" string on hover.
    - PaymentDialog mount point passes through `hotelRoom={selectedOrder.custom_hotel_room || null}` and `hotelRoomLabel={selectedOrder.customer_name || null}` so the dialog knows whether to offer the Room tab.
    - **Payment button is hidden entirely on charged drafts** (`selectedOrder.custom_charge_to_room !== 1`) so a cashier can't accidentally try to pay an already-charged draft twice. Print button is unchanged because cashiers still need to hand the guest a receipt for the room charge.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `ury_pos/api.py`, `hooks.py`, `setup.py`, `ury_order.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 720.21 → 729.70 kB (about 9 kB for new CustomerSelect branches, Payment room panel, and the order-type additions).
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (creates the six new custom fields via fixtures + setup.py), confirms the iHotel app is installed on the same bench, and walks the matrix:
    - **Feature off (default):** POS Profile `custom_ihotel_enabled` unset → no mode toggle in CustomerSelect → PaymentDialog shows 2-way toggle → Orders sidebar has no "Room Charges" filter. Nothing about the POS feels different.
    - **Feature on, no hotel customer:** enable toggle + set Charge Type on the profile → CustomerSelect has the Walk-in / Hotel Guest toggle → pick Hotel Guest + search a regular Cash Customer → "no open hotel stays" message → toggle back to Walk-in → order works normally.
    - **Feature on, hotel customer:** create (via iHotel desk) a Guest linked to a Customer + an open iHotel Profile for that guest on a specific Room → in the POS, pick Hotel Guest → search that customer → pick → room list shows that one room → pick → amber "Customer + Room X" card shows → order items → Submit → reopen the draft in the Orders page → refresh, list row shows (nothing yet because custom_charge_to_room is still 0) → open in right panel → Payment → PaymentDialog shows 3-way toggle → **Room tab is pre-selected** → click "Charge $X to Room Y" → success toast → Orders page refreshes → switch sidebar to **Room Charges** filter → the invoice appears there with an amber "Room X" badge → open the iHotel Profile in the desk → see the new Folio Charge row with the right description and amount.
    - **Double-charge guard:** try the Payment button on the charged draft → button is hidden (the `custom_charge_to_room !== 1` guard). From the desk, call `charge_invoice_to_room` on the same invoice → backend rejects with a clear error.
    - **Reload recovery:** while on the POS page with an un-paid hotel-tagged draft, hard-reload the browser → `loadTableOrder` rehydrates `hotelRoom` + `ihotelProfile` → CustomerSelect re-enters Hotel Guest mode with the room pre-selected.
    - **Close Shift with a charged draft:** shift has 1 paid invoice + 1 charged draft + 0 real unpaid drafts → End Shift → pre-flight shows the paid invoice only → close succeeds without the "Transfer Required" banner firing.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The charged draft's docstatus is 0 forever.** This is the one-line summary of the architectural bet. It means: (a) no GL entries for this invoice from ERPNext; (b) the invoice never shows up in submitted POS Invoice reports; (c) `docstatus=0` filters on the Orders page now have to explicitly exclude charged drafts via the new "Room Charges" bucket so cashiers aren't confused. iHotel owns the accounting — the POS owns the item breakdown + printable receipt + audit trail via the Folio Charge reference. If iHotel's checkout flow doesn't eventually post GL entries tied to these charges, reporting will have a blind spot. Worth a follow-up audit once the first production site is running the integration.
  - **`charge_invoice_to_room` uses `db.set_value` (not `doc.save()`)** to stamp the invoice. This bypasses the POS Invoice validate hook cycle deliberately — we've already computed totals + built the Folio Charge row, we just need to flip three flags. Running full validate would re-calculate taxes and re-trigger everything in [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py) for a change that's purely metadata. Same pattern used by the Close Shift transfer flow (2026-04-10 entry).
  - **No support for per-item charge types.** A single Folio Charge row is written for the whole invoice with one charge_type (from the profile). If a future flow needs "food" vs "minibar" charge types in the same order, the cashier would have to split the order or the backend would need to group by charge_type — not in scope today.
  - **No partial room charge.** You can't charge half to the room and half to cash. The room option is all-or-nothing; for split scenarios the cashier uses the Split Bill tab with cash/card modes. If partial room charges become a real need, the cleanest path is a per-payer "Room" mode in the split UI — it fits into the existing `collapsePayers` grouping by mode, it just needs a per-payer "room number" field. Deferred.
  - **Take Away + Room:** confirmed working through the same flow — the `selectedOrderType` check in CustomerSelect doesn't interfere with hotel mode. Dine-in orders also work; the `restaurant_table` field is independent of `custom_hotel_room` so a cashier can seat a hotel guest at a physical table AND charge to their room.
  - **Error from the customer picker when iHotel is off**: none. The mode toggle is fully hidden behind the `ihotelEnabled` check, and the hotel-specific state fields default to null. Non-hotel installations see zero behavioral change.
  - **The "Room Charges" sidebar filter is new and sorts alphabetically after "Recently Paid"** via the order of `getOrderStatusTypes` returns — which is deterministic. The filter's SQL predicate (`AND pi.custom_charge_to_room = 1`) is the ONLY way a POS Invoice with `custom_charge_to_room=1` appears in the Orders page; Draft and Unbilled explicitly exclude it. The Orders page is authoritative for cashier visibility.
  - **The Close Shift pre-flight excludes charged drafts** from the "unpaid drafts blocking close" check. This was the last rough edge — without it, every charged draft would show up in the End Shift dialog's "Transfer Required" list even though there's nothing to transfer (the money moves through iHotel). The check is a one-line WHERE clause addition in `_get_shift_invoice_breakdown`.

### 2026-04-11 — Table merge: block empty-table selections + "still in use?" per-source picker
- **Two UX fixes on top of the merge feature that shipped earlier today.** Both came out of the user's first test pass:
  1. Merge mode let cashiers pick Available tables (no order on them), which is meaningless — you can't merge nothing into something. The cashier accidentally merged empty tables and silently lost visibility on them.
  2. When customers physically move from one table to join another (one of the two real merge cases — the other being "two groups at adjacent tables combine their tabs without moving"), the source table should return to Available so the next customer can be seated there. The previous behavior forced the source to hide behind the master, so a free table disappeared from the grid.
- **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `merge_tables`)**
  - **New "every selected table must have a draft" rule.** Checks `_get_active_drafts_on_table` for the master AND each source. Empty master throws "Table Is Available". Empty sources throws "Empty Tables Selected" listing the first 10 bad names. The old "Orders Must Be Merged" branch is gone — empty-table merges were the only way to hit it.
  - `merge_orders` is now forced to `1` internally. Every merge always combines orders because every selected table has one.
  - **New `freed_sources` parameter** (list of source table names). When a source name appears in this list, the backend keeps the source AVAILABLE in the grid — it does NOT set `merged_into`, leaves `occupied=0`, and clears `latest_invoice_time`. The source's order is still combined onto the master (via the `merge_pos_invoices` call), but the physical table is back in the pool for the next customer. Sources NOT in the list keep the old behavior: `merged_into=master`, hidden from the grid until the merge is reversed.
  - **Return shape extended** with `freed_count` so the frontend can show a friendly "2 returned to Available" suffix in the success toast.
- **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `unmerge_tables`)**
