  - **New "freed source reused" guard** added as the first check before reversing anything. For each `source_tables` row whose current `merged_into` is NOT pointing at this master (i.e. it was freed at merge time and the table is Available in the grid), checks for any draft POS Invoice on that source with `creation > log.merged_at`. If found, throws "Unmerge Blocked: Tables Reused" listing the offending table → invoice names. Rationale: restoring the old merged order onto a source that now has a new customer on it would create a two-order collision — the cashier has to resolve the new order first (pay or cancel).
- **Frontend:**
  - **[pos/src/lib/table-api.ts](pos/src/lib/table-api.ts)** `mergeTables` signature simplified: `(tableNames, master, notes, freedSources)`. `mergeOrders` is always implicitly ON, removed from the signature. `freedSources` is a `string[]`. `TableMergeResult` type gains `freed_count`.
  - **[pos/src/components/TableMergeDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/TableMergeDialog.tsx)** rewritten:
    - The `draftCounts` / "merge orders" checkbox dance is gone — every selected table is guaranteed to have a draft, so we just always merge the orders. Less UI friction.
    - New **per-source occupancy picker**: for each source table, a two-button row with "Still in use" (default, amber background when selected — hidden behind master until unmerge) and "Now empty" (green when selected — returns to Available grid). Clicking a button updates a `Record<source, 'still_in_use'|'now_empty'>` state.
    - Submit computes `freedSources` (the names marked `now_empty`) and passes them through to the API.
    - Footer shows a small emerald line "N tables will be returned to the Available grid after the merge" when any sources are marked empty.
  - **[pos/src/pages/Table.tsx](pos/src/pages/Table.tsx)** merge mode updates:
    - Available tables (`!isOccupied`) are now **unselectable** in merge mode. Their cards render with `opacity-40 cursor-not-allowed`, click handler shows a "Only tables with active orders can be merged" toast. The wobble animation only applies to occupied tables.
    - The `openMergeDialog` prefetch step that called `getTableOrder` for every selected table is removed — we already know every selection has a draft because the grid blocks empty-table selection.
    - Merge confirm handler signature updated to match the new dialog: `{master, freedSources, notes}`. Success toast shows "· N returned to Available" suffix when any sources were freed.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 719.91 → 720.21 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user walks the matrix:
    - **Empty selection:** enter merge mode → try clicking an Available table → card doesn't respond, toast "Only tables with active orders can be merged".
    - **Empty backend test:** (unreachable via UI now, but still test by curl or captain bypass) — backend still throws "Empty Tables Selected" as a safety net.
    - **Still-in-use merge:** pick 2 occupied tables → default "Still in use" on the source → Submit → master shows `+1` badge, source hidden from grid. Unchanged from yesterday.
    - **Now-empty merge:** pick 2 occupied tables → flip the source to "Now empty" → Submit → master shows combined order, **source stays in the grid as Available** for the next customer. Toast says "· 1 returned to Available".
    - **Reused-table unmerge guard:** merge A+B with B marked "Now empty" → seat new customer at B and create a new order → try to unmerge → backend throws "Unmerge Blocked: Tables Reused" naming B and the new invoice.
    - **Reused-table cleanup:** same scenario → pay the new order on B → retry unmerge → succeeds (the new invoice is no longer a draft, doesn't match the guard).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **"Still in use" is the safe default** because it matches the legacy behavior: the source table is hidden, one physical table hosts the combined order. A cashier who doesn't care about the freed-table distinction (most cases) just clicks Merge and gets the old behavior.
  - **The freed-source guard on unmerge only checks drafts**, not paid invoices. A paid invoice on a freed source is fine — it's final and doesn't collide with the restored merge order. The guard specifically catches the "new customer currently has an unpaid tab on that table" case.
  - **Freed sources don't get `merged_into` set**, so `getTable` will include them in the grid. The merge log still snapshots them in `source_tables`, so unmerge still knows they existed and can restore the order onto them (assuming the guard passes).
  - **The backend normalizes `merge_orders` to always-1**, but keeps the parameter in the signature for backward compatibility with any external callers (desk scripts, etc.) that might still pass it. The parameter is silently ignored.
  - **Why not prompt per table in merge mode instead of in the dialog?** Two-step UX is better: first the cashier picks WHICH tables to merge (selection mode), then the dialog confirms the intent with full context. Prompting during selection would make the selection step noisier.

### 2026-04-11 — Table merge / unmerge (cross-room, optional order merge, post-merge order reassignment)
- **Feature:** on `/pos/table`, cashiers can now merge multiple URY Tables into a single master — same-branch only, cross-room allowed. When the selected tables have active draft orders, the merge also combines those drafts into one order on the master via the existing `merge_pos_invoices` code path. Nested merges work: merging a master into another master chains the trees transitively. Unmerge reverses everything. When new orders are placed on the master AFTER the merge, unmerge is either blocked (default) or unlocked via a per-profile toggle that then asks the cashier to assign each post-merge order to a destination table.
- **Schema:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_table/ury_table.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_table/ury_table.json) — new `merged_into` Link field (Link → URY Table, read-only, in_standard_filter). Native field on the URY-owned DocType, edited directly on the JSON.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_table_merge_log/ury_table_merge_log.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_table_merge_log/ury_table_merge_log.json) + `.py` + `__init__.py` — new `URY Table Merge Log` doctype with naming `TMERGE-{YYYY}-{MM}-{DD}-{####}`, status enum `Active` / `Unmerged`, audit fields (merged_at / merged_by / unmerged_at / unmerged_by), a `merged_orders` check plus optional `order_merge_log` link to the sister `URY Order Merge Log` when orders were also merged, a `master_pre_merge_occupied` snapshot check, and a child table `source_tables`.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_table_merge_source/ury_table_merge_source.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_table_merge_source/ury_table_merge_source.json) + `.py` + `__init__.py` — new child doctype with `source_table`, `original_room`, `original_occupied`, `had_active_order`, `original_invoice` per source.
  - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures — new POS Profile custom field `custom_allow_unmerge_after_new_orders` (Check, default 0). When checked, cashiers can unmerge after placing new orders on the merged master; the unmerge UI asks them to pick a destination for each post-merge order. When unchecked (default), unmerge is blocked as soon as the master has any post-merge order.
  - [ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py) — `WRITE_DOCTYPES` gains `URY Table Merge Log` with `read + write + create + select + report`. URY Cashier / Captain / Manager get DocPerm rows on next `after_migrate`.
- **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - **`_get_top_level_master_table(table_name)`** — walks the `merged_into` chain to find the topmost master, capped at 20 hops to defend against cycles. Used by `merge_tables` to redirect "merge B into C" when B is itself a master — the operation retargets to B's top-level master so the chain stays consistent. Nested merges (A → B → C) are supported: unmerging C restores B as a master of A without disturbing the inner merge.
  - **`_get_active_drafts_on_table(table_name)`** — one SQL query returning the draft POS Invoices currently sitting on a table. Used by the merge validator to decide whether to force `merge_orders=1` AND by the order-merge-alongside path to collect every draft across master + sources.
  - **`merge_tables(tables, merge_orders, master, notes)`** whitelisted:
    - Dedupes + validates at least 2 distinct tables.
    - Resolves master (explicit param or first-in-list); if the master is itself already merged, walks up the chain.
    - Same-branch enforcement is hard — throws "Cross-Branch Merge Blocked" otherwise. Cross-room is allowed.
    - Source tables must be top-level (`merged_into` not set). The master CAN be a master already.
    - If any source has a draft AND `merge_orders=0`, throws "Orders Must Be Merged" so the cashier can't strand a draft on a hidden table.
    - Captain restriction (`custom_restrict_merge_to_captain`) gates the operation. Cashiers with drafts from other cashiers on the selected tables are rejected unless they're captains.
    - Builds a `URY Table Merge Log` with `master_pre_merge_occupied` snapshotted + one `URY Table Merge Source` row per source (room, occupied, had_active_order, original_invoice).
    - When `merge_orders=1` and there are ≥ 2 drafts across the selection, delegates to `merge_pos_invoices()` (reusing the existing captain-gated order merge logic). Re-homes the resulting order-merge master's `restaurant_table` to the table-merge master so the combined order sits on the final chosen table. The returned `URY Order Merge Log` name is stored on the `URY Table Merge Log` via `order_merge_log`.
    - Flips each source's `merged_into → master`, sets `occupied=0` + `latest_invoice_time=null`. Master's `occupied` is set to 1 when the merge involved any drafts.
    - Returns `{merge_log, master_table, merged_count, order_merge_log, order_master}`.
  - **`_get_post_merge_invoices(master_table, merged_at)`** — returns POS Invoices on the master whose `creation > merged_at`. Feeds the unmerge flow's "new orders blocking unmerge" check.
  - **`get_active_table_merge_log(table)`** whitelisted — returns the Active merge log when `table` is a master, including the source list, post-merge orders, and the profile's `custom_allow_unmerge_after_new_orders` flag so the frontend can pick the right unmerge mode without a second round-trip.
  - **`unmerge_tables(merge_log, order_assignments)`** whitelisted:
    - Validates log is still `Active`, master table still exists, caller can merge on the profile.
    - Checks for post-merge orders on the master. If any exist AND the profile's flag is off, throws "Unmerge Blocked: New Orders" naming the first 5. If the flag is on, requires an `order_assignments` map for every post-merge invoice with destinations that must be either the master or one of the merge's source tables. Applies the reassignments via `db.set_value("POS Invoice", name, "restaurant_table", dest)`.
    - Calls `unmerge_pos_invoices(log.order_merge_log)` first (if orders were merged alongside) so pre-merge drafts are restored BEFORE the source tables get re-occupied.
    - Clears `merged_into` on each source and restores its `occupied` state from the snapshot (or force-occupied when the source had an active order at merge time, since the order unmerge above just restored it).
    - Resets the master's `occupied` if it had no post-merge orders and wasn't occupied pre-merge.
    - Marks the log `Unmerged` + stamps audit fields.
    - Returns `{merge_log, master_table, unmerged_count, reassigned_orders}`.
  - **`getTable(room)` rewritten** — now runs SQL that filters `merged_into IS NULL` so merged sources never appear in the POS grid, and adds two correlated subqueries for `merge_log_name` + `merge_source_count` so the frontend can render the `+N` badge on master cards without a second round-trip.
- **Frontend:**
  - **[pos/src/lib/table-api.ts](pos/src/lib/table-api.ts)** — new types: `TableMergeSource`, `TableMergePostMergeOrder`, `ActiveTableMergeLog`, `TableMergeResult`, `TableUnmergeResult`. Extended `Table` with `merged_into`, `merge_log_name`, `merge_source_count`. New API helpers: `mergeTables`, `unmergeTables`, `getActiveTableMergeLog`.
  - **[pos/src/components/TableMergeDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/TableMergeDialog.tsx) — new** — confirm dialog with a master dropdown (defaults to first-selected), a pre-checked "Also merge active orders" toggle that forces ON when any source has a draft, a cross-room warning pill when selected tables span rooms, a notes field, and a live preview of which tables will merge into which master. Submit calls `mergeTables(names, mergeOrders, master, notes)`.
  - **[pos/src/components/TableUnmergeDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/TableUnmergeDialog.tsx) — new** — three render modes based on `ActiveTableMergeLog` state:
    1. No post-merge orders → simple confirm message ("Unmerge will restore the source tables…"), enabled Confirm button.
    2. Post-merge orders exist AND profile's flag is OFF → red "Unmerge blocked" banner with the first 5 blocking invoice names, no Confirm button shown.
    3. Post-merge orders exist AND profile's flag is ON → per-order table with a destination Select dropdown (master + each source). Confirm button disabled until every row has an assignment.
  - **[pos/src/pages/Table.tsx](pos/src/pages/Table.tsx)** — full rewrite:
    - New `mergeMode` state + enter/exit/toggle helpers. Merge mode collects table selections across rooms (stored in the tables cache) so cross-room merges survive a room switch.
    - New "Merge Tables" button in the header bar, gated by the existing `canMergeOrders` helper (reuses `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain` per the user's direction). When merge mode is on, replaces with an amber bar showing "N selected · Cancel · Merge".
    - In merge mode, every table card gets the `ury-wobble` class (staggered delays via `ury-wobble-d{idx % 6}`) and its click handler becomes a selection toggle instead of a "start order" navigation. Selected cards get an amber ring + a checkmark badge in the top-right.
    - Master tables with an Active merge log show a `+N` amber pill with the merge icon alongside the Occupied/Available badge, no click needed to surface the state.
    - Merged masters render an "Unmerge" button at the bottom of the card when `canMerge` is true — click opens `TableUnmergeDialog` via `getActiveTableMergeLog(tableName)`.
    - Successful merge / unmerge invalidates the tables cache, reloads the current room, and refreshes the room counts so the grid reflects the new state immediately.
    - Draft counts for the merge dialog are fetched on-demand when the user clicks Merge (via `getTableOrder` per selected table) so the dialog can decide whether to force the "merge orders" checkbox.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py ury/hooks.py ury/setup.py ury/permissions.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on all the new doctype JSONs + `custom_field.json` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 704.04 → 719.91 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` to install the new `URY Table.merged_into` field, the two new doctypes, the new POS Profile custom field, and the updated permission baseline. Then walks the matrix:
    - **Simple merge:** pick 2 empty tables in the same room → Merge → confirm dialog shows master pre-selected + "merge orders" unchecked → Submit → master shows `+1` badge, source disappears from the grid.
    - **Merge with orders:** create drafts on both tables → pick both → Merge → "merge orders" is forced ON and disabled → Submit → master order now has combined items, source table hidden.
    - **Cross-room merge:** pick one table from Room A and one from Room B → amber "cross-room" warning → Submit → master's room shows the combined table with `+1` badge.
    - **Nested merge:** merge A+B → A; now merge A and C → A. Chain: B→A, C→A. Walking the merge logs shows two separate logs, each reversible.
    - **Simple unmerge:** merged master with no new orders → Unmerge → simple confirm → all sources restored, master order (if merged) split back.
    - **Blocked unmerge (default):** merged master, place new order on master → Unmerge → red "Unmerge blocked: new orders" banner, no Confirm button.
    - **Allowed unmerge with reassignment:** flip `custom_allow_unmerge_after_new_orders = 1` on the POS Profile → same scenario → assignment table with destination picker per post-merge order, default master, can reassign to sources → Submit → each post-merge invoice's `restaurant_table` updated, merge reversed.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Draft count detection is cheap but approximate.** `openMergeDialog` uses `getTableOrder` per selected table which returns the single active draft if there is one (or none). If a table has multiple drafts (unusual), we'd count it as 1. Acceptable for the force-merge-orders decision since the backend re-validates and will merge everything anyway.
  - **The merge-mode selection spans rooms.** A captain can enter merge mode in Room A, select tables, switch to Room B, select more, and hit Merge — the selection persists because it's stored in a separate state slot and the cross-room tables are pulled from the `tablesCache` at dialog-open time. This is how the cross-room merge flow actually works in the UI.
  - **Order merge-along delegates to `merge_pos_invoices`** which requires same-terminal. Cross-terminal table merges are therefore implicitly blocked when orders are involved. Empty-table cross-terminal merges theoretically work but aren't a real use case. Not worth adding a separate guard.
  - **Post-merge orders are defined by `creation > merged_at`**, not by item diffs on the master's existing combined order. If a cashier ADDS items to the master's combined draft after the merge (rather than creating a new order), those items ride along through the unmerge without triggering the "new orders" block. The flag and assignment flow only concern whole new POS Invoice docs. Documented clearly in the dialog copy.
  - **The captain restriction reuses `_user_can_merge_orders`** and the `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain` POS Profile flag. Per user's direction: "3. okay thats fine" (reusing the existing gate). No new captain-only table flag.
  - **`getTable` now returns an SQL result, not a `frappe.get_all` result.** The shape is slightly different but the frontend already handled an array of `{name, occupied, …}` dicts, and the new columns (`merged_into`, `merge_log_name`, `merge_source_count`) are additive.
  - **Re-homing the order-merge master's `restaurant_table`** onto the table-merge master via `db.set_value` bypasses validation. Deliberate — the order doc has already been validated during the earlier `merge_pos_invoices` call; we just need the table link to match the final chosen master. Running full validate again would be redundant and slow.
  - **The merge log's `notes` field gets an auto-note `"Auto-merged alongside table merge <name>"` when orders are merged alongside** so the order merge log's audit trail points back at the table merge that triggered it. One-way link (table → order), which is enough — the table merge log stores `order_merge_log` for the reverse lookup.
  - **Unmerge of an outer merge doesn't touch inner merges.** If A→B and B→C, unmerging C restores B as a top-level master with A still merged in. The cashier can then unmerge B→A separately. This matches how the order merge code already behaves.

### 2026-04-11 — Close Shift: root cause was a misconfigured Mode of Payment Account
- **Symptom:** Multiple rounds yesterday chasing a cascade on Close Shift that surfaced as `"Could not find Reference Name: POS-CLO-2026-00001"` at the top and `"Sales Invoice ACC-SINV-2026-00001: Customer is required against Receivable account 1310 - Debtors - BB"` buried deep in the traceback. Closing worked for Administrator (on cash-only test data) but failed for every URY role on shifts that had a Credit Card payment. We threw a LOT of defense at this — child-row customer re-reads, `before_submit` self-healing hook on Sales Invoice, session swap to Administrator, monkey-patching `SalesInvoice.make_customer_gl_entry` and `make_pos_gl_entries` for the duration of the submit — none of it helped because **the bug wasn't in any of those places**.
- **Actual root cause:** the `Credit Card` Mode of Payment had its Default Account set to `1310 - Debtors - BB` (the company's Receivable account) instead of a Bank/Cash account. When a POS Invoice gets paid via Credit Card, ERPNext stores that receivable-typed account on the `Sales Invoice Payment` child row. At consolidation time, `SalesInvoice.make_pos_gl_entries` ([sales_invoice.py:1858](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py#L1858)) builds the offset GL entry using `"account": payment_mode.account` WITHOUT setting `party_type` / `party`. The GL validator (`gl_entry.py:143`) then sees a debit against a Receivable account with no party and throws. That rolls back the merge log submit, which tries to mark the closing entry as "Failed" by adding a Comment, which fails link validation because the closing entry was never inserted (the rollback cascaded), and THAT becomes the misleading `"Could not find Reference Name"` top-level error that made this so hard to track.
- **How we isolated it:** a temporary diagnostic probe (`ury/ury_pos/_probe.py`, deleted after) that monkey-patched `SalesInvoice.make_customer_gl_entry` + `make_pos_gl_entries` to print every GL entry as it was built. Output showed:
  ```
  GLE: account='1310 - Debtors - BB'  party='Cash Customer'  debit=20.0  credit=0    ← correct customer GLE
  GLE: account='1310 - Debtors - BB'  party='Cash Customer'  debit=0     credit=10.0 ← Cash payment, OK
  GLE: account='1310 - Debtors - BB'  party=None             debit=10.0  credit=0    ← Credit Card offset ← BROKEN
  GLE: account='1310 - Debtors - BB'  party='Cash Customer'  debit=0     credit=10.0
  GLE: account='1110 - Cash - BB'     party=None             debit=10.0  credit=0    ← Cash offset, OK
  ```
  The Credit Card offset entry tried to post to the Debtors account (Receivable) with no party. Running `SELECT mode_of_payment, account FROM 'tabMode of Payment Account' WHERE company = 'Build Build'` confirmed: `Cash → 1110 - Cash - BB`, `Credit Card → 1310 - Debtors - BB`. Fix: change the Default Account on the Credit Card Mode of Payment to a real bank/cash account in the desk. **No code change needed to unblock the user — the closing flow works.**
- **Code changes in this round (cleanup + error handling):**
  - **Stripped the phantom defenses.** Reverted: the Administrator session swap, `frappe.flags.ignore_permissions = True`, monkey-patching `SalesInvoice.make_customer_gl_entry` + `make_pos_gl_entries`, the post-insert child-row blank-customer diagnostic, the separate `before_submit` hook on Sales Invoice (ury_sales_invoice.py `ensure_consolidated_customer`). All gone. The submit is back to a plain `closing_doc.insert(ignore_permissions=True) → closing_doc.submit()` wrapped in a `try/except`. The `ignore_permissions=True` on insert is standard Frappe practice for "caller has been authorized upstream, trust the internal bookkeeping" and is NOT the phantom — it's needed because URY roles legitimately don't have DocPerms on the long tail of GL / stock doctypes downstream.
  - **New `_validate_close_preflight(paid_invoices, company)`** runs before `closing_doc.insert()`. Two checks:
    1. Every paid invoice has a `customer` set. First 5 bad names in the error.
    2. Every `Mode of Payment` used on the shift's paid invoices has an account configured for the company AND **that account is NOT a Receivable account**. This is the exact check that would have saved us yesterday. Error lists every misconfigured mode with the reason (`"Credit Card: account '1310 - Debtors - BB' is a Receivable account"`) and tells the user to open Mode of Payment in the desk to fix it. Title: "Mode of Payment Account Misconfigured".
  - **New `_wrap_close_error(err, company)`** runs as the `except` handler around `closing_doc.submit()`. If the pre-flight somehow missed the issue (new ERPNext edge case, data corruption, something we haven't seen), the downstream error still fires but we re-throw it with a **hint block** pointing at the most likely root cause. Hint switches on message content:
    - `"Customer is required against Receivable account"` → hint about Mode of Payment Default Account pointing at a Receivable account
    - `"debit_to"` or `"receivable account"` → hint about POS Profile Write Off / Receivable Account configuration
    - `"no permission"` / `"permission"` → hint about re-running `bench migrate` to refresh URY's permission baseline
    - `"customer_group"` / `"territory"` → hint about the customer master having a missing required field
    - Default → hint to check the Error Log in the desk for the full traceback
  - **Sales Invoice hook file cleaned up** — [ury_sales_invoice.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_sales_invoice.py) no longer has the `before_submit` + `ensure_consolidated_customer` implementation. Just `before_insert` and `on_update` remain. [hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) `doc_events["Sales Invoice"]` drops the `before_submit` entry.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `ury_pos/api.py`, `ury_sales_invoice.py`, `hooks.py` — OK.
  - **No frontend changes** — pure backend cleanup.
  - **User confirmed** the close works from the React POS front-end after changing the Credit Card Mode of Payment's Default Account to a non-Receivable account in the desk.
  - **Still to do (when the user hits an edge case):** the new pre-flight + error wrapper will catch misconfigurations on the next run with actionable messages. If a new edge case emerges, we'll get a friendlier hint + the original error text instead of a 4-level cascade.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Lesson — resist the urge to monkey-patch ERPNext internals when chasing a bug.** We burned an entire afternoon adding defensive layers (session swap, flag flip, method patches, hook self-healing) instead of running one diagnostic probe to see what ERPNext was actually doing. The probe took ~5 minutes to write and gave us the exact answer. Next time a cascade error has a misleading top-level message, the first move is `bench execute` a short script that instruments the failing code path.
  - **`ignore_permissions=True` on insert IS still the right call.** URY Cashier / URY Captain don't have explicit DocPerms on GL Entry, Stock Ledger Entry, Payment Ledger Entry, Customer balance updates, Account outstanding updates, etc. Granting them is brittle and leaks access. The flag tells Frappe "the caller is already authorized at a higher level, skip the per-doctype perm checks on internal writes". This is different from what we tried yesterday (`frappe.flags.ignore_permissions = True` module-level + full session swap) — THAT was chasing the phantom customer issue. THIS is the normal Frappe pattern.
  - **Why the pre-flight check for Receivable-typed Mode of Payment accounts is the right place to stop this.** The consolidation chain's GL entry building is deep inside ERPNext internals and fragile to override. Catching the misconfiguration at the top of `submit_pos_closing_entry` is simple, direct, and gives the cashier an error they can act on without a developer's help. The check is cheap (one SQL query against the shift's payment rows + one `frappe.db.get_value` per distinct mode).
  - **The error wrapper is a safety net, not the primary defense.** Ideally the pre-flight catches everything and `_wrap_close_error` never fires. But new ERPNext versions can add new failure paths, admins can corrupt data in novel ways, etc. The wrapper is there so the next time something unexpected breaks, the cashier at least gets a breadcrumb pointing at a likely cause instead of a bare stack trace.
  - **Other companies to audit:** if you have multiple companies configured, check each one's Mode of Payment → Default Account settings. The Build Build / BB case was the only one in the test data but a production deployment might have silently broken configs that haven't been exercised yet.
  - **The `Cash Customer` record has `customer_group = None` and `territory = None`.** Those are marked `reqd: 1` in the Customer doctype but the record somehow exists anyway (probably created via direct SQL or an older fixture import). This is unrelated to the Close Shift bug but is its own latent problem — any future flow that triggers full Customer validation will throw on that record. Worth fixing in the desk (Territory → pick any, Customer Group → pick any) as preventive maintenance.

### 2026-04-10 — Close Shift: honest totals + unpaid-draft transfer gate
- **Symptom:** User clicked End Shift → the close dialog showed `INVOICES: 2`, `GRAND TOTAL: ₵0`, `NET TOTAL: ₵0`, and `Cash opening/expected: ₵0`. Two paid invoices existed on the shift, but every number was zero. Separately: the user asked for a mandatory "transfer unpaid drafts to another cashier" step before the close is allowed to proceed.
- **Root cause (totals):** `preview_pos_closing_entry` was wrapping ERPNext's `make_closing_entry_from_opening` which populates totals via a query filtered by `posting_date + posting_time BETWEEN opening.period_start_date AND NOW`. Cashiers sometimes backdate `posting_date` (see the "Outdated POS Opening Entry" flow from earlier today) and those invoices fall outside the timestamp window — ERPNext drops them from BOTH the totals sum AND the count, but URY's preview was deriving the count from `len(closing_doc.pos_invoices)` which happened to still show 2 on the user's data. The exact reason count=2 survived while totals=0 is a quirk of ERPNext's `get_invoices` filter shape on the user's particular dataset; the fix is not to rely on that helper at all.
- **Backend fixes ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - **New private helper `_scope_invoices_for_opening_entry(opening_doc)`** — returns a SQL `WHERE` clause + params that scope POS Invoices to `pos_profile = opening.pos_profile AND owner = opening.user AND (consolidated_invoice IS NULL OR '') AND creation >= opening.creation`. Uses `creation` (row insert timestamp), NOT `posting_date + posting_time`, so backdated invoices still count. Scopes by `custom_terminal` too when the opening entry has one (with the defensive null fallback — matches the pattern in `getPosInvoice`).
  - **New private helper `_get_shift_invoice_breakdown(opening_doc)`** — runs two SQL queries against the shared scope: one for paid (`docstatus=1 AND status IN ('Paid','Consolidated','Return')`), one for draft (`docstatus=0 AND not merged-into`). Returns `(paid_rows, draft_rows)`.
  - **New private helper `_sum_payments_for_paid_invoices(paid_invoices)`** — groups `tabSales Invoice Payment` rows by `mode_of_payment` and sums, one query across all paid invoice names. Feeds the reconciliation table.
  - **`preview_pos_closing_entry` rewritten** — no longer calls `make_closing_entry_from_opening`. Runs the three helpers above, computes grand/net/qty/tax totals by plain sum, builds the reconciliation table from the payment-mode aggregation UNIONed with the opening entry's `balance_details` (so modes with an opening float always show up even if nothing was collected in that mode). Returns a new response shape that adds: `draft_count`, `draft_grand_total`, `draft_invoices[]` (per-draft details for the UI list), and `transfer_candidates[]` (other cashiers on the same branch from the ExPOS Users table, current user excluded).
  - **`submit_pos_closing_entry` rewritten** — now takes an optional `transfer_to` user id. New enforcement: if `draft` list is non-empty and `transfer_to` is missing, throws a "Transfer Required" error that names the first 5 drafts ("+N more" beyond that). If `transfer_to` is present, validates it's a URY Cashier / URY Captain on the same branch and NOT the current user, then bulk-reassigns ownership via `frappe.db.set_value("POS Invoice", row_name, {"owner": target, "cashier": target_full_name, "remarks": transfer_note}, update_modified=True)` for each draft. The `remarks` field gets a one-line audit note `"Transferred from <user> on shift close (<opening_entry_name>)"` so the paper trail is visible in the desk.
  - After the transfer (or if no drafts exist), it builds the POS Closing Entry doc **from scratch** (not via `make_closing_entry_from_opening`) — same totals logic as the preview plus the cashier-supplied counted amounts, inserts + submits. Returns `{name, transferred, transfer_to}` so the frontend can show how many were moved.
- **Frontend fixes:**
  - **[pos/src/lib/pos-closing-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-closing-api.ts)** — added `POSClosingDraftInvoice`, `POSClosingTransferCandidate`, extended `POSClosingPreview` with the new fields (`draft_count`, `draft_grand_total`, `draft_invoices`, `transfer_candidates`), added `POSClosingSubmitResult` with `transferred` + `transfer_to`, threaded an optional `transferTo` argument through `submitClosingEntry`.
  - **[pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx):**
    - New `transferTo` state slot.
    - Summary stats now labels "Paid Invoices" (was "Invoices") so cashiers don't confuse the paid count with the total count.
    - New amber block rendered above the Payment Reconciliation table when `preview.draft_count > 0`: an AlertTriangle banner with "N unpaid orders blocking close · Total ₵X", a scrollable amber-bordered table listing each draft with `{order_name, customer, table, total}`, and a "Transfer to:" row with a Select dropdown populated from `preview.transfer_candidates`. When the branch has no other cashiers, shows an italic "Add one in the desk" message instead of an empty select.
    - Footer **Close Shift button is disabled** when `draft_count > 0 && !transferTo` — its label switches to "Select a Cashier" and the styling goes gray. Also adds a client-side guard in `handleSubmit` that sets a local error if the user somehow bypasses the disabled state.
    - `handleSubmit` passes `transferTo || undefined` as the third arg to `submitClosingEntry`.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 701.49 → 704.04 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user walks the matrix:
    - **Wrong totals:** open End Shift dialog for the current shift → now shows the correct paid count AND correct grand total, net total, and per-mode expected amounts derived from actual Sales Invoice Payment rows.
    - **Draft-blocking (no drafts):** shift with 0 drafts → no amber block, Close Shift button is enabled immediately.
    - **Draft-blocking (with drafts):** shift with 2 drafts → amber block shows with the 2 draft rows, transfer dropdown pre-empty, Close Shift button says "Select a Cashier" and is disabled. Pick a cashier → button lights up → Close Shift → backend reassigns owner on the 2 drafts + submits the closing entry with paid-only totals.
    - **Target cashier login:** log in as the transfer target → open Orders page → the 2 transferred drafts are visible under "My Orders" (because their `owner` is now set to the target).
    - **Edge cases:** branch has no other cashiers → the amber block shows "Add one in the desk"; try transferring to yourself (backend guard) → ValidationError; try transferring with a target not on the branch → ValidationError.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Scoping by `creation`, not `posting_date + posting_time`, is the big shift.** `creation` is set at DB insert time and never changes, so backdated `posting_date` edits don't affect whether the invoice counts toward a shift. The closing entry will still record the (possibly-backdated) posting_date on each row — that's an accounting-side concern. The invariant we care about is "every invoice CREATED during this shift gets counted", which `creation` guarantees.
  - **The `_scope_invoices_for_opening_entry` helper's `owner = opening.user` filter is important.** If a captain opens an entry and a sub-cashier creates invoices under a different user, those won't be included in the captain's close. Under URY's per-terminal model (post-2026-04-08) this is the intended behavior — each user closes their own shift. Multi-cashier sub-POS-closing flow is separate.
  - **Transferring ownership via `db.set_value` bypasses Frappe's normal `Document.save()` lifecycle** — no `validate` hooks fire, no `on_update` fires. That's intentional: (a) the only changes are owner/cashier/remarks, none of which should trigger POS Invoice validators; (b) running validate on N drafts during a close would be slow; (c) validate on a draft invoice with a new owner could trip on something unrelated. If a specific hook really needs to run on transfer, a future tweak can call `doc.run_method` selectively.
  - **The `cashier` custom field is updated alongside `owner`** because some URY print paths format the receipt using `cashier` (the full-name string) rather than the owner's user id. Keeping them in sync means the transferred draft's receipt shows the new cashier's name if it's later printed/paid.
  - **Audit trail goes through `remarks`** rather than a new custom field. The Frappe Version table also records the owner change automatically. A dedicated `custom_transferred_from` field would be cleaner for reporting, but the remarks approach keeps this round lean — if a report needs that structured data later, it's a one-field addition.
  - **The backend rejects close WITH a list of draft names, not just a generic error.** The cashier sees exactly which orders are blocking them. Limited to the first 5 + "+N more" to keep the error readable.
  - **`_sum_payments_for_paid_invoices` excludes the change_amount correction** that ERPNext's `get_payments` does (subtracting change from the bank account's mode). For URY's current cash-heavy flow this difference is usually negligible (change is typically in the same mode as the payment). If change-heavy flows cause discrepancies, the helper can be extended to apply the same correction.

### 2026-04-10 — User-menu role gating + End Shift + mobile-view cleanup + unauth landing + geofencing
- **Five related polish items bundled before a push.** All user-directive; planned up-front.
- **Avatar dropdown — role gating + End Shift ([pos/src/components/Header.tsx](pos/src/components/Header.tsx))**
  - New [pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts) helper `canAccessDeskAndTerminalSwitch(user)` — returns true for Administrator / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain. URY Cashier is excluded.
  - "Change Terminal" and "Switch To Desk" menu items are now wrapped in `{canSeeAdminActions && ...}`. Cashiers no longer see them — a terminal binding is meant to be permanent per device, and dropping a cashier into the Frappe desk exposes masters they shouldn't touch.
  - New "End Shift" menu item visible to **everyone** (cashiers need to close their own day). Handler fetches the current open entry via `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminalName)`, opens the existing `<POSClosingDialog>` pre-wired to that entry. On success, page reloads so `POSOpeningProvider` kicks back into the opening flow. Errors propagate through `extractFrappeServerError` → `showToast.error({title, description})`.
  - Header now mounts `<POSClosingDialog>` conditionally alongside its own dropdown so the dialog doesn't need to be in the outer `App` tree.
- **Mobile view — ([pos/src/components/ScreenSizeDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ScreenSizeDialog.tsx))**
  - Removed the "You can use URY POS Version 1 for mobile devices / Switch to Version 1" section and its handler. The legacy Vue POS is sunset (Dec 2025 per README) so the deep-link no longer makes sense. Dialog still shows the "Desktop Only" message + current/required width for diagnostics.
- **Unauthenticated /pos landing — ([pos/src/App.tsx](pos/src/App.tsx))**
  - Old behavior: an unauth visit to `/pos` triggered `getTerminals()` which threw `"Function ury.ury_pos.api.get_terminals is not whitelisted"` because Guest users aren't allowed to hit whitelisted endpoints. The setup screen rendered that raw error under a "Setup Error" title.
  - New: before touching any privileged endpoint, call `getLoggedUser()` (which routes through Frappe's guest-safe `frappe.auth.get_logged_user`). When the result is `"Guest"` or null, short-circuit into a new `UnauthenticatedLanding` component — a clean "Sign in to continue" card with a "Go to Login" button that deep-links to `/login?redirect-to=%2Fpos`. The user lands back on the POS automatically after authenticating.
  - The terminal-resolution effect is gated on `authChecked && !isGuest` so nothing privileged runs until we know the user is logged in.
- **Geofence — per-POS Profile opt-in login gate (schema + backend + frontend + desk script)**
  - **Design:** master switch on the POS Profile. When off, no location check, no browser permission prompt. When on, the React POS asks the browser for a GPS fix right after the terminal resolves and POSTs it to a whitelisted `validate_geofence` endpoint. Users outside the allowed radius are blocked from opening the POS. Per-user overrides (or "use company location" checkbox) live in the Branch's ExPOS Users table.
  - **Schema additions:**
    - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_user/ury_user.json](ury/ury/doctype/ury_user/ury_user.json) — native fields on the URY User child table: `geofence_section` (Section Break, collapsible), `custom_use_company_location` (Check, default 1), `custom_latitude` / `custom_longitude` (Float, precision 6), `custom_geofence_radius_meters` (Int override). Edited the DocType JSON directly because URY User is a URY-owned DocType — custom fields aren't needed.
    - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures list — POS Profile custom fields: `custom_geofence_section`, `custom_geofence_enabled` (Check, default 0 — feature master switch), `custom_company_latitude` / `custom_company_longitude` (Float, precision 6), `custom_geofence_radius_meters` (Int, default 200). All depend on `custom_geofence_enabled` so the fields collapse when the feature is off. Registered in BOTH `custom_field.json` AND `setup.py` per the dual-source-of-truth rule — existing installs pick them up via `create_custom_fields` on `after_migrate`.
  - **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
    - Private helper `_haversine_meters(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2)` — great-circle distance in meters via the standard haversine formula. No external dependencies (stdlib `math`).
    - Private helper `_resolve_user_geofence(pos_profile_name)` — returns `(target_lat, target_lon, radius_meters, source_label)` or `None`. Resolution order: (1) POS Profile must have `custom_geofence_enabled=1`; (2) look for a URY User row on the profile's Branch for this session's user; if found and the row has `custom_use_company_location=0` + explicit lat/lon, use those (with optional per-user radius override); (3) otherwise fall back to the profile's company coords. Returns `None` when the feature is off OR configured but missing coords — the endpoint treats `None` as "not configured" and lets the user through so a misconfigured admin doesn't lock themselves out.
    - New whitelisted `get_geofence_config(terminal)` — returns `{enabled, radius_meters, source}` so the frontend can decide whether to request geolocation. Returns `{enabled: 0}` when off.
    - New whitelisted `validate_geofence(latitude, longitude, terminal)` — computes distance via haversine, throws `ValidationError` with a clear "You are X m away from the allowed location (limit Y m)" message when out of range. Returns `{ok: 1, distance_meters, radius_meters, source}` on success. The security note in the docstring is honest: this trusts client-supplied coordinates (devtools spoofing is possible), so it's a policy control, not cryptographic — the goal is to keep honest cashiers in their branch and raise the bar for shared-credential abuse from off-site.
  - **Frontend ([pos/src/lib/geofence-api.ts](pos/src/lib/geofence-api.ts) — new file):**
    - Thin wrapper exporting `getGeofenceConfig`, `validateGeofence`, and `getBrowserPosition()` — the last one promisifies `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition` with a 15s timeout + high accuracy + 30s maxAge cache, and translates the native error codes (`PERMISSION_DENIED`, `POSITION_UNAVAILABLE`, `TIMEOUT`) into friendly human-readable strings the blocking screen can show.
  - **Frontend — App.tsx gate:**
    - New state: `geofenceChecking`, `geofenceError`, `geofencePassed`, `geofenceAttempt`. A new effect runs after the terminal resolves: calls `getGeofenceConfig(terminal.terminal)`, and if `enabled === 0` marks `geofencePassed = true` immediately (no browser prompt). When enabled, awaits `getBrowserPosition()` → `validateGeofence(...)`. On success, `geofencePassed = true`; on failure, stores the error message. Effect deps include `geofenceAttempt` so clicking "Try Again" re-fires.
    - New render branches: (a) "Verifying Your Location" card with an animated MapPin while checking; (b) "Location Check Failed" card with the parsed error + "Try Again" button (bumps `geofenceAttempt`) + "Sign Out" button (calls `logout()` → redirects to login).
    - `initializeApp` effect is now gated on `terminal && geofencePassed` so nothing privileged runs until the check passes.
  - **Desk-side Client Script ([ury/public/js/pos_profile_geofence.js](ury/public/js/pos_profile_geofence.js) — new file):**
    - Adds a "Get Current Location" custom button on POS Profile via `frm.add_custom_button(... "Geofence")`. Click → `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition` → on success, stamps `custom_company_latitude` + `custom_company_longitude` with 6-decimal precision, auto-enables `custom_geofence_enabled` (convenience — the admin is clearly opting in), and shows a green toast with the coords + accuracy. Error branches mirror the React POS's friendly messages.
    - Wired via `doctype_js = {"POS Profile": "public/js/pos_profile_geofence.js"}` in `hooks.py`. The file is served from `/assets/ury/js/` automatically by Frappe's asset pipeline.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `api.py`, `hooks.py`, `setup.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` + `ury_user.json` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 694.77 → 701.49 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` to install the new POS Profile fields + the URY User schema changes. Then walks the matrix:
    - **Avatar dropdown:** log in as URY Cashier → open user menu → sees ONLY "End Shift", "Clear Cache", "Logout" (no Change Terminal / Switch To Desk). Log in as URY Captain → sees all five. End Shift click → in-POS closing dialog opens with the current entry pre-loaded.
    - **Mobile view:** resize browser to 900px wide → the Desktop Only card shows, NO "Switch to Version 1" button.
    - **Unauth:** hit `/pos` in an incognito tab → "Sign in to continue" card + "Go to Login" button → click → lands on `/login?redirect-to=%2Fpos` → after login, browser comes back to `/pos`.
    - **Geofence (feature off):** default install has `custom_geofence_enabled=0` → POS loads normally with no browser permission prompt.
    - **Geofence (feature on, company coords):** enable the toggle, click "Get Current Location" on the POS Profile form in the desk → lat/lon fields populate → Save. Open `/pos` on a device within ~200m → allow location → verification passes silently → POS loads. Open from 1km away → blocked with "You are 1000 m away from the allowed location (limit 200 m)" + Try Again / Sign Out buttons.
    - **Geofence (per-user coords):** on the Branch's ExPOS Users row, uncheck "Use Company Location", set lat/lon to a different location, Save. That user is now geofenced against the per-user coords instead of the company ones.
    - **Geofence (admin misconfiguration):** toggle enabled but no company coords set AND no per-user coords → `_resolve_user_geofence` returns `None` → endpoint returns `{enabled: 0}` → POS loads (fails open, doesn't lock out the admin who enabled the feature prematurely).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **End Shift for cashiers was the main motivation for this round.** The existing closing flow required either the Shift Hours banner firing (which only works when `custom_shift_hours > 0`) or the desk-side POS Opening Entry form. Neither was discoverable. The user-menu entry gives every cashier a one-click path to "I'm done — count my drawer".
  - **The geofence feature trusts client-side coordinates.** A motivated user with devtools can spoof `navigator.geolocation` and satisfy the check from anywhere. This is explicit in the backend docstring. The threat model is "honest cashier accidentally opens the POS from home" and "shared credential abuse from off-site without tech knowledge" — not "sophisticated attacker trying to bypass the check". If stronger guarantees are needed later, the answer is either a physical token (bound terminal, which URY already has via `URY POS Terminal`) or IP-based allowlisting in the reverse proxy.
  - **The feature fails open on misconfiguration**, not closed. If an admin enables the toggle but forgets to set coordinates, the POS still loads. The alternative (fail closed) would mean a single wrong save on POS Profile could lock every cashier out of the POS — too risky for a polish feature. Admins get a clear state: the toggle is on in the form, the coords are blank, and they can fix it without locking themselves out.
  - **`custom_use_company_location` defaults to 1** on new URY User rows, so every existing user (and every new one) falls back to the profile's company coords by default. Admins only touch per-user overrides for the unusual case (remote workers, delivery staff with their own allowed area, etc.).
  - **The desk-side Client Script is served via `doctype_js`**, not as a Client Script fixture. Two reasons: (a) a static JS file under `public/js/` ships with the app on install (no separate fixture import step), (b) it's easier to edit and version-control. The tradeoff is that the JS runs on every POS Profile form load regardless of customization, but the script's `refresh` hook is cheap (one field existence check + one `add_custom_button` call) so that's fine.
  - **The geofence check runs ONCE per session** (per device reload), not on every API call. The cost is a single GPS fix + one backend round-trip at login. If the user walks away from the geofenced area while the POS is open, they stay authenticated — this is deliberate. A continuous check would be hostile (GPS drains battery, and a brief step outside to grab something shouldn't kick the cashier out).
  - **`logout` is now statically imported** in App.tsx instead of dynamic-imported inside the Sign Out button handler. The dynamic import was triggering a Vite build warning about a module being both statically and dynamically imported.

### 2026-04-10 — Return hardening: over-return guard + "Returned" badge on the original
- **Symptom:** After the first round of Returns shipped, user reported: (1) "that returned item can be returned again" — the same row could be returned multiple times, adding up to more than the original quantity; (2) "the return badge does not show on those paid items" — after returning invoice SAF00012, opening SAF00012 in the Paid filter showed no visual cue that a return existed against it.
- **Root causes:**
  1. Neither the preview endpoint (`get_return_preview`) nor the submit endpoint (`create_pos_return`) tracked *already-returned* quantities. The per-row qty bound was `<= original_qty`, not `<= original_qty - already_returned`. A cashier could pick the full qty, submit, reopen the dialog, and pick the full qty again — and the backend would happily create a second return for the same items.
  2. The Orders list query (`getPosInvoice` / `searchPosInvoice`) didn't surface any "has this invoice been returned" flag. The list row had no way to know, and therefore no way to render a badge.
- **Fixes — Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - New helper `_get_returned_qty_per_row(source_invoice_name)` — one SQL query that joins POS Invoice Item child rows to their parent POS Invoice, filters to `is_return=1 AND docstatus=1 AND return_against=<source>`, groups by the child row's `pos_invoice_item` back-reference, and sums `ABS(qty)`. Returns a `{source_row_name: total_returned_qty}` dict. Docstatus filter means cancelled returns (`docstatus=2`) are excluded — so Undo Return restores the capacity to return the rows again. One query regardless of how many rows/returns exist.
  - `get_return_preview()` now computes `qty_already_returned` + `qty_remaining` per row via the new helper and includes them in each item in the response. Also returns a top-level `fully_returned: 0|1` flag (true when every row's `qty_remaining` is 0 — nothing left to return).
  - `create_pos_return()` now re-validates against `remaining = original_qty - already_returned` (server-side, not just UI). Error message names the item AND the original/returned/remaining numbers so the cashier understands the state: `"Cannot return X of Item — only Y left after previous returns (original Z, already returned W)."`. Tolerance of 1e-6 on the comparison for float safety.
  - `getPosInvoice()` + `searchPosInvoice()` SELECT list gains `pi.is_return`, `pi.return_against`, and a new correlated subquery `active_return_count` counting active (`docstatus=1`) returns written against each row. The count is returned to the frontend so the list rows / cards can render the "Returned" badge without an extra round-trip.
- **Fixes — Frontend:**
  - [pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts): `ReturnPreviewItem` gains `qty_already_returned` and `qty_remaining`. `ReturnPreview` gains `fully_returned`. `POSInvoice` gains `is_return`, `return_against`, `active_return_count`. Same additions mirrored in [pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts).
  - [pos/src/components/ReturnDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ReturnDialog.tsx):
    - Every per-item qty bound now uses `row.qty_remaining` instead of `row.qty`. `returnAll` only fills rows that still have remaining > 0.
    - Per-item card gains two new pills next to the item name: **"Fully Returned"** (gray, when `qty_remaining === 0`) and **"Partial"** (amber, when some was returned but not all). The row itself is rendered with `opacity-60` + gray background when exhausted, and its +/-/input controls are all `disabled`.
    - Sublabel shows `Original: X · Returned: Y · Remaining: Z · Rate $` when any qty has been returned (only "Original: X · Rate $" when none has).
    - New amber banner above the refund-mode section shows "Every item on this invoice has already been returned." when `preview.fully_returned === 1`.
  - [pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx):
    - List view row-end badge column gains an orange **"Returned"** pill with the RotateCcw icon, hover-title shows the count. Rendered when `active_return_count > 0 && !is_return` so only the original shows the badge (not the return doc itself, which has its own "Return" status badge).
    - Card view has the same badge in the same style alongside Merged / Served / Status badges.
    - Right panel header gains the same Returned pill next to the Served / Status badges when a paid invoice has been returned against.
  - **No separate gate on the Return button** — the dialog itself handles the fully-returned case gracefully (`fully_returned` banner + every row's +/- disabled + Submit disabled via `anyPicked = false`). If a cashier clicks Return on a fully-returned invoice, they open the dialog, see the banner, close it. Trading one extra click for a simpler state machine.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 692.72 → 694.77 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user walks the matrix. (a) return 2 of 5 on item Fanta → see "Returned" badge on SAF00012 in the list → reopen return dialog → Fanta shows "Partial" pill + `Original: 5 · Returned: 2 · Remaining: 3`, stepper capped at 3. (b) return another 2 → reopen → Fanta shows `Remaining: 1`, stepper capped at 1. (c) return the last 1 → reopen → Fanta is "Fully Returned", grayed out, stepper disabled. (d) all items fully returned → reopen → "Every item has been returned" banner shows. (e) Undo Return the last one → reopen → Remaining jumps back up, badge disappears on the original if no active returns remain.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The Returned badge counts are the result of a correlated subquery per row.** For big Orders lists (hundreds of rows) this is one scalar subquery per row — noticeable but not painful under typical POS loads. If it ever shows up in profiling, it could be replaced with a LEFT JOIN + GROUP BY. Not worth preemptively optimizing.
  - **Cancelled returns are excluded via `docstatus=1` filter.** So a cashier can over-return, Undo the return, and try again — the capacity is fully restored. This was the original design intent (returns should be fully reversible) and falls out of the query filter naturally.
  - **The `pos_invoice_item` back-ref is set by ERPNext's `make_return_doc`** at line 621 of `sales_and_purchase_return.py`. Without that back-ref, the returned-qty lookup wouldn't work because there'd be no way to correlate a child row on the return doc with its source row on the original. URY never touches this field, so the back-ref is reliable.
  - **The 1e-6 tolerance on qty comparisons** handles the edge case where `original_qty - already_returned` involves repeated float subtraction (e.g. original=5, return 1.333, return 1.333 → remaining = 2.334000...01 or similar). Without the tolerance, a seemingly-exact final return would be rejected. Not relevant to integer qtys but cheap insurance for decimal-qty items.
  - **No schema changes this round** — the new behavior rides entirely on ERPNext's native `is_return`, `return_against`, `docstatus`, and `pos_invoice_item` fields. The `custom_restrict_returns_to_captain` field from the first round is still the only URY-side schema artifact the return feature depends on.

### 2026-04-10 — `validate_invoice` crashed first return with "POS Invoice <autoname> not found"
- **Symptom:** First test of the new Return Orders feature. User picked paid invoice `SAF00012`, opened the return dialog, selected an item, hit Process Return. Backend threw `DoesNotExistError: POS Invoice SAF00013 not found` with title "Message". The error name (SAF00013) was exactly +1 from the invoice being returned, which made it look like a naming-off-by-one bug. It wasn't.
- **Root cause:** `validate_invoice` in [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py) has an item-removal guard: if `invoice_printed == 1 && remove_items == 0`, it loads the "original" items via `frappe.get_doc("POS Invoice", doc.name)` and diff-checks them against the in-memory doc to block removals on already-printed invoices. The hook assumes it's always running on an UPDATE — it never checks `doc.is_new()`. Returns are the first flow that creates a fresh POS Invoice with `invoice_printed=1` pre-set (because `erpnext.controllers.sales_and_purchase_return.make_return_doc` copies ALL fields from the source, including `invoice_printed`). So on insert:
  1. `make_return_doc` builds the return doc in-memory with `invoice_printed=1` inherited from source.
  2. `return_doc.insert()` → autoname assigns the next SAF name (`SAF00013`) → before_insert hooks fire → DB row created → validate hook fires.
  3. `validate_invoice` sees `invoice_printed == 1`, enters the guard branch.
  4. `frappe.get_doc("POS Invoice", doc.name)` tries to load `SAF00013` from the DB — but the insert hasn't committed yet, **the hook is running DURING the save transaction, not after it**. The row isn't queryable as a separate doc yet at the validate stage.
  5. Frappe raises `DoesNotExistError` with the standard `"POS Invoice {name} not found"` message. Transaction rolls back. User sees the cryptic "+1" error.
- **Fix:** [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py) `validate_invoice` now early-returns when `doc.is_new() or doc.is_return`. The item-removal guard is conceptually an UPDATE-only check — there's no "original" state to diff against on a fresh insert, and returns inherit the source's items wholesale anyway (the whole concept of "item removal on a return" doesn't make sense). Comment in the code explaining why so future-me doesn't strip the guard.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_invoice.py` — OK.
  - **No frontend changes** — backend-only, no rebuild needed.
  - **Still to do:** user re-tries the return flow. Expects: successful return creation, return invoice visible under the Return status filter, Undo Return button works.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **This was a latent bug in existing URY code, not a regression introduced by the return feature.** Any future flow that creates a POS Invoice with `invoice_printed=1` pre-set (return docs, re-imports, data migrations, future features) would have hit the same trap. Fixing it once here covers all those cases.
  - **The `validate_invoice_print` hook at line 128 uses `frappe.db.get_value`**, not `frappe.get_doc`. `get_value` returns None for a missing row instead of throwing, so that hook doesn't have the same trap. Left unchanged.
  - **`restrict_existing_order` (before_insert)** does `frappe.db.exists("POS Invoice", {restaurant_table, docstatus: 0, invoice_printed: 0})`. Returns inherit `invoice_printed = 1` from the source, so this filter doesn't match on a return doc. Also safe.
  - **Lesson (again):** URY's doc-event hooks were written assuming exactly one insert path (cashier creates draft via the POS) and exactly one update path (cashier edits/submits the draft). Any hook that blindly calls `frappe.get_doc(..., doc.name)` inside validate/before_save is a time bomb for any future insert path with non-default field values. The `doc.is_new()` check should become a reflex when writing these.

### 2026-04-10 — Split Bill (per-payer) + Return Orders (partial + reversible)
- **Two features bundled** because they both touch the Payment / post-Payment flow on the Orders page. Design decisions up-front with the user: (a) split per-payer, not per-item; (b) split allows per-payer mode of payment (one pays cash, another card); (c) returns default captain-only but can be flipped OFF per profile to let cashiers return their own; (d) returns use ERPNext's native `make_return_doc` + `cancel()` so we inherit all the accounting gymnastics for free.
- **Split Bill — [pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/PaymentDialog.tsx) rewrite:**
  - New "Split Bill" toggle at the top of the dialog. When OFF, the existing per-mode input grid shows (unchanged behavior). When ON, the grid is replaced with a per-payer card stack.
  - Two split modes:
    - **Equal**: enter N (2–10 payers) via +/- stepper; amounts auto-compute via `distributeEqually(total, n)` which uses cent-safe math — `Math.floor(totalCents / n)` base with the remainder distributed across the first K payers so no one gets more than 1¢ over the base. Sum always equals the total exactly. Amount inputs are read-only in Equal mode.
    - **Custom**: per-payer amount input is editable. Each payer row has a "fill remaining" icon that snaps the amount to `total - sum(others)`. A "Reset to even split" button snaps all rows back to equal. Live balance bar at the bottom shows collected vs total with a colour-coded progress fill (blue filling → green balanced → yellow over).
  - Each payer card has its own Mode of Payment dropdown (radix Select). Cashiers can mix cash/card/etc per payer.
  - Submit button disabled until `splitBalanced` (within 1¢ tolerance) AND every payer has a non-zero amount.
  - On submit, `collapsePayers()` groups payer rows by mode into the POS Invoice native `payments[]` child table (so two payers both paying Cash become a single "Cash $X" row on the desk side, keeping the doctype clean). The split is a cashier-side UX concept only — no backend schema changes needed.
  - Right-column order summary gains a "Split Breakdown" card listing each payer's share + their chosen mode when split is enabled. Acts as a receipt preview.
  - Existing `sumAmounts(...)` helper uses round-to-cent math to prevent JS float drift on balance checks (a classic gotcha with e.g. `0.1 + 0.2 !== 0.3`).
- **Return Orders — new backend + new frontend component:**
  - **Schema:** new `POS Profile.custom_restrict_returns_to_captain` Check field, **default 1 (captain-only)**. Registered in BOTH `custom_field.json` + `setup.py` + `hooks.py` fixtures (dual-source-of-truth).
  - **Backend ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py))** gains three whitelisted methods + one helper:
    - `_user_can_return_orders(pos_profile_name)` — returns `(can_return, is_captain)`. Captain/Manager/Admin always pass. Cashiers pass only when the profile's `custom_restrict_returns_to_captain` is 0. The helper defaults the restrict flag to 1 when the field isn't set (fresh install hasn't migrated yet), matching the schema default.
    - `get_return_preview(invoice)` — validates the invoice is a submitted, paid, non-return POS Invoice that the caller has permission to return. Returns `{invoice, customer, customer_name, grand_total, currency, items, payments}` where `items[]` is a per-row list with `row_name` (the POS Invoice Item row's `name`) for correlation back to the source on submit.
    - `create_pos_return(invoice, items, refund_mode, notes)` — uses **ERPNext's `make_return_doc("POS Invoice", invoice)` as the base doc**, then filters the auto-generated items down to just the rows the cashier picked (matching on `pos_invoice_item` back-ref), sets each picked row's qty to the NEGATIVE of the cashier's selected qty, clears the auto-generated payments and appends a single refund row using the chosen `refund_mode`, runs `calculate_taxes_and_totals()` to compute the refund amount, sets `paid_amount`, and does `insert() + submit()`. ERPNext handles the stock reversal, negative tax rows, and GL entries automatically. ValidationErrors propagate cleanly.
    - `reverse_pos_return(return_invoice)` — validates the doc is a submitted return invoice and the caller has permission, then calls ERPNext's native `doc.cancel()`. Cancel reverses all GL + stock entries. No custom audit doctype needed (unlike merge, which needed a Merge Log because the master/source relationship isn't native).
  - `getPosProfile()` now returns `custom_restrict_returns_to_captain` in its response (defaults to 1 when the column is null) so the frontend can gate the button without an extra round-trip.
  - **Frontend helpers ([pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts))**: new `ReturnPreview`, `ReturnPreviewItem`, `ReturnPreviewPayment`, `ReturnResult`, `ReverseReturnResult` types + `getReturnPreview`, `createPosReturn`, `reversePosReturn` API functions.
  - **Role helper ([pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts))**: new `canReturnOrders(user, posProfile)`. Captains/managers/admins always pass. Cashiers pass only when `posProfile.custom_restrict_returns_to_captain === 0`. The helper treats `undefined` as restricted (matching the schema default).
  - **New component — [pos/src/components/ReturnDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/ReturnDialog.tsx):**
    - State machine: `loading → form → submitting → success` with an `error` state for preview fetch failures or submit-time ValidationErrors.
    - Form body renders each item as a card with a +/− qty stepper (bounded to `[0, original_qty]`), a numeric input, and a live line total. Selected rows get an orange accent border. Two shortcut buttons at the top: "Return All" (sets every row to its max) and "Clear".
    - Below the items: a Refund Mode of Payment dropdown pre-seeded with the original invoice's first payment mode (or POS default, or first available), plus an optional free-text notes field.
    - Footer has a live "Refund total" and a large orange "Process Return" button. Disabled until `anyPicked`.
    - On success: shows a green CheckCircle2 with the return invoice name + refund amount for ~900ms, then calls `onReturned(result)` so the parent can close + reload.
  - **Orders.tsx wiring:**
    - Imports `canReturnOrders` + `reversePosReturn` + `ReturnDialog`.
    - New state: `showReturnDialog`, `reverseLoading`. New memo `canReturn`. New helper `isReturnDoc(order)` that checks `status === 'Return'` OR `is_return === 1`.
    - Sticky bottom bar gains TWO new conditional buttons alongside the existing Payment:
      - **Return**: shown when `canReturn && (status === 'Paid' || status === 'Consolidated') && !isReturnDoc(selectedOrder)`. Opens the ReturnDialog for the selected invoice.
      - **Undo Return**: shown when `canReturn && isReturnDoc(selectedOrder)`. Calls `reversePosReturn` directly with a loading spinner. Errors propagate through `extractFrappeServerError` → `showToast.error({title, description})`.
    - Post-return success triggers `fetchOrders()` + `clearSelectedOrder()` so the list refreshes and the right panel resets.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `ury/ury_pos/api.py`, `ury/hooks.py`, `ury/setup.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on `ury/fixtures/custom_field.json` — OK.
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 674.76 → 692.72 kB (most of the +18kB is the new ReturnDialog component + Select dropdown wiring in PaymentDialog).
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` to install the new POS Profile field, then walks the matrix.
    - **Split:** open a draft order → Payment → toggle Split Bill → pick Equal, N=4, verify amounts sum exactly to total → pick Custom, type $50 on payer 1, click "fill remaining" on payer 2 → verify balance bar turns green → change per-payer mode to Cash + Card + Card → Submit → single invoice with two Cash rows collapsed to one, plus the Card row.
    - **Return (as captain):** pick a paid invoice → Return button visible → open dialog → pick partial qty on one item → Process Return → success → switch sidebar to Return filter → new return invoice visible → right panel shows Undo Return → click → reversed → stock restored.
    - **Return (as cashier, restricted):** default profile has `custom_restrict_returns_to_captain = 1` → Return button hidden. Flip the checkbox OFF on the profile → save → Return button appears for the cashier's own paid orders.
    - **Return (full):** pick a paid invoice → Return All → Process → refund equals grand_total.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **No custom return audit doctype.** ERPNext's `is_return` + `return_against` + the automatic Version history on the source invoice give us everything we need. The merge feature needed a custom log because the master/source relationship isn't native; returns have native ERPNext semantics so reusing them is cleaner.
  - **`make_return_doc` uses `get_mapped_doc` under the hood** — it pre-populates the return with every item, then we filter down to what the cashier picked. The filtering happens AFTER the helper runs because the helper sets up the `pos_invoice_item` back-refs we need to correlate rows.
  - **Single refund mode per return** is a deliberate simplification (and matches the user's direction). If a customer paid $100 cash + $50 card originally and wants $50 back, the cashier picks one mode — they refund either $50 cash OR $50 card, not split. Matches how real cash drawers actually work.
  - **Cent-safe distribution in the split dialog** is non-negotiable for monetary math. `total / n` via float division would introduce half-cent drift that accumulates into visible rounding errors. `distributeEqually` works in integer cents, floors the base, and distributes the remainder across the first K payers — a classic pattern. The sum is provably exact.
  - **Collapsing payer rows by mode on submit** keeps the POS Invoice's payments child table clean in the desk view. If two people both paid cash, the desk sees one "Cash $X" row. Audit-wise this means the per-payer attribution is NOT preserved on the invoice itself — only in the cashier's moment-of-payment view. Acceptable trade-off because the invoice is one document representing one sale; "who paid what" isn't a concept the POS Invoice doctype has slots for.
  - **The Return button's orange theming** is deliberate — it's visually distinct from the blue Payment button and the amber Undo Return button, so cashiers can't accidentally mix up the three destructive-ish actions at a glance.
  - **Split and Return are orthogonal.** A cashier can split-pay a new order AND later return part of it. The return flow doesn't know or care how the original was split-paid — it just reads the final `payments[]` on the source invoice for the refund-mode default hint.
  - **`custom_restrict_returns_to_captain`'s default is 1 (restricted)** while the merge equivalent (`custom_restrict_merge_to_captain`) defaults to 0. This mirrors the user's explicit ask — "restrict to only Captain for the return but the splitting any cashier can do". Merge is a draft-only operation and can always be unmerged; returns are money-out-of-the-drawer and stock reversal, so the conservative default is right.

### 2026-04-10 — Proactive "Outdated POS Opening Entry" detection at POS load
- **Symptom:** After shipping the `make_invoice` unwrap (same day, entry below), the user tapped Payment on a draft order and finally saw a clean ERPNext message in the toast — *but* the new message was a different bug: `POS Opening Entry - POS-OPE-2026-00003 is outdated. Please close the POS and create a new POS Opening Entry.` The opening entry was created on an earlier day; ERPNext's `validate_pos_opening_entry` ([sales_invoice.py:1200](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py#L1200)) hard-throws whenever `frappe.utils.get_date_str(opening_entries[0].period_start_date) != frappe.utils.today()`. The unwrap fix actually worked — the user now saw the real error text — but the underlying lockout needed a proactive dialog, not just a cleaner error toast.
- **Root cause:** ERPNext's check is a straight date comparison against the site's `today()`. URY had no frontend guard for it. Two near-adjacent guards existed but neither fired:
  1. `ShiftHoursBanner` polls the current entry but is gated on `custom_shift_hours > 0` — when shift hours are disabled (default), the polling loop doesn't even start, so an entry from yesterday produces no banner.
  2. `POSOpeningProvider` only checks `custom_daily_pos_close` (a separate opt-in rule) and the `checkPOSOpening` "is there an open entry for this terminal?" gate. An entry from yesterday passes both checks cleanly because it IS open and IS on this terminal.
  So nothing caught the universal ERPNext rule, and the cashier only found out at Payment time — after building a full order.
- **Files changed:**
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx) — added a new `outdatedEntry` state slot alongside the existing `validation` state. After the open-check + daily-close-check both pass, calls `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminalName)` and compares `entry.period_start_date.slice(0, 10)` against a local-timezone "YYYY-MM-DD" for today. When the entry's date is older than today, skips the normal POS render and instead renders a full-screen wrapper containing (a) a red "Outdated POS Opening Entry" banner explaining why, with the entry's start date, and (b) `<POSClosingDialog>` pre-wired to the outdated entry's name. Both `onCancel` and `onClosed` reload the page — reload re-runs the check, so cancelling a closing submission lands back on this same screen (effectively "can't escape" without closing the shift). Fetch failure on `getCurrentPosOpenEntry` is non-fatal — if the lookup fails, the provider falls through to the normal POS render so the Payment-time error still surfaces as a safety net.
  - New helper `localToday()` at the top of the provider file. Computes "YYYY-MM-DD" from `new Date()` using local-timezone getters so the string comparison matches Frappe's site-tz `period_start_date` exactly (as long as the POS device is in the same tz as the Frappe site, the normal case).
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 672.55 kB → 674.71 kB.
  - **Still to do:** user hard-reloads the POS on a day after the outdated entry was created. Expects: red "Outdated POS Opening Entry" banner + the closing dialog pre-loaded with `POS-OPE-2026-00003`'s payment rows. Counts cash/card, submits close → page reloads → opening dialog appears → starts a new shift → can finally ring orders. Also verify: clicking the dialog's X or Cancel reloads the page (and re-fires the check → lands right back on this screen) instead of silently dumping into a broken POS.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Why proactive at POS load, not reactive at submit time?** The error happens on `invoice.save()` during `validate_pos_opening_entry` — that means the cashier has already built the entire order (items, discounts, customer, payments) before the error fires. A reactive approach would pop the close dialog at Payment time but the user's work context is mid-payment, not "start a shift". The proactive load-time check catches it before any order work starts, which is the right UX for a "can't use the POS at all until you close the shift" state.
  - **The date comparison is string-based** (`"2026-04-09" < "2026-04-10"`) which is safe because both sides are ISO-8601 date strings. No timezone arithmetic, no Date parsing surprises. The only edge case is if the POS device's clock timezone differs from the Frappe site's — a real-world mismatch would produce either a false positive (banner fires when the entry is actually today's) or a false negative (banner misses a truly outdated entry and Payment fails with the ERPNext error). Acceptable for normal restaurant operations where the POS device and the server are in the same location.
  - **The Shift Hours banner is unchanged.** Its scope is still "shift length elapsed for a profile that opted into shift hours". The date-mismatch check in POSOpeningProvider is orthogonal and universal — fires on EVERY profile, regardless of shift_hours setting. Both can fire together on the same reload: if shift_hours is set AND the entry is from yesterday, the provider's stale-date check wins (it's hit first and short-circuits the render). The banner is only reachable when the POS has actually loaded.
  - **`POSClosingDialog.onCancel` reloads.** This is deliberate — the dialog's X button and Cancel button both trigger `onCancel`, and on this code path, reloading means "re-fire the check", which immediately re-opens this same dialog. There's no "dismiss and use the POS" path because ERPNext will reject any invoice until the entry is closed. If a future feature adds a "close shift without counting the drawer" shortcut, the cancel path can be rewired.
  - **Cross-reference with the existing `Existing Entry` branch in POSOpeningDialog.** That branch handles a DIFFERENT ERPNext check: `check_open_pos_exists` blocks NEW opening entries when another profile-scoped entry is already open. This fix handles the "entry is open but stale" case. Both guards are needed.

### 2026-04-10 — Negative-stock toggle on POS Profile + payment-error unwrapping
