- **Symptom:** Tapping Payment on a draft order surfaced `frappe.exceptions.ValidationError: Error while settling order: Row #1: Item Fanta has no stock in warehouse Finished Goods - BB.` in DevTools, but the React POS toast showed only "There was an error." Two separate problems stacked: (1) the error itself — ERPNext's POS Invoice stock validator wouldn't let stock-tracked items go negative; (2) the UI couldn't show the real ERPNext message because `make_invoice` wrapped `invoice.submit()` in `try/except: frappe.throw(f"Error while settling order: {e}")`, mangling the original ValidationError into a plain re-raise that lost the title, indicator, and `raise_exception` flag — exactly the same anti-pattern previously fixed on `sync_order` (see 2026-04-08 entry). Cashier wanted a per-profile escape hatch they could toggle at runtime instead of having to manually flip `Item.allow_negative_stock` on every menu item by hand.
- **Schema:**
  - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) — new `POS Profile.custom_allow_negative_stock_on_menu_items` Check field, default 0, inserted after `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain`. Registered in BOTH `custom_field.json` AND `setup.py` so existing installs pick it up via `after_migrate` calling `create_custom_fields` (the dual-source-of-truth rule).
- **Backend changes:**
  - [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_profile.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_profile.py) — new `on_update(doc, method)` orchestrator + new `sync_negative_stock_on_menu_items(doc, method)` handler. The handler is gated by `doc.has_value_changed("custom_allow_negative_stock_on_menu_items")` so resaving the profile for unrelated reasons is a no-op. When the field changes: (a) collect every URY Menu name reachable from the profile's restaurant — `URY Restaurant.active_menu` + `menu_for_room[].menu` + `order_type_menu[].menu`, all unioned into a set so duplicates are dropped; (b) pull every distinct `Item` linked from any URY Menu Item under those menus; (c) filter to `is_stock_item = 1` (so service items / non-stock items are skipped even if they're on a menu); (d) bulk-update `Item.allow_negative_stock` via `frappe.db.set_value(..., update_modified=False)` to the new value (1 or 0). Bypasses `Item.validate()` deliberately — we don't need item validation just to toggle one Check field, and `update_modified=False` keeps the modified timestamp on the Item masters from being touched. Surfaces a blue alert msgprint with the count of items + menus updated so the admin gets confirmation. New helper `_collect_menus_for_restaurant(restaurant_name)` returns the union set; tolerates missing fields so a misconfigured restaurant doesn't crash the save.
  - [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) `doc_events` — POS Profile gains `on_update: ury.ury.hooks.ury_pos_profile.on_update` alongside the existing `validate` hook.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) `make_invoice` — removed the `try/except: frappe.throw(f"Error while settling order: {e}")` wrap around `invoice.submit()`. ValidationErrors now propagate cleanly so the React POS's `extractFrappeServerError()` helper can pick out the title and render an actionable rich toast. Long source comment left in place explaining why so future-me doesn't reintroduce the wrap. Anti-pattern grep across `ury/` confirms no other live instances remain (only references in CLAUDE.md and this same file's comments).
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_profile.py`, `ury/setup.py`, `ury/hooks.py`, `ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on `ury/fixtures/custom_field.json` — OK.
  - **No frontend changes** — backend-only round, no `yarn build` needed.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (creates the new POS Profile field via the fixtures + setup.py paths and wires the new doc_event), then walks the matrix: (a) try to pay an order with an out-of-stock item with `custom_allow_negative_stock_on_menu_items` OFF — sees the real "Item Out of Stock" error in the toast (not the generic wrapper); (b) flip the toggle ON and save the POS Profile — sees the blue alert "Updated allow_negative_stock = 1 on N stock-tracked items across M menus"; (c) re-try the payment — succeeds; (d) flip the toggle OFF and save — sees the alert with count, then re-try the payment — sees the stock error again; (e) save the POS Profile for unrelated reasons (e.g. tweaking printer settings) — no alert fires because `has_value_changed` returns false.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **`Item.allow_negative_stock` is a per-Item flag on the Item master**, not a per-POS-Profile property. Two POS Profiles sharing the same Item will fight: whichever one was saved last wins. This is documented in the field description and in the function docstring. Acceptable for the user's setup (one branch typically has one POS Profile per terminal but they all share the same restaurant menu).
  - **The "off" path stamps a literal `0`**, not NULL — so an admin who manually flipped `allow_negative_stock` on an item for some other reason (e.g. perishables that should always be allowed to go negative) will get it cleared the next time the toggle is flipped off. The toggle is meant to be the source of truth for menu items; admins who want per-item exceptions should remove those items from URY Menu before flipping the global toggle.
  - **Items shared between menus get one flip, not many.** The `Item.name in (set)` filter dedupes by item code before the loop, so an item appearing on both the dine-in menu and a takeaway menu only gets one `set_value` call.
  - **Bulk update bypasses `Item.validate()`** intentionally. We're toggling one Check field on N items; running the full Item validate cycle on every one would be slow and could hit unrelated validation errors that would block the toggle from completing across the rest of the items. `update_modified=False` also keeps the modified timestamp from changing, so admins inspecting Item history don't see a flurry of "modified" events from a profile save.
  - **The msgprint alert shows the COUNT of stock items, not the names** to keep the toast short. If an admin needs to audit which items got toggled, they can query `Item` filtered by `name in (URY Menu Item linked from these menus) AND is_stock_item = 1 AND allow_negative_stock = <target>`.
  - **`make_invoice` unwrap is the third instance of the same anti-pattern fix.** First was `sync_order` on 2026-04-08, second was the install/migrate `setup()` swallow on 2026-04-08, this is the third. Lesson: any new whitelisted method that wraps an ERPNext primitive (`save()`, `submit()`, `cancel()`) should let the primitive's exceptions propagate cleanly. The frontend already knows how to parse them.
  - **`on_update` runs AFTER `validate`** in Frappe's lifecycle, so by the time the negative-stock sync fires the field's new value is already on the doc. `has_value_changed` reports the diff against the doc's pre-save state (or returns True for new docs), which is exactly what we need.

### 2026-04-09 — Orders page right section: print restriction + Paid filter + Merge Orders feature
- **Three asks bundled into one round** because they all touch the Orders page right section / sidebar.
  1. **Print restriction:** cashier can print an invoice once; after that the print icon disappears for them. URY Captain / URY Manager / Administrator can always re-print.
  2. **"Paid" status filter:** the sidebar didn't show "Paid" by default. Moved from EXTENDED (gated by `view_all_status` flag) to BASE so cashiers see it unconditionally. Consolidated and Return stay in EXTENDED.
  3. **Merge Orders feature:** select 2+ draft orders, merge into one master with full audit log and one-click rollback. Per-POS-Profile setting controls whether cashiers can merge or only captains.
- **Schema changes:**
  - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) — two new custom fields:
    - `POS Invoice.custom_merged_into` (Link → POS Invoice, read-only, in_standard_filter). Set when an invoice has been merged into a master; the Orders page list filters out invoices that have this set.
    - `POS Profile.custom_restrict_merge_to_captain` (Check, default 0). When 1, only captains/managers/admins can use the Merge Orders button.
  - Both registered in BOTH `custom_field.json` AND `setup.py` so the dual-source-of-truth rule is satisfied — existing installs get the fields on next `bench migrate`.
- **New DocTypes:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order_merge_log/](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order_merge_log/) — `URY Order Merge Log` (standalone, naming `MERGE-{YYYY}-{MM}-{DD}-{####}`). Fields: `master_invoice`, `branch`, `custom_terminal`, `merged_at`, `merged_by`, `merged_by_full_name`, `notes`, `status` (Active/Unmerged), `unmerged_at`, `unmerged_by`, `master_pre_merge_items_json` (Long Text — snapshot of master.items BEFORE the merge so unmerge can restore them losslessly), `source_invoices` child table.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order_merge_source/](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order_merge_source/) — `URY Order Merge Source` (child, `istable: 1`). Per-source snapshot: `source_invoice`, `original_status`, `original_grand_total`, `original_customer`, `original_customer_name`, `original_restaurant_table`, `original_items_json`, `original_payments_json`. The JSON blobs preserve enough state to restore the source on unmerge.
  - Permissions: System Manager + URY Manager + URY Captain + URY Cashier all get read+write+create on the log. Only System Manager + URY Manager can delete.
- **Backend changes ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - New `merge_pos_invoices(invoices, notes=None)` whitelisted method. Validates: same branch, same terminal, all `docstatus=0` and `status="Draft"`, none already merged. First invoice in the list becomes the master. Snapshots master.items into `master_pre_merge_items_json`. For each source: snapshots its items+payments+customer+table into a `URY Order Merge Source` row, appends source items to master, frees the source's restaurant_table (if any), stamps `source.custom_merged_into = master.name`, saves source. Saves master once at the end so totals get recomputed once. Inserts the merge log. Returns `{merge_log, master_invoice, merged_count}`.
  - New `unmerge_pos_invoices(merge_log)` whitelisted method. Validates: log is in `Active` status, master is still unpaid (Draft + docstatus 0), caller can merge on this profile. Restores `master.items` from the snapshot. For each source: clears `custom_merged_into`, re-occupies the source's table (if any), saves. Marks log `status=Unmerged`, sets `unmerged_at` + `unmerged_by`. Returns `{merge_log, master_invoice, unmerged_count}`.
  - New `get_active_merge_log_for_invoice(invoice)` whitelisted method. Returns the most recent Active merge log where the given invoice is the master, or None. Used by the right panel to decide whether to show the Unmerge button.
  - New `_user_can_merge_orders(pos_profile)` helper. Returns `(can_merge, is_captain)`. Captains/managers/admins can always merge. Cashiers can merge only when the profile's `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain` is OFF. Same logic gates the frontend button via `canMergeOrders`.
  - New `_serialise_invoice_items` / `_serialise_invoice_payments` / `_restore_invoice_items` helpers for the JSON snapshot/restore round-trip.
  - `getPosInvoice` and `searchPosInvoice` now exclude rows where `custom_merged_into IS NOT NULL` so merged sources don't clutter the orders list. Master invoices (which never have this set) remain visible.
  - `getPosProfile()` returns `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain` so the frontend can gate the merge button without an extra round-trip.
- **Backend permissions:**
  - [ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py) `WRITE_DOCTYPES` gains `URY Order Merge Log` (read+write+create). The next `bench migrate` adds the Custom DocPerm rows so URY Cashier / URY Captain users can read and create merge logs without needing extra ERPNext roles.
- **Translations:** [ury/translations/en.csv](ury/translations/en.csv) — added `URY Order Merge Log → ExPOS Order Merge Log` and `URY Order Merge Source → ExPOS Order Merge Source` so the desk shows the rebrand.
- **Frontend changes:**
  - [pos/src/data/order-types.ts](pos/src/data/order-types.ts) — moved "Paid" from `EXTENDED_ORDER_STATUS_TYPES` to `BASE_ORDER_STATUS_TYPES`. Cashiers now see Paid in the sidebar by default. `view_all_status` flag still gates Consolidated and Return.
  - [pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts) — two new helpers:
    - `canReprintInvoice(user)` — Admin / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain. Used by the right panel to decide whether to show the print button after `invoice_printed=1`.
    - `canMergeOrders(user, posProfile)` — captains always can; cashiers can only when `posProfile.custom_restrict_merge_to_captain !== 1`. Used to show/hide the Merge Orders button.
  - [pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts) — `PosProfileLimited` and `PosProfileCombined` types gain `custom_restrict_merge_to_captain`.
  - [pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts) — `POSInvoice` type gains `custom_merged_into`. New `ActiveMergeLog`, `MergeResult`, `UnmergeResult` types. New API helpers: `mergeOrders`, `unmergeOrders`, `getActiveMergeLogForInvoice`.
  - [pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts) — new state: `mergeMode`, `mergeSelection`, `mergeLoading`, `selectedOrderMergeLog`. New actions: `enterMergeMode`, `exitMergeMode`, `toggleMergeSelection`, `mergeSelectedOrders(notes?)`, `unmergeOrder(mergeLogName)`. `selectOrder` now also fetches the active merge log for the selected invoice (fire-and-forget — doesn't gate selection if the lookup fails). Server-error parsing in the merge/unmerge actions surfaces the friendly Frappe message instead of a generic catchall.
  - [pos/src/components/ui/merge-mode.css](pos/src/components/ui/merge-mode.css) — new file with the `ury-wobble` keyframe (~0.6° rotate, 450ms cycle, 1px Y-axis bob) plus 6 staggered delay classes so a grid of cards doesn't pendulum-sync. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - [pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) — large additions:
    - **Top toolbar split:** when `mergeMode = false`, the existing toolbar shows a new **"Merge Orders"** button (only when `canMergeOrders` returns true) before the Card/List toggle. When `mergeMode = true`, the toolbar is replaced with an amber **Merge Mode bar** showing "X selected" + Cancel + Merge buttons (Merge disabled until ≥ 2 selected).
    - **Card view:** in merge mode, mergeable cards (Draft + not already merged) get the `ury-wobble` class with a delay-stagger based on grid index, and clicking them toggles selection (instead of opening the right panel). Non-mergeable cards (Paid, Consolidated, Return, etc.) are dimmed (`opacity-40`) and not clickable. Selected-for-merge cards get an amber ring + a checkmark badge in the top-right corner.
    - **List view:** same logic but on table rows. Selected rows get `bg-amber-50` and an inline checkmark icon next to the order name.
    - **Right panel — Unmerge banner:** when `selectedOrderMergeLog` is set AND `selectedOrder.status === 'Draft'`, an amber banner appears just above the sticky bottom row showing "Merged from N orders by <user>" + an **Unmerge** button. Click → calls `unmergeOrder` → reload + toast.
    - **Right panel — print restriction:** the print button is now wrapped in `(selectedOrder.invoice_printed === 0 || canReprint) && ...`. Cashiers see it for the first print only; captains/managers/admins always see it.
    - **`enterMergeMode`** auto-clears the right panel selection because the right-panel context doesn't make sense in merge mode.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean. Bundle 665.91 → 672.55 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `api.py`, `permissions.py`, `ury_order_merge_log.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on the new doctype JSONs + custom_field.json — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (creates the two doctypes via the URY module sync, the two custom fields via fixtures+setup.py, and the new permissions baseline row). Then walks the merge matrix: (a) cashier on Bar 1 with restrict-merge OFF — can merge their own draft orders, can unmerge. (b) cashier with restrict-merge ON — sees no Merge Orders button. (c) captain with restrict-merge ON — sees the button, can merge any cashier's orders on the same terminal. (d) try to merge orders from different terminals — backend rejects with "different terminal" error. (e) merge two orders, then try to pay the master — works. (f) try to unmerge after payment — backend rejects with "no longer a draft". (g) print an invoice as cashier — print button disappears after first print. (h) re-login as captain on the same printed invoice — print button visible.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The merge log preserves a JSON snapshot of master + each source.** Storage cost is small (a few KB per merge) and reporting can extract structured fields later if needed. JSON blobs can't be queried directly in MariaDB without `JSON_EXTRACT`, so reports will need to denormalise. Acceptable trade-off vs. building proper child tables for items / payments / etc.
  - **Source invoices are NOT deleted on merge.** They're flagged via `custom_merged_into` and hidden from the Orders page list. They still exist in the desk for audit. KOTs that referenced the source invoice continue to reference it (the source still exists). On unmerge, the flag is cleared and the source becomes active again.
  - **Multi-level merge is unsupported by design.** A source can't itself have `custom_merged_into` set when merging. If you want to merge merge-of-merges, unmerge first.
  - **Cross-terminal merge is banned** for security and reporting cleanliness. Captains can merge across cashiers on the same terminal but never across terminals.
  - **Once the master is paid, the merge is locked in.** `unmerge_pos_invoices` rejects with "Master invoice is no longer a draft. Once an order is paid, the merge can't be reversed." This is the user's explicit constraint.
  - **The merge log is its own audit trail.** Visible in the desk under `/app/ury-order-merge-log`. Reporting will be built later by querying this doctype directly.
  - **The Orders page right panel isn't visible in merge mode** because `enterMergeMode` clears the selected order. If the user clicks Cancel and exits merge mode, they have to re-click an order to see its details. Acceptable — the merge mode is a focused operation.
  - **The wobble animation is gated by `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)`.** Users who have reduced-motion enabled in their OS won't see the wobble; they still see the ring + checkmark when selecting.

### 2026-04-09 — URY Captain hit a 403 on POS Profile + role permission baseline
- **Symptom:** A user logged in with ONLY the `URY Captain` role (no Sales User, no Stock User, no other ERPNext role) loaded `/pos`, registered a terminal, and immediately hit `403 FORBIDDEN` on `GET /api/resource/POS Profile/<name>`. The error: `User <strong>captain@gmail.com</strong> does not have doctype access via role permission for document <strong>POS Profile</strong>`. Same problem applies to URY Cashier — sites that have a "clean" role list (just URY Cashier) hit a wall on the first POS Profile read. Sites that worked were "working by accident" because their cashiers also had `Sales User` or similar generic ERPNext roles.
- **Root cause:** `URY Cashier`, `URY Captain`, and `URY Manager` are URY-specific roles. They have permissions on URY-owned doctypes (URY Restaurant, URY POS Terminal, URY Menu, etc.) via the doctype JSON. But they have **zero permissions on standard ERPNext doctypes** that the React POS reads (POS Profile, POS Settings, POS Invoice, Customer, Item, Item Price, Branch, Currency, Mode of Payment, Sales Taxes and Charges Template, Price List, Company, Customer Group, Territory, POS Opening Entry, POS Closing Entry, URY KOT). Every direct `db.getDoc` and every whitelisted method that touches one of these via `frappe.get_doc` would 403.
- **Files added:**
  - [ury/permissions.py](ury/permissions.py) — new module with `ensure_role_permissions()`. Defines two doctype lists: `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` (POS Profile, URY Restaurant, Item, Branch, etc. — 19 doctypes) and `WRITE_DOCTYPES` (Customer, POS Invoice, POS Opening Entry, POS Closing Entry, URY KOT — read+write+create+submit). Plus a `CAPTAIN_EXTRA_WRITE_DOCTYPES` list for things only captains/managers should be able to do (currently just `cancel` on POS Invoice). Iterates the lists and creates a `Custom DocPerm` row for each `(role, doctype, permlevel=0)` combination that doesn't already exist. **Idempotent:** skips if a built-in DocPerm already grants the role, skips if a Custom DocPerm already exists (so admin tweaks aren't overwritten on every migrate). Returns a `{inserted, skipped}` count summary printed to the bench log.
- **Files changed:**
  - [ury/install.py](ury/install.py) — `after_install` and `after_migrate` now call `ensure_role_permissions()` (wrapped in `_safe_ensure_role_permissions` so a single failure doesn't crash the whole migrate). Uses the same `click.secho` pattern as `ensure_pos_settings_configured` for the error path.
  - [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) — this Fixes log entry; new Gotchas note about URY role permissions.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `permissions.py` and `install.py` — OK.
  - **No frontend changes** — pure backend setup, no `yarn build`.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (which fires `after_migrate` → `ensure_role_permissions`). Output should show something like `[URY] ensure_role_permissions: 60 new perm rows, 6 already existed.` (the 6 already-existing are the URY-owned doctypes whose JSON already grants the URY roles). Then in the incognito captain tab, hard-reload `/pos` — the captain should now sail through terminal selection, see the Orders page, and use the Cashier filter dropdown.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **The "URY Cashier" role only worked before because of accidental extra roles.** Most existing sites have cashiers who are also `Sales User` or have inherited `POS User` somewhere. This bug only surfaces on a clean URY-only role assignment. The new baseline makes URY-only role assignments work correctly going forward.
  - **Skipping existing Custom DocPerm rows is intentional** — admins who deliberately disabled, say, "URY Cashier can cancel POS Invoice" shouldn't have it re-enabled on every migrate. But it does mean: if the baseline list grows and a doctype gets added, sites that already have a (different) Custom DocPerm row for that doctype won't automatically get the new baseline. They'd need to either delete the row or set the missing perm flags manually. Acceptable trade-off.
  - **`merge_with_existing=True` mode** is used for `CAPTAIN_EXTRA_WRITE_DOCTYPES` so the captain's `cancel: 1` flag gets ADDED to their existing read+write+create+submit row from `WRITE_DOCTYPES` instead of creating a duplicate row.
  - **The list does NOT include POS Settings via `frappe.db.get_single_value`** because singletons read directly via `frappe.db.get_single_value` don't enforce DocType permissions. They're still in `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` for the case where the React POS calls `db.getDoc('POS Settings')` directly (it doesn't today, but might in the future).
  - **`URY Manager` is included** in the role list. URY Manager is the URY admin role; under the new baseline they get the same permissions as URY Cashier and URY Captain plus the cancel extra. Most URY Manager users probably also have `System Manager` from ERPNext which trumps everything, but the explicit permissions are belt-and-braces.
  - **The list should grow over time as the React POS adds new API calls.** When you add a new doctype to the React POS, also add it to `READ_ONLY_DOCTYPES` or `WRITE_DOCTYPES`. The next migrate catches all existing sites up.

### 2026-04-09 — `custom_terminal` never stamped on fresh POS Invoices (`invoice.branch is None` race)
- **Symptom:** Even after Fix 1 (defensive null fallback) and Fix 2 (custom field actually exists on the doctype now), every newly-created order STILL had no `custom_terminal` value. The Orders page worked because the null fallback caught untagged orders, but the actual stamping was broken.
- **Diagnosis (one-shot via temporary `frappe.log_error` in `sync_order`):** the diagnostic log showed `terminal_arg='Roof Top' | terminal_branch='Accra' | invoice.branch=None | will_stamp=False`. The frontend was correctly sending `terminal: "Roof Top"`, the URY POS Terminal record had the right branch, but `invoice.branch` was `None` at the moment of comparison.
- **Root cause:** `branch` on POS Invoice is a URY custom field with `fetch_from = "pos_profile.branch"` — it gets populated by ERPNext during `validate()`/`save()`, NOT at the moment we set `invoice.pos_profile = pos_profile`. My terminal-stamping check ran BEFORE `invoice.save()`, so on a freshly-created `frappe.new_doc("POS Invoice")` from `get_order_invoice()`, `invoice.branch` was always `None`. The comparison `terminal_branch == invoice.branch` was always `'Accra' != None`, so the stamp was always silently dropped on every fresh order. Existing-invoice updates worked (because branch was already populated), which is why this never showed up in earlier testing — testers had been editing existing orders, not creating fresh ones.
- **Files changed:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) `sync_order` — terminal stamping now derives `invoice_branch` via `invoice.branch or frappe.db.get_value("POS Profile", pos_profile, "branch")`. The fallback handles the new-invoice case where `branch` hasn't been populated by `fetch_from` yet. The security check (terminal must be on the same branch as the invoice's POS Profile) still works in both paths. The temporary diagnostic `frappe.log_error` was removed in the same edit.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user makes a fresh order → opens it in the desk → confirms the Terminal field is now populated with the device's terminal name (not empty).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Lesson — `fetch_from` fields aren't populated until `validate()`/`save()` runs.** Any time you need to read a `fetch_from` field's value BEFORE save (for a comparison, a hook decision, etc.), you have to fetch it explicitly from the source DocType. The framework only triggers the fetch during the validate phase. This is the third URY bug caused by the same misunderstanding (after `getBranch` Administrator fall-throughs and the `getBranchRoom` IndexError) — they all stem from "trust the field that will be populated later" logic that runs too early.
  - The diagnostic `frappe.log_error` shipped briefly under method title `[URY diag] sync_order terminal stamp`. Removed in the same commit. The Error Log entry the user saw is harmless and can be cleared from `/app/error-log`.
  - Per-user diagnosis cycle: one log message → one root cause → one-line fix. The `frappe.log_error` debugging pattern is a good template for the next time we have an "X is null when it shouldn't be" mystery on the backend.

### 2026-04-09 — Custom field dual-source-of-truth bug + defensive null fallback for the Orders page
- **Symptom:** After the Orders page rewrite shipped, the user logged in, made an order via the React POS, opened the Orders page, and saw zero orders. The order existed in the desk but didn't show on the page. The order's JSON (via `frappe.client.get`) had no `custom_terminal` field at all — not null, not empty, just missing — even though `custom_restaurant_room` (a sibling URY custom field on POS Invoice) was populated correctly.
- **Root cause:** Two separate problems stacked:
  1. **`custom_terminal` doesn't exist on POS Invoice on the user's site.** The field is in `ury/fixtures/custom_field.json` and registered in `hooks.py` `fixtures`, BUT Frappe only imports fixtures on initial app install — `bench migrate` does NOT re-import them (intentional, to prevent overwriting admin customizations). The user installed URY months ago, way before the `custom_terminal` field shipped in commit `edc3d96` (the terminal binding round). So the column never got created on their site. When `sync_order` does `invoice.custom_terminal = terminal`, Frappe silently creates a Python attribute on the doc but **doesn't persist it** because the doctype doesn't know about the column. Failure is silent — no error, no log entry, just a missing field on every order forever.
  2. **The Orders page filter requires `custom_terminal = <terminal>`.** Even if some orders did have a custom_terminal stamp, any order without one (legacy orders, orders created on the broken site) would never show up. The filter was too strict for the migration path.
- **Files changed:**
  - **Fix 1 (immediate, defensive):** [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `getPosInvoice` and `searchPosInvoice` — the `custom_terminal` filter changed from `pi.custom_terminal = %s` to `(pi.custom_terminal = %s OR pi.custom_terminal IS NULL OR pi.custom_terminal = '')`. The branch filter still keeps the scope bounded — an Accra user never sees Tamale orders — but untagged orders within the user's branch now show up on every terminal. This handles legacy orders forever and unblocks the user immediately without requiring a backfill.
  - **Fix 2 (root cause):** [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py) `get_custom_fields()` — added `custom_terminal` to BOTH the POS Invoice list AND the POS Opening Entry list, plus `custom_shift_hours` and `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` on POS Profile (same problem — added in `custom_field.json` only, missing from `setup.py`). The `after_migrate` hook (wired earlier this round) calls `setup()` which calls `create_custom_fields()`, which idempotently creates any fields that don't already exist on the doctype. Existing sites get caught up on their next `bench migrate`.
  - [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) — new Gotcha entry documenting the dual-source-of-truth rule: any custom field added to URY must live in BOTH `custom_field.json` AND `setup.py`, or pre-existing installs silently lose the field.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `setup.py` and `ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - **No frontend changes** — pure backend, no `yarn build` needed.
  - **User confirmed Fix 1 worked:** the order showed up on the Orders page after restarting bench web (with the defensive null filter). Now waiting on `bench migrate` to apply Fix 2 so newer orders get properly stamped going forward.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **`create_custom_fields` is idempotent.** Existing fields on the doctype that already match the `setup.py` definition are no-ops. Existing fields that DIFFER from the definition get updated to match (this is `frappe.custom.doctype.custom_field.custom_field.create_custom_fields` behavior). So adding a field to `setup.py` that's already correct in `custom_field.json` is safe — the second pass on existing sites is a no-op, and on broken sites it creates the missing column.
  - **The 8 historical custom fields in `setup.py` predate the `custom_field.json` migration and have lived there since 2024.** The new `setup.py` entries follow the same shape but include URY-style metadata (`in_standard_filter`, `description`, etc.) so the field renders the same as the fixture version. Worth keeping in mind: the OLDER setup.py entries don't have most of those flags and produce a slightly less polished desk UX for those fields.
  - **Other custom fields added since this user installed URY** that may have the same problem (untested, listed for awareness): `POS Profile.custom_enable_kot_reprint`, the various `custom_*` fields on POS Profile from the multi-cashier era, `POS Opening Entry.custom_rooms`, and several `Branch.*` fields. If any of those bite later, the fix is the same — add them to `setup.py`'s `get_custom_fields()`. For now, only the four fields that are actively load-bearing in current React POS code paths (`custom_terminal` on POS Invoice + POS Opening Entry, `custom_shift_hours`, `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end`) are ported over.
  - The defensive null fallback in Fix 1 stays even after Fix 2 catches up. It's a safety net for any future field-existence-vs-frontend-filter race, and it correctly handles the "this site upgraded mid-day, existing orders are untagged forever" case without forcing a manual SQL backfill.

### 2026-04-09 — Orders page cleanup: today-only by default, per-terminal, per-user, list/card toggle
- **Symptom / motivation:** The Orders page showed every POS Invoice ever created on the user's branch — yesterday's, last week's, every cashier's. Captains and cashiers saw the same firehose. No way to scope to "today, this terminal, my orders" which is what 95% of cashier traffic actually wants. Also no list view — the card grid wasted space when scanning lots of orders.
- **Backend changes ([ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py)):**
  - **`getPosInvoice` rewritten.** Was four near-identical 50-line SQL blocks (Draft / Unbilled / Recently Paid / default), each only differing in the `status` and a small extra-WHERE clause. Refactored to one builder + a `status_map` lookup. New optional params: `terminal`, `posting_date`, `cashier`. The SQL now JOINs to `tabUser` to return `owner_full_name` for the new "Cashier" column. Saves ~80 lines of duplicated SQL and means future filter additions only touch one query.
  - **New helper `_resolve_orders_scope(terminal, cashier)`** — translates the frontend's `cashier` arg into an SQL `owner` clause. Three modes: `"mine"` (default, current user only), `"all"` (all users on this terminal's branch with URY Cashier or URY Captain role — fetched via the URY User child table on Branch), or a specific user id (validated against the same role list). Enforces the privilege check server-side: if the requesting user isn't Administrator / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain, **any non-mine request is silently downgraded to mine**. The HR-rang-an-order edge case is handled by the role filter — only users with URY Cashier/Captain role show up in "all" or in the dropdown.
  - **New helper `_get_cashier_users_on_branch(branch_name)`** + **new whitelisted endpoint `get_cashier_users_for_terminal(terminal)`** — returns the list of cashier users (URY Cashier / URY Captain) attached to the terminal's branch via the URY User child table. Used by the captain's "Cashier" filter dropdown. Returns `[{user, full_name}]`.
  - **`searchPosInvoice` rewritten** — same param shape as `getPosInvoice` (terminal / posting_date / cashier). Honours the same scope rules. Without this, a captain who typed into the search box would get yesterday's orders from another terminal — a hole in the per-terminal model.
  - **`getInvoiceForCashier` collapsed** to a 6-line wrapper around `getPosInvoice`. Was 80 lines of duplicated SQL. Only the legacy Vue POS at `urypos/src/stores/recentOrder.js` still calls it; the wrapper preserves backward compat until the Vue POS is removed (sunset Dec 2025 per README — already past).
- **Frontend changes:**
  - [pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts) — new `canSeeAllTerminalOrders(user)` helper. Same allowed-role list as `canManageMenuPrices` (Administrator / System Manager / URY Manager / URY Captain). Backend re-validates the same list, so this is just a UI hint — cashiers don't see the dropdown at all.
  - [pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts](pos/src/lib/invoice-api.ts) — `POSInvoice` type gains `customer_name`, `custom_terminal`, `owner`, `owner_full_name`, `custom_order_status`. New `CashierUser` type. `getPOSInvoices` and `searchPosInvoice` accept `terminal`, `posting_date`, `cashier`. New `getCashierUsersForTerminal(terminal)` helper.
  - [pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts](pos/src/store/slices/orders-slice.ts) — new state: `selectedDate` (defaults to today's local-iso date), `viewMode` ('card'|'list', defaults to 'card'), `cashierFilter` (defaults to 'mine'), `cashierUsers`, `cashierUsersLoading`. New actions: `setSelectedDate`, `resetSelectedDateToToday`, `setViewMode(mode, userName)` (persists per-user via `storage.setItem('ury_pos_orders_view_mode:' + userName, mode)`), `hydrateViewMode(userName)`, `setCashierFilter`, `fetchCashierUsers`. `fetchOrders` reads `getSavedTerminal()` from localStorage (same source of truth as App.tsx and config-slice — no cross-store dependency) and includes `terminal`, `posting_date`, `cashier` in the request. The slice's `POSInvoice` type was updated to match the new backend response shape (added `customer_name`, `custom_terminal`, `owner`, `owner_full_name`).
  - [pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderStatusSidebar.tsx) — sidebar now has THREE cards: Order Status (existing), Posting Date (new — date input + "Today" reset button, max=today so the user can't pick the future), and Cashier (new, **only rendered when `canSeeAllTerminalOrders(user)`**). The Cashier card is a `<select>` with three groupings: "My Orders" (default), "All Cashiers", and an `<optgroup label="Pick a Cashier">` listing individual users from the new endpoint. Lazy-loads the cashier list once on mount via `fetchCashierUsers`. All three cards share the same `bg-gray-50 border rounded-lg p-4` styling so they look like siblings.
  - [pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx](pos/src/pages/Orders.tsx) — large refactor:
    - **View-mode toolbar** at the top of the orders area: two buttons (LayoutGrid icon for Card, ListIcon for List). Persisted via `setViewMode(..., user.name)`. Hydrated on user-known via `hydrateViewMode(user.name)` so the cashier's preference survives logout/login.
    - **List view** is a clean table: `Order # | Customer | Type/Table | [Cashier] | Time | Total | Status`. The Cashier column only renders when `cashierFilter !== 'mine'` (otherwise every row would say the same name). Click a row → `selectOrder` (same flow as cards). Selected row gets `bg-blue-50` instead of the card-view ring.
    - **Card view** unchanged structurally, but the per-card content now includes a "Cashier" line (with `UserCheck` icon) when `cashierFilter !== 'mine'`. Card view also uses `customer_name` if available (was showing just the customer ID).
    - **`formatDateTime` replaced** with two helpers: `formatOrderDateTime` (shows time-only when date is today, full date+time otherwise) and `formatTimeOnly` (always time-only, used in the list view's Time column). When the user is browsing yesterday's or older orders, the year and date show up; for today's orders the date is implicit and we save space.
    - **Empty state** upgraded from a one-line "No orders found" to: title + body explaining the date/status/terminal scope, plus a "Reset to Today" button that only renders when `selectedDate !== today`.
    - **`showCashierColumn` logic** memoised at the top of the component so both the list-view header and the card-view conditional render share the same flag.
    - **Cleanups (8):** removed three production `console.log` debug statements; fixed `formatDateTime` to handle the "yesterday is ambiguous" case; right-panel customer display now uses `customer_name` (was showing the customer ID); right-panel order-name `<h2>` got a `title=` for full-name hover on truncation; the duplicate `if (error)` block earlier in the file (already removed in a prior round) stayed removed; the dead `getOrderTotal` console.log was removed; the empty state got proper guidance; the per-card type/table line still uses the existing string composition.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 657.23 kB → 665.75 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user walks the Orders page test matrix: (a) cashier login → only sees their own orders, today's date, current terminal; (b) captain login → sees the new Cashier card in the sidebar, defaults to "My Orders" but can flip to "All Cashiers" or pick a specific user; (c) toggle list/card → POS reloads → preference persists; (d) two cashiers logged in on the same terminal with different view-mode prefs → each sees their own preferred view; (e) pick a date in the past via the Posting Date picker → orders for that date show up; (f) "Reset to Today" appears only when the date isn't today and works; (g) search box still respects the same scope (terminal + date + cashier); (h) HR user with no URY Cashier role rings an order somehow (manual desk creation) — the captain's "All Cashiers" filter does NOT show it.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - The Cashier filter dropdown's "All Cashiers" mode fetches `_get_cashier_users_on_branch` server-side every time `getPosInvoice` is called with `cashier="all"`. That's a JOIN per request, not great at scale. For shifts with hundreds of orders this is a few extra ms per call. Could be cached per request via `frappe.local.cache` if it shows up in profiling.
  - **The "specific user" mode validates the user is on the URY Cashier/Captain list of the terminal's branch** — so a captain at branch A can't snoop on a captain at branch B. The validation happens both at the role check AND at the cashier-list lookup; only users in BOTH lists pass.
  - **`viewMode` is persisted with key `ury_pos_orders_view_mode:<user_email>`** — per-user. Different cashiers on the same shared terminal can have different preferences. Note that `user.name` in Frappe is the email, so the key contains the `@`. localStorage handles that fine.
  - **`canSeeAllTerminalOrders` and `canManageMenuPrices` have identical role lists** today. If they ever diverge, split into two helpers — for now they're separate functions for clarity at the call site.
  - The legacy Vue POS still references `getInvoiceForCashier`. The wrapper will keep working until the Vue POS is removed from the tree. When that happens, also drop `getInvoiceForCashier` entirely.
  - The new `formatOrderDateTime` shows the year only when not-today. For January-1st-this-year vs January-1st-last-year ambiguity exists. Acceptable for now; calendar-aware formatting is overkill.
  - **`selectedDate` is stored as a local-timezone ISO date string** (`YYYY-MM-DD`) computed from the browser's `Date()`. If the POS device is in a different timezone than the Frappe site, the "today" boundary may be off by a few hours at midnight. Same caveat as the ShiftHoursBanner. Acceptable for normal restaurant operations.

### 2026-04-09 — In-POS Close Shift dialog (replaces deep-link to desk)
- **Symptom / motivation:** The Existing-Entry branch and the Shift Hours banner both deep-linked to `/app/pos-opening-entry/<name>` in a new tab when the user wanted to close the shift. User wanted feature parity with the opening dialog: a single in-POS UX, no jumping to the desk just to submit a closing entry.
- **Files added:**
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) — two new whitelisted endpoints:
    - `preview_pos_closing_entry(opening_entry)` — wraps ERPNext's `make_closing_entry_from_opening` (in `apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_closing_entry/pos_closing_entry.py`) which already does the heavy lifting of querying invoices in the period and computing expected amounts per payment mode. Returns a slim dict shape: opening_entry, period_start_date, period_end_date, totals, and a `payments` array with `{mode_of_payment, opening_amount, expected_amount, closing_amount}`. The `make_` helper hard-codes `opening_amount=0`, so we override it from the opening entry's `balance_details` child table to keep the dialog's "opening / expected / counted / diff" math correct. `closing_amount` is pre-seeded with `expected_amount` so a one-click close (no edits) just works for the common "drawer matches" case.
    - `submit_pos_closing_entry(opening_entry, closing_amounts)` — accepts a JSON map of `{mode_of_payment: counted_amount}`, runs the same `make_closing_entry_from_opening` to build a fresh draft (no client trust on the totals), applies the user's counted values, computes the difference per row, then `insert()` + `submit()`. Errors propagate as ValidationError so the frontend's standard error pipeline picks them up.
  - [pos/src/lib/pos-closing-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-closing-api.ts) — new module with `previewClosingEntry(openingEntry)` and `submitClosingEntry(openingEntry, closingAmounts)` helpers + the `POSClosingPreview` and `POSClosingPaymentRow` types.
  - [pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSClosingDialog.tsx) — new dialog. State machine: `loading → form → submitting → success`, with an `error` state for the initial preview fetch failing. The form body shows three summary stat cards (invoice count, grand total, net total) above a payment-reconciliation table with one row per mode of payment: read-only opening + expected, editable counted (defaults to expected), and a live-computed colour-coded difference (green when over, red when short, gray when matched). Footer row sums the net difference. Header has Cancel and Close Shift buttons. On submit, the dialog briefly shows a success state ("Shift Closed — Closing Entry: POS-CLOSE-…") before the parent decides what to do (typically `window.location.reload()`).
- **Files changed:**
  - [pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx](pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx) — Close Shift button no longer opens a new tab. Instead it sets `showClosingDialog = true` and renders `<POSClosingDialog>` inline. On `onClosed`, the banner triggers a full page reload so the cleared shift state propagates everywhere.
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx) — the `existing-entry` branch's "Close Existing Entry" button now opens `POSClosingDialog` directly instead of deep-linking. On success, the dialog reloads so the opening flow restarts against a clean profile.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 649.30 kB → 657.09 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user reproduces the existing-entry collision, clicks Close Existing Entry, sees the new dialog with one row per mode (cash, card, etc.) showing opening + expected + editable counted + live diff, edits the counted values to mismatch deliberately, submits, sees the success flash, then sees the opening dialog reload cleanly so they can start a new shift. Same path via the Shift Hours banner: set `custom_shift_hours=1`, backdate the entry, wait for the orange banner, click Close Shift, complete the form.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Both endpoints fully wrap ERPNext's `make_closing_entry_from_opening`** so we don't duplicate the invoice-totals query in URY code. If ERPNext changes the closing logic (taxes, returns, multi-currency), we automatically inherit it. The only thing we override is the hard-coded `opening_amount=0` because URY actually uses opening balances per mode of payment, which most ERPNext POS deployments don't.
  - **No client trust on the closing totals.** The frontend never sends grand_total / net_total / expected amounts back to the server — the backend rebuilds them from invoices on submit. The only client-supplied data is the closing_amount per mode of payment (i.e., what the cashier physically counted in the drawer). Backend recomputes diffs.
  - **Sub POS Closing flow** in [ury/ury/doctype/sub_pos_closing/sub_pos_closing.py](ury/ury/doctype/sub_pos_closing/sub_pos_closing.py) is unchanged. It's part of the deprecated multi-cashier captain model that round 2 of phase-1 dismantled. Eventually we'll either remove it or rebuild it on the new per-terminal model, but it's not in the way of anything right now.
  - **The dialog uses `formatCurrency` from `pos/src/lib/utils.ts`** for amount display. That helper currently uses the `currencySymbol` from localStorage which is set at app boot via `fetchCurrencySymbol`. As long as the user has loaded the POS at least once successfully, the symbol is correct.
  - **`submit_pos_closing_entry` is whitelisted with no role check.** Any user who can hit `/api/method/...` can submit a close on their own session. ERPNext's POS Closing Entry doctype permissions still apply (the user must have create permission on POS Closing Entry), so it's not a wide-open hole — but worth knowing.

### 2026-04-09 — Existing-Entry dialog branch + Shift Hours banner
- **Symptom:** After yesterday's per-terminal scoping shipped, the user logged in today and tried to open a new POS Opening Entry on Bar 1. The dialog showed `**Bar 1** is open. Close the POS or cancel the existing POS Opening Entry to create a new POS Opening Entry.` (with raw `<strong>` HTML rendering as asterisks). Two problems:
  1. **No way out from the dialog.** The error came from ERPNext's `check_open_pos_exists` which filters by `(pos_profile, status=Open)` and ignores terminal scoping entirely. Even with our new per-terminal logic on the URY side, ERPNext's standard validation refuses to create a second open entry for the same profile while another is still open. The dialog had no button to actually do anything about it — the user was stuck.
  2. **No prevention.** The reason the entry was still open was that nobody closed yesterday's shift. The user wanted a configurable shift length per POS Profile so the POS would prompt cashiers to close before this collision happens.
- **Root cause for collision:** ERPNext's [pos_opening_entry.py:64-71](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_opening_entry/pos_opening_entry.py#L64-L71) `check_open_pos_exists` is profile-scoped, not terminal-scoped. URY's per-terminal `posOpening()` correctly returned "no entry on this terminal" → dialog showed → user submitted → ERPNext rejected. The URY backend can't easily override ERPNext's validation since it lives in the parent app's `validate()` method, so the fix is on the frontend: detect this specific error and switch the dialog to a focused "Existing Open Entry" mode with a deep-link to the blocking entry.
- **Schema changes (Fix B):**
  - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) — two new POS Profile fields: `custom_shift_hours` (Int, default 0 = disabled) and `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` (Check, depends_on `custom_shift_hours > 0`).
- **Backend changes:**
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `getPosProfile()` — returns both new shift fields in the response so the frontend can configure the watcher.
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) new `get_pos_open_entry(terminal=None)` whitelisted method. Returns `{name, period_start_date, posting_date, pos_profile, user}` for the currently-open entry, or None. Three lookup modes: (1) per-terminal + per-user (when multi_cashier ON), (2) per-terminal only (when multi_cashier OFF), (3) **fallback by `pos_profile` only** for when the per-terminal/user check finds nothing but ERPNext's profile-scoped check would still block — that's the case Fix A uses to find the blocking entry's name to deep-link to.
- **Frontend changes (Fix A — Existing Entry dialog branch):**
  - [pos/src/lib/pos-opening-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-opening-api.ts) — new `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminal?)` helper wrapping the new whitelisted method.
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx) — `OpeningMode` gets a new `existing-entry` variant with the blocking entry name + the parsed error message. The submit-handler catch block uses `extractFrappeServerError` (so the `<strong>` HTML is stripped automatically) and matches on `parsed.title === 'POS Opening Entry Exists'` OR a regex on the message body (defensive fallback in case the title is translated). When matched, it calls `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminalName)` to look up which entry is blocking and switches the dialog to the new mode. The render branch shows an orange "Existing Open Entry" card with the entry name + a primary button **Close Existing Entry** (deep-link to `/app/pos-opening-entry/<name>` in a new tab) + a secondary **I've Closed It — Reload** button.
- **Frontend changes (Fix B — Shift Hours banner):**
  - [pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-profile-api.ts) — `PosProfileLimited` and `PosProfileCombined` types gain `custom_shift_hours` and `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end`. `getCombinedPosProfile` propagates both through the merge.
  - [pos/src/store/pos-store.ts](pos/src/store/pos-store.ts) — new state fields `shiftExpired: boolean` and `shiftBlocked: boolean`, new action `setShiftExpired(expired, blocked)`. The action no-ops when both flags already match the requested values to prevent re-renders from the 60-second poll timer.
  - [pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx](pos/src/components/ShiftHoursBanner.tsx) — new component. Reads `posProfile.custom_shift_hours` (returns null when 0). Polls `getCurrentPosOpenEntry(terminalName)` once a minute, parses the entry's `period_start_date` (Frappe local datetime → JS Date), computes elapsed hours, and sets the store flags when elapsed >= shift_hours. Renders a sticky orange banner at the top of the POS with a "Close Shift" deep-link button. When the profile's `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` is enabled, the banner turns red and is non-dismissible (load-bearing UI). Otherwise the banner is dismissible per session via an X button.
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx) — mounts `<ShiftHoursBanner />` as a sibling to `children`, so the banner is visible across all POS routes whenever the user is past the opening dialog.
  - [pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) — destructures `shiftBlocked` from the store, blocks `handleSubmit` with a friendly toast when true, and adds `shiftBlocked` to the `isInteractionDisabled` computed value so the order UI grays out cleanly when the hard-block flag is on.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 645.16 kB → 649.30 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile ury/ury_pos/api.py` — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` to apply the two new POS Profile fields. Then walks the matrix: (a) hit the existing-entry collision (try to open while another is open) → see the new dialog branch with Close Existing Entry button; (b) set `custom_shift_hours=2`, leave `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` off, open a fresh entry, fudge `period_start_date` 3h back via the desk → orange banner appears with Close Shift button; (c) flip `custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` on, repeat → banner turns red and is non-dismissible, OrderPanel submit is blocked with the friendly toast.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - The banner polls every 60 seconds. For shift_hours=8, that means a worst-case ~1-minute delay between hitting the threshold and seeing the banner. Acceptable. For very short shift_hours values (testing) the delay is also 1 minute — bear that in mind during the test matrix.
  - The "block orders" hard block is a frontend-only check. If a malicious client bypassed it, the order would still go through on the backend. I'm leaving it as a soft trust check because the threat model here is "remind cashiers to close shifts", not "prevent unauthorized sales after hours". A hard backend block would need a similar guard in `sync_order` and is overkill for this feature.
  - ShiftHoursBanner's `parseFrappeDateTime` parses the entry's `period_start_date` as **local time**, not UTC. Frappe stores datetimes in the site's timezone without an offset suffix, so `new Date('2026-04-09T08:00:00')` parses as the browser's local time. As long as the POS device is in the same timezone as the Frappe site (the normal case), elapsed hours are correct. Cross-timezone setups would need a fix.
  - The existing-entry branch is reached via title-match `'POS Opening Entry Exists'` OR a regex on the message body. The regex is the fallback for translated installs; English installs always match the title.
  - **`custom_block_orders_after_shift_end` description includes `depends_on` `eval:doc.custom_shift_hours > 0`**, so the checkbox is hidden in the desk until shift hours are set. Cleaner admin UX.

### 2026-04-08 — POS Opening Entry per-terminal scoping (revamp phase 1, round 2)
- **Symptom / motivation:** After binding `URY POS Terminal` to `POS Profile` (round 1), opening entries were still branch-scoped. Two terminals on the same branch shared a single opening entry — the first to open "the branch" satisfied the open-check for everyone on every till at that branch. Wrong for independent cash drawers: Bar 1 and Restaurant A each have their own physical drawer and need their own opening balance + closing reconciliation. Phase 1 round 2 closes that loop.
- **Design decision (user confirmed):** scoping rules depend on the POS Profile's existing `custom_enable_multiple_cashier` flag.
  - **`multi_cashier` OFF (shared mode):** one POS Opening Entry per terminal per shift. The first user to arrive on a terminal opens it; everyone else who logs in onto that terminal just enters the POS without their own opening entry. Per-invoice attribution still happens via `POS Invoice.owner` (Frappe standard) and `POS Invoice.custom_terminal` (URY field added in round 1).
  - **`multi_cashier` ON (strict mode):** one POS Opening Entry per `(terminal, user)` per shift. Each user opens their own entry when they start their shift and closes their own when they leave. Used when management wants strict per-cashier shift accounting.
- **Schema changes:**
  - [ury/fixtures/custom_field.json](ury/fixtures/custom_field.json) + [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) fixtures — new `POS Opening Entry.custom_terminal` Link field (Link → URY POS Terminal, read-only, `in_list_view: 1` + `in_standard_filter: 1` so it shows in the desk list and filter bar). Mirrors the `POS Invoice.custom_terminal` field from round 1.
- **Backend changes:**
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `posOpening(terminal=None)` — new optional `terminal` parameter. When supplied, looks up the POS Profile from the terminal, reads `custom_enable_multiple_cashier`, and filters POS Opening Entry by `(custom_terminal, status, docstatus)` plus `user` if multi_cashier is ON. When omitted, falls back to the legacy branch-only check so the legacy Vue POS keeps working.
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `validate_pos_close(pos_profile, terminal=None)` — same scoping. The "previous day not closed" check is now per-terminal (and per-user when multi_cashier is ON), so an unclosed entry on Bar 1 doesn't block the cashier on Restaurant A.
  - [ury/ury_pos/api.py](ury/ury_pos/api.py) `getPosProfile()` multi_cashier branch — **massively simplified.** The old code did a SQL join through `Multiple Rooms` to figure out "who opened the captain's entry" and used that to set `cashier`/`owner`. The whole concept is gone now. New code just sets `cashier = owner = frappe.session.user`. Per-terminal scoping makes the room/captain lookup unnecessary. Also drops the dependency on `getBranchRoom()` from this code path, which was the source of the original "No room assigned to this user" landmine.
  - [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_opening_entry.py) — `main_pos_open_check` (captain-must-be-open gate) **DELETED**. New `validate_terminal_branch` hook verifies that a supplied `custom_terminal`'s branch matches the entry's branch (server-side trust check, mirrors the same check on URY POS Terminal save). `set_cashier_room` and `set_current_time` kept — they're harmless metadata stamping.
  - [ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py](ury/ury/hooks/ury_pos_closing_entry.py) — `sub_pos_close_check` (sub-cashier-must-close-first gate) **DELETED**. Captain/sub-cashier ordering is gone everywhere now. Comments left in both hook files explaining why, so future-me doesn't reintroduce the gates.
  - [ury/patches/v0_2/backfill_opening_entry_terminal.py](ury/patches/v0_2/backfill_opening_entry_terminal.py) + [ury/patches.txt](ury/patches.txt) — idempotent `[post_model_sync]` patch that backfills `custom_terminal` on existing POS Opening Entry rows. For each entry with no terminal: looks up `URY POS Terminal` rows matching `(branch, pos_profile, disabled=0)`. 1 candidate → stamp it. 0 → skip and log (admin must set manually). >1 → skip and log the candidates so admin can disambiguate. Defensive `frappe.db.has_column` guard.
- **Frontend changes:**
  - [pos/src/lib/pos-opening-api.ts](pos/src/lib/pos-opening-api.ts) — `checkPOSOpening(terminal?)`, `validatePOSClose(pos_profile, terminal?)`, and `CreatePOSOpeningPayload` gained an optional `custom_terminal` field. Comment on `createAndSubmitPOSOpening` updated to note that the captain gate is gone. `hasMainCashierOpened` marked `@deprecated` but kept in case the dialog's Join/Waiting branches are re-enabled for a future role-gated workflow.
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningProvider.tsx) — pulls `terminalName` from the POS store and passes it to both `checkPOSOpening` and `validatePOSClose`.
  - [pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx](pos/src/components/POSOpeningDialog.tsx) — pulls `terminalName` from the store, threads it through `OpeningBranchProps` to `OpeningBranch`, and includes `custom_terminal: terminalName` in the `createAndSubmitPOSOpening` payload (only when the terminal is set; the conditional spread keeps the payload clean otherwise). The Join Session / Waiting for Main Cashier branches are **left in place** per user's request — they're unreachable code paths under the new model but the UI stays ready in case a future role-gated "manager-must-open" workflow wants them.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 644.79 kB → 645.16 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on all four edited Python files + the new patch — OK.
  - `json.load` on `custom_field.json` — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench --site <site> migrate` (runs the new patch), then walks the matrix: (a) shared mode (multi_cashier OFF), first user opens terminal → entry created with `custom_terminal` set; (b) second user logs in on the same terminal → enters POS without seeing the open dialog; (c) third user logs in on a DIFFERENT terminal on the same branch → sees the open dialog (NOT the first terminal's entry); (d) strict mode (multi_cashier ON), each of two users on the same terminal must open their own entry; (e) user closes the entry, second day login on same terminal shows the open dialog cleanly.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Captain/sub-cashier deprecation is partial.** The `custom_main_cashier` checkbox on POS Profile User and the `custom_rooms` table on POS Opening Entry are still in the schema. The backend stops reading them. Sub POS Closing flow ([ury/ury/doctype/sub_pos_closing/sub_pos_closing.py](ury/ury/doctype/sub_pos_closing/sub_pos_closing.py)) and the closing-entry `calculate_closing_amount` hook still reference Sub POS Closing — those weren't touched in this round because they're a separate flow. They'll either need to be removed in a follow-up or kept around as a "legacy multi-cashier strict mode" that the new strict mode replaces.
  - **`getCashier(room)` SQL** in `ury_pos/api.py:538-556` still uses the old branch + room query. Only called from the legacy Vue POS (`urypos/src/stores/Table.js:148`), not from the React POS. Left unchanged for backward compat. Will become irrelevant when the legacy Vue POS is sunset (Dec 2025 per README).
  - **The backfill patch's "0 matches" branch silently skips** — meaning an existing opening entry on a branch without any terminals stays unstamped. It still shows up in the desk; only the new `posOpening(terminal=...)` check treats it as not-found. Acceptable because the admin will see "Please Open POS Entry" the next time they try to load the POS, and they can either re-stamp the entry manually or close it and open a new one through the dialog.
  - **The `OpeningBranch.tsx` sub-cashier branches are dead code** under the new model. They don't fire because `mainCashierUser` resolution depends on `posProfile.applicable_for_users[].custom_main_cashier` which is no longer the source of truth. Per user's instruction we kept them in case they're useful for a future "manager-must-open" gate. Don't be alarmed by the unreachable paths.

### 2026-04-08 — "Set Price in Menu" button invisible on the Price Not Set toast
- **Symptom:** After the previous round shipped the rich Price Not Set toast with a role-gated action button, a user logged in as `URY Captain` + `URY Manager` + `System Manager` still didn't see the button on the toast. Cashiers correctly didn't see it. Admin correctly saw it. Captain/manager didn't — looked like a role check bug.
- **Investigation:** Added a one-shot diagnostic `console.log({userName, userRoles, canEdit, ...})` in the `Price Not Set` branch of `OrderPanel.tsx`. On reproduction, console showed: `userRoles: Array(44)` containing `['... System Manager, URY Cashier, URY Manager, URY Captain]`, `canEdit: true`. So `canManageMenuPrices` was returning true correctly and `content.action` was being passed to `showToast.error` correctly — the button was in the React tree, it just wasn't visible to the eye.
- **Root cause:** The button's Tailwind classes were `bg-white/20 hover:bg-white/30 text-white`. That assumes the react-toastify `colored` theme's dark-red background for error toasts, which is the default when you pass `theme: 'colored'`. **But [pos/src/components/ui/toast.css](pos/src/components/ui/toast.css) overrides the colored theme with pastel backgrounds** (`#fef2f2` for error, `#ecfdf5` for success, `#eff6ff` for info) via `!important` rules — that's the URY house style. Against a pale-pink background with white text, the button was effectively invisible. White text on almost-white background. It was in the DOM the entire time; you just couldn't see it.
- **Files changed:**
  - [pos/src/components/ui/toast.tsx](pos/src/components/ui/toast.tsx) — button styles switched from white-on-dark (`bg-white/20 text-white`) to `currentColor`-aware inline styles: `backgroundColor: rgba(0,0,0,0.08)`, `border: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,0.2)`, `color: currentColor`. The dark-overlay background is visible on every pastel toast variant; the currentColor text picks up whatever foreground the toast type set (dark red on error, dark green on success, dark blue on info) so the button label is always readable. Inline hover handlers bump the overlay to `rgba(0,0,0,0.16)` — couldn't use Tailwind's `hover:` prefix cleanly because we're on inline styles. Added a long source comment explaining the trap so future-me doesn't re-ship the same button and wonder why it's invisible again.
  - [pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) — removed the one-shot `[URY diagnostic]` console.log that we used to identify the bug. `canEdit: true` in the probe output proved the role logic was correct, which scoped the fix to visual rendering only.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle rebuilt.
  - **Still to do:** user walks Case A / Case E one more time (zero-rate Sprite as captain → button VISIBLE now; zero-rate Sprite as cashier → button still hidden).
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Lesson:** Any time a component uses the `showToast.error/success/info` rich-content mode with an action button, check that the button's colours work against URY's pastel backgrounds, NOT against react-toastify's default dark "colored" theme. The default theme is overridden by [toast.css](pos/src/components/ui/toast.css) with `!important`, which is load-bearing for the URY brand look but traps anyone writing new toast content that assumes dark backgrounds.
  - The diagnostic `console.log` shipped briefly in `index-BnqyBqR5.js`. Removed in the same commit as the fix so production users never see it.
  - If we ever add a third toast variant (warning, neutral) with a different pastel background, the `currentColor`-based button will still work because it only depends on the foreground colour being dark — which every pastel toast already enforces for legibility.

### 2026-04-08 — "Sales Invoice mode is activated" blocker + sync_order error-wrapping cleanup
- **Symptom:** After the previous round's menu/toast fixes shipped, user still couldn't submit orders. DevTools Response showed three stacked `_server_messages`: (1) the old "Item Price added for <a>Fanta</a>..." side-effect alert (still firing because the menu hadn't been re-saved after the UOM fix), (2) the real `ValidationError` `"Sales Invoice mode is activated in POS. Please create Sales Invoice instead."`, and (3) `"Error while updating order: Sales Invoice mode is activated..."` — a duplicate wrapper of message 2.
- **Root causes:**
  1. **`POS Settings.invoice_type = "Sales Invoice"`** on the site. ERPNext's `POSInvoice.validate_is_pos_using_sales_invoice()` ([apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py:466-469](apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py#L466-L469)) rejects any POS Invoice save when that singleton is in Sales Invoice mode. URY is architecturally incompatible with that mode — it creates POS Invoices directly. **This setting had to be flipped to "POS Invoice" manually the first time**, and the failure mode was a confusing stack of server messages because of Root Cause 2.
  2. **`sync_order` wrapped `invoice.save()` in `try/except` and re-threw.** The legacy code was `try: invoice.save() except Exception as e: frappe.throw(f"Error while updating order: {e}")` — which caught ERPNext's well-formed ValidationError, stripped its title, stripped its indicator, prefixed the message with "Error while updating order:", and re-threw as a plain ValidationError with the stringified original. The frontend `extractFrappeServerError` then had to choose between the original (message 2) and the wrapper (message 3) and ended up showing the noisier one. Removing the wrapper lets ERPNext's errors propagate cleanly — they already have all the metadata the frontend needs.
- **Files changed:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) — deleted the `try/except` around `invoice.save()`. ValidationErrors now propagate unwrapped.
  - [ury/install.py](ury/install.py) — new `ensure_pos_settings_configured()` function that auto-sets `POS Settings.invoice_type = "POS Invoice"` if it isn't already. Called from both `after_install` (for fresh installs) and a new `after_migrate` (for existing installs — every `bench migrate` brings drifted sites back into a working state). Logs a yellow `[URY]` line when a correction is applied, or a red warning if the set fails for any reason (permissions, missing DocType, etc.) — either way it doesn't crash install or migrate.
  - [ury/hooks.py](ury/hooks.py) — uncommented `after_install` and added `after_migrate`, both pointing at `ury.install`. Note: `after_install` was commented out before this round, which means `ury.install.after_install` (the `setup()` call) was never actually running. Bundling the rewire with the new POS Settings guard so upgrades get both in one go.
  - [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) — added a Gotchas entry documenting the `POS Settings.invoice_type` dependency, plus this Fixes log entry.
- **Verification:**
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on `install.py`, `hooks.py`, `ury_order.py` — OK.
  - No React frontend changes this round — bundle not rebuilt.
  - **User pre-verified in the browser:** flipped `POS Settings.invoice_type` to "POS Invoice" manually + ran `bench migrate` + re-saved the URY Menu → order submission worked cleanly. This round just codifies that manual fix so future installs don't hit the same wall.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - **Because `after_install` was previously commented out, `setup()` (the `create_custom_fields` call in [ury/setup.py](ury/setup.py)) was never running on `after_install`.** That means URY installs relied entirely on the `fixtures` list in hooks.py to create those custom fields. The wire-up here restores the intended behaviour but it's a latent behaviour change worth flagging if any existing site starts seeing duplicate or re-created custom fields on their next migrate. The `create_custom_fields` function is idempotent so there shouldn't be damage, but keep an eye on it.
  - The `try/except` that silently swallows `setup()` failures in `install.py` is kept as-is — I don't want to surface latent install errors from someone else's 3-year-old code in the middle of a permissions-revamp session. That's its own cleanup pass.
  - No `sync_order` callers relied on the "Error while updating order:" prefix. Grep confirmed.

### 2026-04-08 — "Item Price added for <a>...</a>" noise toast + Price Not Set rich error
- **Symptom:** After tapping a menu item (Fanta) and hitting Submit, the POS showed a toast containing raw HTML: `tem Price added for <a href="http://.../desk/item/Fanta" rel="noopener noreferrer">Fanta</a> in Price List S...`. The user wanted a cleaner notification and specifically a way to set the price from the POS.
- **Root cause (three stacked bugs):**
  1. **UOM missing on menu-synced Item Prices.** `URYMenu.make_price_list` in [ury/ury/doctype/ury_menu/ury_menu.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_menu/ury_menu.py#L36-L46) was creating Item Price rows without setting `uom`. ERPNext's standard POS Invoice validation in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py:insert_item_price()` filters Item Price lookups by `uom = item.stock_uom` — so it couldn't match the URY-created row, fell through to the "auto-insert missing price" branch (Stock Settings `auto_insert_price_list_rate_if_missing` defaults to on), created a *second* duplicate Item Price, and fired `frappe.msgprint("Item Price added for <a href='...'>Fanta</a> in Price List ...", alert=True)` on **every** POS submission.
  2. **OrderPanel picked the wrong `_server_messages` entry.** [pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) read `JSON.parse(messages[0])` — always the first entry. When the response carried BOTH ERPNext's side-effect alert AND the real ValidationError, the user saw the alert. Classic "first in the array" trap — Frappe's convention is to mark the thrown error with `raise_exception: 1`, so consumers should look for that flag, not blindly grab index zero.
  3. **Toast rendered HTML as plain text.** react-toastify's string mode doesn't strip tags, so `<a href="...">Fanta</a>` showed up literally in the toast. Even after fixing which message to pick, any legit message with a desk link would have looked broken.
- **Files changed:**
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_menu/ury_menu.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_menu/ury_menu.py) — `make_price_list` now reads `Item.stock_uom` and stamps it on every Item Price it inserts. Kills the ERPNext auto-insert path at the root.
  - [ury/patches/v0_2/backfill_item_price_uom.py](ury/patches/v0_2/backfill_item_price_uom.py) + [ury/patches.txt](ury/patches.txt) — idempotent `[post_model_sync]` patch that walks all menu-linked Price Lists (`Price List.restaurant_menu IS NOT NULL`) and backfills `uom` on any Item Price rows where it's null. Defensive `frappe.db.has_column` guard. Runs after the terminal backfill. Safe to re-run.
  - [ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py](ury/ury/doctype/ury_order/ury_order.py) — `sync_order` now throws `title="Price Not Set"` with a clean sentence naming the item AND the menu (ExPOS Menu via translation), covering BOTH "no Item Price row" AND "row exists but rate is 0/null". Previously the "no row" case threw a terse `"No item price found for Item X in Price List Y"` and the "zero rate" case silently stamped a zero-rate line onto the invoice.
  - [pos/src/lib/utils.ts](pos/src/lib/utils.ts) — new `parseFrappeServerMessages()` + `extractFrappeServerError()` helpers. The extractor walks the full `_server_messages` array, prefers the entry with `raise_exception: 1`, falls back to the last entry, then `err.message`, then a caller-supplied fallback. Strips HTML tags from the final string so `<a>` etc. render as plain text. Returns `{title, message, raw}` so callers can switch on the title (e.g. "Price Not Set" → rich toast).
  - [pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts](pos/src/lib/role-utils.ts) — new `canManageMenuPrices(user)` helper. Returns true for `Administrator`, `System Manager`, `URY Manager`, `URY Captain`. Gates the "Set Price in Menu" action button in the Price Not Set toast so cashiers don't see a button they can't use.
  - [pos/src/components/ui/toast.tsx](pos/src/components/ui/toast.tsx) — `showToast.error/success/info` now accept either a `string` (legacy shorthand, unchanged behaviour) or a `RichToastContent` object `{title, description?, action?: {label, onClick}}`. A rich toast with an action auto-closes after 8 s instead of 2 s and disables closeOnClick so the user has time to hit the button. Action button is rendered inside the toast card with an ExternalLink icon.
  - [pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx](pos/src/components/OrderPanel.tsx) — submit-handler's catch block now uses `extractFrappeServerError()`. When the parsed title is `"Price Not Set"`, shows a rich toast with title "Price Not Set", description = the server sentence, and an action `"Set Price in Menu"` that opens `/app/ury-menu` in a new tab — **only** for users where `canManageMenuPrices(user)` returns true. All other errors render as plain-text toasts with the parsed message.
- **Verification:**
  - `yarn build` in `pos/` — clean, bundle 643.10 kB → 644.79 kB.
  - `python3 -m py_compile` on both edited Python files + new patch — OK.
  - **Still to do:** user runs `bench migrate` (runs the backfill), then tests the three freshinstall states: (a) item with no Item Price row at all → rich Price Not Set toast, action button visible for admin/captain/manager, hidden for cashier; (b) item with rate 0 → same toast; (c) menu saved with a rate → resync creates Item Price rows WITH `uom` set; (d) submit an order → no "Item Price added for <a>..." noise toast appears anymore.
- **Notes / follow-ups:**
  - The `extractFrappeServerError` helper should replace the ad-hoc `_server_messages` parsers scattered across `config-slice.ts`, `App.tsx`, `menu-api.ts`, `aggregator-api.ts`, `customer-api.ts`, `POSOpeningDialog.tsx`. **Not touching those in this round** to keep scope tight, but the next time any of them misbehave, migrate them to the helper instead of adding a sixth copy of the parser.
  - The "Set Price in Menu" action deep-links to `/app/ury-menu` (the list view), not a specific menu record. The list is usually short enough that one click lands the admin on the right menu. A more precise deep-link would need the menu name in the frontend payload — punt to phase 2 if desired.
  - **Role list for `canManageMenuPrices`:** user asked for "administrator and captain". I included `URY Manager` + `System Manager` as well because (a) `URY Manager` already has write access on URY Menu via DocType permissions, and (b) `System Manager` is Frappe's framework god-role and will always need access. If the user wants a stricter list (Administrator + URY Captain only), trim the `allowed` array in `role-utils.ts`. Flagged in the commit message.
  - **Rate is still optional on the URY Menu Item DocType JSON.** Per user's request we enforce at order time instead of schema time. Admins can save work-in-progress menus without getting blocked.

### 2026-04-08 — URY POS Terminal ↔ POS Profile binding (revamp phase 1, round 1)
- **Symptom / motivation:** The React POS never showed the cashier *which* POS Profile they were ringing in.
